VOCATIONAL ONTOLOGY BIBLE
"Ein neues Leben in Deutschland" — Complete Production-Ready Career Module Specification
Master Document v1.0 | 20 Professions | Full 5-Part Granular Architecture
PROFESSION 1: KFZ-MECHATRONIKER/IN (Automotive Mechanic / Car Technician)
1. The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint
Visual Landscape:
The workshop (die Kfz-Werkstatt) is a cavernous, oil-stained concrete hall lit by banks of flickering fluorescent tubes and portable halogen work lamps. Four hydraulic two-post lifts (Zweisäulen-Hebebühnen) stand in a row, each holding a vehicle at chest height. Along the west wall runs a red Würth tool chest system (der Werkzeugwagen) stretching eight meters, every drawer labeled with embossed white lettering: Steckschlüssel, Drehmoment, Elektrik. A glass-partitioned Meisterbüro sits in the northeast corner with a desk buried under Arbeitsaufträge and a wall calendar from Bosch Automotive. The floor is polished concrete with yellow-painted walkways and red-hatched safety zones around each lift. A tire rack (das Reifenregal) towers to the ceiling near the rolling gate (das Rolltor), stacked with seasonal tires. A diagnostic station with a Bosch KTS 560 scan tool and a laptop running ESI[tronic] software glows blue in the corner. Posters of brake system cross-sections and timing belt replacement intervals line the walls. An exhaust extraction system (die Abgasabsauganlage) with flexible hoses hangs from ceiling rails above each bay.

Specific Smells:
The dominant scent is a cocktail of old motor oil (warm, slightly burnt), brake cleaner spray (sharp, acetone-like chemical bite), and rubber from fresh tires. Underneath lies the metallic tang of iron filings from the grinding station, the faint sweetness of coolant (ethylene glycol), and occasional diesel exhaust when a vehicle is started inside the hall. Near the parts-washing station (die Teilewaschanlage), the solvent smell is overwhelming and acrid.

Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:
Baseline ambient noise sits at approximately 75 dB — the constant hum of the compressed air system (der Kompressor), punctuated by the violent staccato bark of pneumatic impact wrenches (der Druckluft-Schlagschrauber) hitting 110+ dB in sharp bursts. The hydraulic lifts emit a deep, groaning whine during ascent and a hissing sigh when descending. Radio plays Deutschlandfunk or a local Berlin station at low volume, barely audible over metal-on-metal clanging. Periodically, the diagnostic laptop emits a series of electronic beeps. Communication requires raised voices or direct proximity.

The Meister / Boss Persona:
Name: Meister Ralf Weber
Age: 54
Personality: Meister Weber is a stocky, barrel-chested man with permanently oil-darkened fingernails, a grey handlebar mustache, and steel-rimmed glasses perched on his forehead. He completed his Kfz-Meisterprüfung in 1998 and has run this independent Werkstatt for 19 years. He is old-school, gruff, and deeply principled. He does not tolerate laziness, imprecision, or — above all — unsafe behavior. However, beneath the bark is genuine investment in his apprentices. He tests newcomers constantly with pop questions about torque specs. He speaks in clipped, rapid Berliner dialect, drops final consonants, and uses dry humor. He addresses the player formally as "Sie" for the first weeks, then switches to "du" as a mark of earned trust — a significant emotional game moment.
Speech Cadence: Fast, declarative sentences. Frequent use of "So." as a sentence starter meaning "Right then." Drops articles in commands ("Schlüssel her!" instead of "Gib mir den Schlüssel!"). Uses "wa?" as a Berliner tag question.
Expectations: Punctuality (7:00 means 6:50). Clean workstation at end of shift. Every bolt torqued to spec, never estimated. Safety glasses on at all times in the hall. Mistakes are tolerated once; repeated mistakes trigger a stern dressing-down.

3. The Workplace Register & Jargon
5 Exact German Imperative Commands Shouted on the Job:

"Bühne hoch!" — [ˈbyːnə hoːx] — "Lift up!" (Command to raise the hydraulic lift. Clipped, no verb, pure shop-floor shorthand.)
"Zündung an!" — [ˈtsʏndʊŋ an] — "Ignition on!" (Shouted across the bay when the Meister wants the player to turn the key to position II for diagnostics.)
"Fass das nicht an!" — [fas das nɪçt an] — "Don't touch that!" (Sharp warning when the player reaches for a component that is still hot, live, or under tension.)
"Halt mal dagegen!" — [halt maːl daˈɡeːɡən] — "Hold it against that!" / "Brace it!" (Instruction to hold a component steady while someone else works on it — requires interpreting spatial deixis.)
"Mach die Wanne drunter!" — [max diː ˈvanə ˈdrʊntɐ] — "Put the drain pan underneath!" (Before draining oil; urgency if oil is already dripping.)
3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:

"Das ist pfusch." — "That's shoddy work." (Used when a repair is done sloppily. Carries moral weight — Meister Weber considers Pfusch a personal insult to the trade.)
"Nach fest kommt ab." — "After tight comes off." (Meaning: if you overtighten a bolt, you'll strip or snap it. A fundamental mechanic's proverb spoken as a teaching moment.)
"Der läuft auf drei Töpfen." — "It's running on three cylinders." (Slang for a misfiring four-cylinder engine. "Topf" = informal for "Zylinder.")
4. Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)
Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Orientation & Safety Briefing (UVV)
Setup: It is 06:52 on a grey Monday morning. The player arrives at the Werkstatt on Sonnenallee. The rolling gate is already half-open. Inside, the compressor chugs to life. Meister Weber stands at a whiteboard near the Meisterbüro, arms crossed, holding a clipboard.

Beat 1 — The Greeting Test:
Meister Weber looks at the player, checks the clipboard, and says:
"Morgen. Sie sind der/die Neue, wa? Haben Sie Sicherheitsschuhe an?"
(Morning. You're the new one, right? Are you wearing safety shoes?)
The player must answer. If they are wearing the correct PSA (Persönliche Schutzausrüstung) modeled on their character (Sicherheitsschuhe S3, Arbeitshandschuhe), Weber nods. If not, he stops everything: "Ohne Sicherheitsschuhe betreten Sie die Halle nicht. Punkt." (Without safety shoes, you do not enter the hall. Period.) The player must leave and return — a 20-minute in-game time penalty and a social reputation hit.

Beat 2 — The Hall Tour & Zone Naming:
Weber walks the player through the hall, pointing. He names each zone:

"Das hier ist der Arbeitsbereich." (This is the work area.)
"Da drüben, die rote Fläche — Gefahrenzone. Da steht man nicht, wenn die Bühne fährt." (Over there, the red area — danger zone. You don't stand there when the lift moves.)
"Das ist die Teilewaschanlage. Handschuhe tragen. Immer." (That's the parts washer. Wear gloves. Always.)
"Der Verbandskasten hängt da. Augendusche daneben." (The first aid kit hangs there. Eyewash station next to it.)
At each stop, the game prompts the player to select or type the correct German noun with article (e.g., selecting "der Verbandskasten" from a radial menu). Errors trigger Weber's correction: "Nein. DER Verbandskasten. Maskulin."
Beat 3 — The Safety Equipment Inventory:
Weber hands the player a PSA checklist (die PSA-Checkliste) on a clipboard. The player must physically locate and pick up each item in the 3D environment and match it to the list:

die Schutzbrille (safety glasses)
die Arbeitshandschuhe (Plural) (work gloves)
der Gehörschutz (ear protection)
die Sicherheitsschuhe (Plural) (safety shoes)
Each item must be placed in the player's locker (der Spind). The locker combination is spoken aloud by Weber: "Ihr Spind ist Nummer siebzehn. Kombination: drei-acht-fünf." — testing numeral comprehension.
Beat 4 — The First Assignment:
Weber points to a dusty VW Golf IV on lift bay 3: "Ihr erster Auftrag: Ölwechsel. Altöl ablassen, neuen Filter rein, 4,3 Liter 5W-30 auffüllen. Auffangwanne nicht vergessen. Los."
(Your first job: oil change. Drain the old oil, new filter in, fill 4.3 liters 5W-30. Don't forget the drain pan. Go.)
The player must recall the vocabulary from the tool ontology, locate the Auffangwanne, and physically perform each step. If the player forgets the Auffangwanne and opens the drain plug, oil floods the floor — triggering a crisis micro-event (environmental spill, Weber furious, cleanup task, reputation loss).

Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — The Standard Routine Task (Brake Pad Replacement)
Setup: Day 14. The player has earned "du" status. A printed Arbeitsauftrag (work order) sits on the player's workbench. It reads:
Fahrzeug: Opel Corsa E, 1.4 Turbo, Bj. 2018. Auftrag: Bremsbeläge vorne wechseln. Bremsscheiben prüfen (Mindestdicke: 20,4 mm). Kunde: Frau Yılmaz. Abholung: 15:00 Uhr.

Beat 1 — Reading the Work Order:
The player must read and comprehend the Arbeitsauftrag. Vocabulary demands: das Baujahr (year of manufacture, abbreviated Bj.), die Mindestdicke (minimum thickness), die Abholung (pickup). A dictionary hover is available at A1 but disappears by B1 difficulty.

Beat 2 — Parts Preparation:
The player walks to the Teilelager (parts storage room) and must request the correct parts from the parts clerk, Azubi Tom:
"Tom, ich brauche Bremsbeläge vorne für einen Opel Corsa E, Baujahr 2018, 1.4 Turbo."
Tom hands over a box. The player must visually verify the part number on the box matches the Arbeitsauftrag. If they skip verification and the parts are wrong, the error is discovered mid-job — causing delay, frustration from the Meister, and a learning moment about Teilenummer prüfen (checking part numbers).

Beat 3 — Executing the Multi-Step Task:

Fahrzeug aufbocken (Jack up the vehicle on the lift).
Rad abnehmen (Remove the wheel — player uses the Druckluft-Schlagschrauber).
Bremssattel lösen (Loosen the brake caliper bolts — two 7mm Allen bolts).
Bremssattel mit Draht aufhängen (Hang the caliper with wire — never let it dangle by the hose; Weber watches and tests if the player knows this).
Alte Bremsbeläge entnehmen (Remove the old brake pads — note their wear pattern).
Bremsscheibe messen (Measure the brake disc with a micrometer; the player reads the measurement in German: "Einundzwanzig Komma zwei Millimeter — über Mindestdicke." — 21.2 mm, above minimum thickness).
Bremskolben zurückdrücken (Push back the brake piston using the Bremskolbenrücksteller).
Neue Bremsbeläge einsetzen (Insert the new brake pads).
Bremssattel montieren und Schrauben festziehen (Reinstall caliper and torque bolts to spec — 35 Nm).
Rad montieren, Radmuttern über Kreuz festziehen, 110 Nm (Mount wheel, tighten lug nuts in cross pattern, 110 Nm).
Each step generates German vocabulary demands (tool names, measurement units, prepositions of place).

Beat 4 — Reporting Completion & Filling the Work Log:
The player approaches Meister Weber:
"Meister Weber, der Bremsenservice am Corsa ist fertig. Beläge gewechselt, Scheiben geprüft — einundzwanzig Komma zwei Millimeter, alles im grünen Bereich."
Weber nods: "Gut. Trag's ins Protokoll ein."
The player must fill out a digital Arbeitsprotokoll on a workshop tablet: Datum, Fahrzeug, Durchgeführte Arbeiten (performed work), Verwendete Teile (parts used), Arbeitszeit in Stunden (labor time in hours), Unterschrift.

Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise — Sudden Brake Failure During Test Drive
Setup: Day 30. The player has completed a full brake service on a Mercedes C-Klasse. Meister Weber tells the player to do a short Probefahrt (test drive) around the block. The player drives out of the Werkstatt into real Berlin-Neukölln traffic.

Beat 1 — The Crisis Trigger:
At 30 km/h on Weserstraße, the player presses the brake pedal. It sinks to the floor with almost no resistance. The brake warning light (die Bremswarnleuchte) illuminates red on the dashboard. A pedestrian steps off the curb ahead.

Beat 2 — Immediate Physical Action:
The player must:

Pump the brake pedal rapidly (pumpen) to attempt to build pressure.
Downshift (herunterschalten) to use engine braking.
Engage the parking brake (die Handbremse / Feststellbremse) gradually.
Steer toward the curb to stop.
If the player freezes or does nothing, the car rolls into traffic — resulting in an accident, police involvement (die Polizei, das Unfallprotokoll), insurance disaster, and potential game-over for the career module.
Beat 3 — The Phone Call:
Stopped safely, the player must call Meister Weber from the car:
"Meister Weber, ich stehe auf der Weserstraße. Die Bremse ist ausgefallen. Das Pedal geht bis zum Boden durch. Ich habe mit der Handbremse angehalten."
(Meister Weber, I am on Weserstraße. The brake has failed. The pedal goes all the way to the floor. I stopped with the parking brake.)
Weber's rapid, stressed response: "Bleib wo du bist. Motor aus. Warnblinker an. Ich komm mit dem Abschleppwagen. Fahr auf keinen Fall weiter, hörst du?"
(Stay where you are. Engine off. Hazard lights on. I'm coming with the tow truck. Do not drive any further, you hear me?)
Vocabulary under stress: der Warnblinker (hazard lights), der Abschleppwagen (tow truck), ausgefallen (failed), durchgehen (to go through — pedal travel).

Beat 4 — Root Cause Analysis Back at the Workshop:
Back in the Werkstatt, Weber and the player inspect together. Weber asks the player to diagnose:
"Guck mal unter den Wagen. Was siehst du?"
The player slides under on a Rollbrett (creeper) and sees: Bremsflüssigkeit (brake fluid) dripping from a Bremsleitung (brake line). The line was kinked during reinstallation. Weber's tone is heavy:
"Die Bremsleitung ist geknickt. Da ist Flüssigkeit ausgetreten. Deshalb kein Druck. Das hätte böse enden können."
(The brake line is kinked. Fluid leaked out. That's why no pressure. That could have ended badly.)
This is a learning scar — the event is permanently logged in the player's Berichtsheft and triggers a spaced-repetition echo of brake system vocabulary for the next 30 in-game days.

5. The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)
Exam Structure:
The Kfz-Mechatroniker/in completes a 3.5-year duale Ausbildung, culminating in the Gesellenprüfung Teil 1 (after 18 months) and Gesellenprüfung Teil 2 (at the end), administered by the Handwerkskammer (HWK) Berlin.

Specific Exam Vocabulary:

German Term	English	Context
die Gesellenprüfung	journeyman's exam	Final practical and written exam
die Zwischenprüfung	intermediate exam	Midpoint assessment (now integrated into GP1)
das Gesellenstück / die Arbeitsprobe	practical exam piece / work sample	Hands-on task performed under exam conditions
der Prüfungsausschuss	exam committee	Panel of Meister evaluators
die Handwerkskammer (HWK)	Chamber of Skilled Crafts	Certifying body
das Berichtsheft	training logbook / report book	Must be complete and signed to be admitted to exam
die Schaltplananalyse	circuit diagram analysis	Written exam component — reading electrical schematics
die Kundenauftragsbearbeitung	customer order processing	Exam section testing communication, cost estimation
das Fachgespräch	technical oral exam	Oral defense of practical work before the committee
die Fahrzeugsystemdiagnose	vehicle system diagnostics	Core exam module — fault code reading, interpretation
der Arbeitsauftrag (prüfungsrelevant)	exam-relevant work order	Simulated work scenario with documentation requirement
die Abgasuntersuchung (AU)	emissions inspection	Legal testing procedure, part of HU/AU certification
die Hauptuntersuchung (HU)	general vehicle inspection (TÜV)	Comprehensive safety and roadworthiness test
das Drehmoment (in Newtonmeter, Nm)	torque (in Newton-meters)	Must be stated precisely during practical exam
die Fehlerspeicherabfrage	fault memory query	OBD-II scan procedure tested in GP2
Visa Stabilization Link:
Upon passing the Gesellenprüfung, the player receives the Gesellenbrief (journeyman's certificate), which triggers an in-game appointment at the Ausländerbehörde for conversion of their visa to a §18a AufenthG Fachkräftevisum (Skilled Worker Residence Permit) — removing the 90-day countdown and granting a 4-year permit.

PROFESSION 2: PFLEGEFACHKRAFT / ALTENPFLEGER/IN (General Nurse / Geriatric Healthcare Nurse)
1. The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint
Visual Landscape:
The setting is a Pflegeheim (residential care home) called "Haus Sonnenschein" on a quiet side street off Sonnenallee. The building is a renovated 1970s block, four stories, with a beige-tiled entrance hall and an elevator that smells of disinfectant and faintly of urine despite constant cleaning. The player works on Station 2, Wohnbereich Linde (Living Area Linden — each area is named after a tree). The hallway is wide enough for two wheelchairs to pass, floored with speckled grey linoleum that squeaks underfoot. Handrails (die Handläufe) line both walls. Room doors are numbered and have name plates with large, readable fonts for the residents. Each resident's room contains an adjustable electric bed (das Pflegebett), a bedside table (der Nachttisch), a call bell (die Rufanlage / der Klingeltaster), a wardrobe, and a small bathroom with grab bars (die Haltegriffe) and a roll-in shower. The Stationszimmer (nurses' station) is a compact room with a medication cabinet (der Medikamentenschrank — locked, double-key system), a computer running Pflegedokumentationssoftware (care documentation software, e.g., DAN or Vivendi), a wall clock, and a whiteboard showing Dienstplan (shift schedule) and Besonderheiten (notable events per resident).

Specific Smells:
The facility smells of industrial-grade Flächendesinfektionsmittel (surface disinfectant — sharp, chemical, slightly citrus-scented), institutional kitchen food (boiled vegetables, mashed potatoes) drifting from the Gemeinschaftsraum (common room) at mealtimes, and the unavoidable undertones of incontinence care — urine, stool — masked by scented hand soap and air freshener but never fully eliminated. The medication room has its own smell: a papery, slightly sweet pharmaceutical scent. Fresh laundry from the Wäscheraum smells aggressively of industrial detergent.

Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:
The hallway is eerily quiet at 60 dB most of the time — shuffling slippers, murmured conversations, a TV blaring in the common room (a resident hard of hearing). This quiet is punctuated by the shrill electronic alarm of the Rufanlage (call bell system) — a two-tone chime that triggers urgency in the player. Occasionally, a resident cries out in confusion or pain. During shift change (die Übergabe), the Stationszimmer is crowded and voices overlap — a challenging listening comprehension moment for the player. At night shift, the silence is oppressive, broken only by periodic rounds (die Kontrollrunde).

The Meister / Boss Persona:
Name: Stationsleitung Frau Brigitte Schneider
Age: 49
Personality: Frau Schneider is a wiry, energetic woman with short-cropped silver hair, reading glasses on a chain, and Birkenstock clogs that clack on the linoleum. She has been a Pflegefachkraft for 26 years and Stationsleitung for 8. She is simultaneously warm and strict. She calls residents by their preferred names (Frau Müller, Herr Hoffmann) and insists the player do the same — never "Oma" or "Opa," which she considers undignified. She is fanatical about documentation: "Was nicht dokumentiert ist, ist nicht passiert." (What isn't documented didn't happen.) She speaks in complete, clear Hochdeutsch with occasional Berlinerisch endearments when comforting a resident ("Na, mein Schatz, wir machen das schon."). She is protective of her team and her residents in equal measure.
Speech Cadence: Measured, deliberate, but can become rapid and clipped during emergencies. Uses medical terminology without slowing down — the player must keep up.
Expectations: Empathy first, efficiency second. Hygiene protocol is non-negotiable. Handschuhe wechseln (change gloves) between every resident. Documentation completed within the same shift, not retroactively. The player must learn to speak to residents with respect, using Sie, making eye contact, announcing every physical action before performing it.

1	desinfizieren (desinfiziert, desinfizierte, desinfiziert)	to disinfect	Hände vor und nach jedem Bewohnerkontakt desinfizieren

10	einreiben (reibt ein, rieb ein, eingerieben)	to apply (cream/lotion) by rubbing	Die Haut mit Pflegelotion einreiben
3. The Workplace Register & Jargon
5 Exact German Imperative Commands on the Job:

"Klingel in Zimmer acht!" — [ˈklɪŋəl ɪn ˈtsɪmɐ axt] — "Call bell in room eight!" (Shouted at the nurses' station. Not a full sentence — shorthand for "The call bell is ringing in room eight, go check.")
"Handschuhe an!" — [ˈhantʃuːə an] — "Gloves on!" (Barked by Frau Schneider when she sees the player approaching a resident without gloves.)
"Drehen Sie sich bitte auf die Seite!" — [ˈdʁeːən ziː zɪç ˈbɪtə aʊf diː ˈzaɪtə] — "Please turn onto your side!" (Said to a resident during repositioning or bed care. Note the formal Sie — always used with residents.)
"Oberkörper hoch!" — [ˈoːbɐkœʁpɐ hoːx] — "Upper body up!" (Clipped command to a colleague during a two-person transfer — means raise the head of the bed or lift the resident's torso.)
"Sturz in Zimmer zwölf! Sofort kommen!" — [ʃtʊʁts ɪn ˈtsɪmɐ tsvœlf | zoˈfɔʁt ˈkɔmən] — "Fall in room twelve! Come immediately!" (Emergency alert — a resident has fallen.)
3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Expressions:

"Pflege ist kein Sprint, sondern ein Marathon." — "Care is not a sprint but a marathon." (Said by Frau Schneider during orientation to set expectations about emotional endurance and pacing.)
"Wir pflegen hier keine Akten, wir pflegen Menschen." — "We don't care for files here, we care for people." (Ironic reminder from Frau Schneider when a team member gets lost in documentation and neglects the human contact.)
"Einmal links, einmal rechts, dann auf den Rücken — Uhr stellen." — "Once left, once right, then on the back — set the timer." (Mnemonic for the two-hourly repositioning protocol to prevent Dekubitus / pressure sores.)
4. Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)
Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Orientation & Hygiene Briefing
Setup: 06:15, Frühdienst (early shift). The player arrives in the locker room (die Umkleide), changes into clean Arbeitskleidung (Kasack — medical scrub top, Pflegehose — care trousers, Arbeitsschuhe — closed, washable shoes). No jewelry, no nail polish, no wristwatch — Frau Schneider inspects.

Beat 1 — The Hygiene Introduction:
Frau Schneider stands before a poster of the WHO Five Moments of Hand Hygiene (die fünf Momente der Händehygiene) in the hallway.
"Bevor Sie irgendetwas anfassen: Hände desinfizieren. Vor dem Bewohnerkontakt. Nach dem Bewohnerkontakt. Nach Kontakt mit der Umgebung des Bewohners. Vor aseptischen Tätigkeiten. Nach Kontakt mit potenziell infektiösem Material. Immer. Ohne Ausnahme."
The player must physically interact with the Desinfektionsmittelspender in the 3D environment, performing the correct hand-rubbing motion (30 seconds, all surfaces, including fingertips, thumbs, and between fingers). A visual timer runs on screen. Skipping this at any point in the game triggers immediate correction.

Beat 2 — Meeting the Residents:
Frau Schneider introduces the player to three key residents:

Herr Hoffmann, 84, Demenz (Alzheimer's): Friendly but confused. May call the player by his daughter's name. Tends to wander. Fall risk (Sturzgefahr).
Frau Kuhn, 78, Diabetes Typ II, insulinpflichtig: Alert and sharp-tongued. Needs blood glucose checks before meals and insulin administration. Strict about her routine.
Frau Yılmaz, 91, bettlägerig (bedridden), Dekubitus Grad II am Kreuzbein: Requires two-hourly repositioning, wound care, and gentle communication. Speaks some German but reverts to Turkish when distressed.
Beat 3 — The First Task — Vitalzeichenkontrolle (Vital Signs Check):
Frau Schneider assigns the player to take Frau Kuhn's vital signs:

Blutdruck messen (take blood pressure) — player must apply the cuff, read the values, and state them in German: "Einhundertdreißig zu fünfundachtzig." (130/85)
Puls zählen (count pulse) — "Zweiundsechzig Schläge pro Minute." (62 bpm)
Blutzucker messen (blood glucose) — prick the finger, read the meter: "Hundertachtundvierzig Milligramm pro Deziliter." (148 mg/dL)
Körpertemperatur messen (temperature) — "Sechsunddreißig Komma acht Grad Celsius." (36.8°C)
All values must be entered into the digital Pflegedokumentation. Incorrect entries are flagged by the system, and Frau Schneider reviews them during the shift.
Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — The Standard Morning Routine (Grundpflege)
Setup: Day 21. The player has settled into the early shift rhythm. The assignment board lists: "Grundpflege Frau Yılmaz, Zimmer 14."

Beat 1 — Preparation:
The player gathers supplies from the Pflegewagen (care trolley):

Waschschüssel (wash basin), Waschlappen (washcloths — separate ones for face, body, intimate area, color-coded), Handtuch (towel), Hautpflegelotion, fresh Inkontinenzeinlage, clean Bettwäsche (bed linen), Einmalhandschuhe, Schürze (apron).
Beat 2 — Communication Before Touch:
The player enters Zimmer 14, knocks (always knock, even if the door is open), and greets:
"Guten Morgen, Frau Yılmaz. Ich bin [Playername]. Ich möchte Sie jetzt waschen und frisch machen. Ist das in Ordnung?"
Frau Yılmaz nods weakly. Every physical action must be announced:
"Ich drehe Sie jetzt auf die rechte Seite." (I'm turning you onto your right side now.)
"Ich wasche Ihnen jetzt den Rücken." (I'm washing your back now.)
Failure to announce actions results in Frau Yılmaz becoming agitated, a dignity-violation flag in the documentation, and a talk with Frau Schneider.

Beat 3 — The Wound Check:
While repositioning Frau Yılmaz, the player inspects the Dekubitus (pressure ulcer) at the Kreuzbein (sacrum). The wound is approximately 3 cm x 2 cm, Grad II (partial thickness skin loss, reddish wound bed, no necrosis). The player must:

Clean the wound with sterile Kochsalzlösung (saline solution).
Apply a fresh Wundauflage (wound dressing — a hydrocolloid dressing, das Hydrokolloid-Pflaster).
Document wound status: size, color, exudate, odor, in the Wunddokumentation using standardized descriptors.
Beat 4 — The Übergabe (Shift Handover):
At 14:00, the early shift ends. The player sits in the Stationszimmer with the incoming Spätdienst (late shift) team. Frau Schneider leads the Übergabe. The player must verbally report on their residents:
"Frau Yılmaz: Grundpflege durchgeführt, Dekubitus-Versorgung am Kreuzbein — Wunde unverändert, kein Geruch, wenig Exsudat. Vitalzeichen stabil. Blutzucker Frau Kuhn: nüchtern hundertzweiunddreißig, Insulin verabreicht nach Anordnung."
This demands connected, professional speech — a major B1/B2 milestone.

Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise — Resident Fall with Head Injury (Sturzereignis)
Setup: Night shift (Nachtdienst), 02:30. The hallway is dark except for low safety lighting. The player is at the nurses' station documenting. A crash echoes from Zimmer 9 — Herr Hoffmann's room.

Beat 1 — Discovery:
The player rushes to Zimmer 9. Herr Hoffmann is on the floor beside his bed, nightgown twisted, confused, mumbling. There is blood on the linoleum near his forehead — a laceration (die Platzwunde) above the right eyebrow. He tried to get out of bed and fell (a classic Sturz bei Demenz).

Beat 2 — Immediate Response Protocol (Sturzprotokoll):
The player must:

Assess consciousness: "Herr Hoffmann, können Sie mich hören? Wissen Sie, wo Sie sind?" (He is disoriented but responsive — GCS 14.)
Do NOT move him until spinal injury is informally assessed (no neck pain on palpation).
Call for help: Use the internal phone to reach the Nacht-Bereitschaftspfleger/in: "Sturz in Zimmer neun. Herr Hoffmann. Kopfplatzwunde. Er ist bei Bewusstsein, aber desorientiert. Ich brauche Hilfe."
Apply pressure to the forehead laceration with a sterile Kompresse (gauze pad).
Check vital signs: Blood pressure, pulse, pupil reaction (Pupillenreaktion — are they equal, reactive to light? Testing for head trauma signs).
Beat 3 — The Physician Call:
The player must call the Bereitschaftsarzt (on-call physician):
"Guten Morgen, Doktor Krüger, hier spricht [Playername] von Station zwei, Haus Sonnenschein. Herr Hoffmann, vierundachtzig Jahre, Demenz, ist um zwei Uhr dreißig gestürzt. Kopfplatzwunde rechts frontal, circa drei Zentimeter. Er ist ansprechbar, aber zeitlich und örtlich desorientiert. Blutdruck einhundertfünfzig zu neunzig, Puls achtundachtzig, Pupillen seitengleich und lichtreagibel. Er nimmt Marcumar. Soll ich ihn ins Krankenhaus schicken?"
This is an extended B2-level telephone report under stress — requiring medical terminology, precise numbers, and appropriate formality. Dr. Krüger responds rapidly and may ask follow-up questions. Based on the Marcumar (blood thinner) information, the doctor orders a Krankenhauseinweisung (hospital admission) for a cranial CT.

Beat 4 — Documentation:
The player must complete:

das Sturzprotokoll (fall protocol form): date, time, location, circumstances, injuries, vital signs, actions taken, physician notified, resident/family notified.
an Ereignisbericht (incident report) for the Pflegedienstleitung (nursing service director).
Update the Pflegedokumentation with the fall and transfer to hospital.
5. The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)
Exam Structure:
The Pflegefachkraft completes a 3-year generalist nursing education (seit 2020 nach dem Pflegeberufegesetz), culminating in a staatliche Prüfung (state examination) with written, oral, and practical components. Foreign-trained nurses seeking Anerkennung (recognition) must pass a Kenntnisprüfung or Anpassungslehrgang. All require certified B2 German, often with the specialized Fachsprachprüfung Pflege.

Specific Exam Vocabulary:

German Term	English	Context
die staatliche Prüfung	state examination	Final certification exam for nurses
die Fachsprachprüfung Pflege (B2)	specialized language exam for nursing	Required by the Pflegekammer for foreign-trained nurses
die Kenntnisprüfung	knowledge/competency exam	For recognition of foreign nursing qualifications
der Anpassungslehrgang	adaptation course	Alternative to Kenntnisprüfung — supervised practice period
die Pflegeplanung	care plan	Written exam component — creating individualized care plans
die Pflegediagnose	nursing diagnosis	Identifying care needs using NANDA or similar frameworks
das Pflegemodell (z.B. Krohwinkel, Roper-Logan-Tierney)	nursing model	Theoretical frameworks tested in written exam
die Dekubitusprophylaxe	pressure ulcer prevention	Core practical exam topic
die Sturzprophylaxe	fall prevention	Core practical exam topic
die Biografiearbeit	biographical care work	Person-centered care approach — oral exam topic
das Fachgespräch (praktische Prüfung)	professional discussion (practical exam)	Oral defense after performing a care sequence
die Pflegedokumentation (prüfungsrelevant)	exam-relevant care documentation	Must be completed accurately during practical exam
der Pflegeprozess (6 Schritte)	nursing process (6 steps)	Assessment, diagnosis, planning, implementation, evaluation, documentation
die Berufsanerkennung	professional recognition	Legal process for foreign qualifications
die Pflegekammer	nursing chamber	Regulatory body (in applicable Bundesländer)
Visa Stabilization Link:
Upon passing the staatliche Prüfung or receiving Berufsanerkennung, the player receives the Erlaubnis zur Führung der Berufsbezeichnung "Pflegefachfrau / Pflegefachmann" (license to use the professional title), triggering a visa conversion to §18a AufenthG (Fachkräftevisum) or the fast-track §18c for recognized healthcare professionals. The 90-day countdown is replaced by a 4-year residence permit with a path to Niederlassungserlaubnis (permanent residence).

PROFESSION 3: FLUGGERÄTMECHANIKER/IN / INDUSTRIEMECHANIKER/IN (Aircraft / Aerospace / Industrial Machine Technician)
1. The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint
Visual Landscape:
The workplace is a massive industrial hangar (die Wartungshalle) at the Schönefeld technical area adjacent to BER airport, operated by a fictional MRO (Maintenance, Repair & Overhaul) company called "AeroBerlin Technik GmbH." The hangar is cathedral-sized — 60 meters wide, 80 meters deep, 25 meters high — with corrugated steel walls and a riveted ceiling grid of I-beams from which halogen flood lamps hang on chains, casting sharp white light and hard shadows. A single-aisle Airbus A320 sits in the center bay, its left engine nacelle open, revealing the turbofan core. Yellow-painted scaffolding (das Arbeitsgerüst) wraps around the fuselage. FOD (Foreign Object Debris/Damage) awareness posters are everywhere — red-bordered signs reading "FOD-ZONE: KEIN LOSES WERKZEUG AUF DEM BODEN." Tool shadow boards (die Werkzeugtafel mit Schattenmarkierung) line the walls — every tool has an outline; a missing tool is immediately visible. The floor is painted grey epoxy with green walkways, yellow equipment zones, and red no-go areas. Carts of numbered parts trays (die Teileschale) sit beside the aircraft. A Toolbox-Kontrollstation near the entry requires every technician to sign tools in and out on a digital log.

Specific Smells:
Aviation hydraulic fluid (Skydrol — a distinctive, acrid, phosphate-ester smell that irritates the nose and skin), Jet A-1 fuel residue (kerosene-like), Loctite thread-locking compound, isopropyl alcohol from cleaning swabs, de-icing fluid (propylene glycol — sweet, heavy), and the metallic tang of freshly drilled aluminum rivets. The hangar air is cold and carries the faint ozone-like scent of high-voltage electrical testing equipment.

Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:
With no engine running, the hangar sits at 65 dB — echoing footsteps, the whir of electric drills, pneumatic rivet guns in staccato bursts (105 dB, hearing protection mandatory). During an engine ground run (der Triebwerksprobelauf), the sound is overwhelming — 130+ dB outside, necessitating full ear protection and communication via hand signals or headset radio. Radios crackle with communication between the Teamleiter on the ground and the cockpit crew. PA announcements echo with heavy reverb off the steel walls.

The Meister / Boss Persona:
Name: Werkstattleiter / Prüfer Herr Dipl.-Ing. Klaus Richter
Age: 57
Personality: Herr Richter is a former Luftwaffe technician turned civilian MRO manager, tall, lean, silver-haired, always in a crisp dark-blue company coverall with his name embroidered above the left pocket. He holds a Prüfer-Lizenz (CAT A/B1) from the Luftfahrt-Bundesamt (LBA). He is meticulous to a degree that feels obsessive — because in aviation, it must be. He treats every loose screw as a potential catastrophe. He speaks in precise, almost clipped Hochdeutsch with zero dialect. He never raises his voice (shouting is for emergencies only). He uses the formal Sie permanently, even with long-term employees. He quizzes the player on ATA chapter numbers and torque values without warning.
Speech Cadence: Deliberate, measured, each word chosen. Pauses before important instructions to ensure the listener is attentive. Uses the phrase "Haben Sie das verstanden?" (Did you understand that?) after every critical instruction — the player must verbally confirm.
Expectations: Zero-tolerance for FOD. Every tool accounted for at every moment. Work must follow the approved AMM (Aircraft Maintenance Manual) procedure step-by-step — no shortcuts, no improvisation. Documentation (die Arbeitskarte) must be signed after each task step. The player must develop a culture of Rückfragen (asking clarifying questions) rather than guessing.

2. Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology
20 Specific Tools / Instruments / Materials:

#	German (with article)	Plural	English

#	Infinitiv	English	Example Context

3. The Workplace Register & Jargon
5 Exact German Imperative Commands on the Job:

"FOD-Check! Alles vom Boden aufheben!" — [ɛf oː deː tʃɛk | ˈaləs fɔm ˈboːdn̩ ˈaʊfheːbn̩] — "FOD check! Pick up everything from the floor!" (Called at every shift end and before aircraft movement.)
"Werkzeug rückzählen!" — [ˈvɛʁktsɔʏk ˈʁʏktsɛːlən] — "Count tools back in!" (Mandatory verification that every tool is returned to its shadow board — missing tool = aircraft grounded.)
"Freigabe erst nach Stempel!" — [ˈfʁaɪɡaːbə eːɐst naːx ˈʃtɛmpl̩] — "Release only after stamp!" (No aircraft component is returned to service without the authorized inspector's stamp on the task card.)
"Abstand halten — Triebwerk läuft!" — [ˈapʃtant ˈhaltn̩ — ˈtʁiːpvɛʁk lɔʏft] — "Keep distance — engine running!" (Safety command during ground run, with mandatory 10-meter exclusion zone.)
"Sicherungsdraht sitzt nicht — nochmal!" — [ˈzɪçəʁʊŋsdʁaːt zɪtst nɪçt — ˈnɔxmaːl] — "Safety wire isn't seated — again!" (Richter's correction when lockwire is improperly twisted.)
3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Expressions:

"In der Luftfahrt gibt es keine Beinahe-Unfälle — nur Vorfälle, die untersucht werden." — "In aviation there are no near-misses — only incidents that are investigated." (Richter's philosophy of mandatory reporting.)
"Das AMM ist dein Koran, deine Bibel und dein Grundgesetz." — "The AMM is your Quran, your Bible, and your constitution." (Said to new technicians to emphasize: follow the manual, always, completely.)
"Wer schlampt, fliegt — im doppelten Sinne." — "Whoever is sloppy flies — in both senses of the word." (Dark humor: you'll be fired, and a badly maintained plane might crash.)
4. Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)
Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Orientation, FOD Briefing & Tool Control
Setup: 07:00, the player reports to the Personaleingang (staff entrance) of the AeroBerlin Technik hangar. A security checkpoint scans their badge (der Werksausweis). The player changes into the company-issued coverall (der Arbeitsoverall) with reflective strips and steel-toed safety boots (die Sicherheitsstiefel S3). A hard hat (der Schutzhelm) and safety glasses (die Schutzbrille) are mandatory in the hangar bay.

Beat 1 — The FOD Briefing:
Herr Richter meets the player at the hangar entrance. Before entering, he stops:
"Bevor Sie diese Halle betreten: Leeren Sie Ihre Taschen. Alles. Schlüssel, Münzen, Kugelschreiber, Kaugummi. FOD — Foreign Object Damage — ist die größte Gefahr in der Luftfahrt. Ein einziger loser Gegenstand kann in ein Triebwerk gelangen und es zerstören. Haben Sie das verstanden?"
The player must physically empty their inventory into a personal effects locker (das Schließfach). A loose pen in the pocket triggers Richter's immediate correction.

Beat 2 — Tool Sign-Out:
The player approaches the Werkzeugausgabe (tool crib). They receive a numbered tool set (der nummerierte Werkzeugsatz). Every tool has a shadow outline on the board. The player must:

Count each tool and verify against the Werkzeugliste (tool list) — 47 items.
Sign the Werkzeugausgabeschein (tool issue form) with date, time, name, badge number.
If a tool is returned damaged or missing at shift end, it triggers a mandatory FOD search of the entire bay and a written Vorfall-Bericht (incident report).
Beat 3 — The Safety Walk:
Richter leads the player around the A320:
"Das hier ist das Bugrad." (This is the nose gear.) "Hier die Tragfläche, Sektion 11 nach ATA-Kapitel." (Here the wing, section 11 per ATA chapter.) "Der rote Bereich hinter dem Triebwerk — Gefahrenzone. Dort stehen Sie nie, wenn das Triebwerk läuft. Der Abgasstrahl hat über 400 Grad."
The player must identify and name components in German. Richter tests with pointing: "Was ist das?" — die Landeklappe (flap), das Höhenruder (elevator), die Vorflügel (slats).

Beat 4 — The First Assignment:
"Ihr erster Auftrag: Sichtprüfung der Fahrwerksschächte auf Leckagen und Korrosion. Arbeitskarte AW-320-1127. Gehen Sie jeden Punkt auf der Karte durch. Kreuzen Sie ab. Wenn Sie etwas finden, melden Sie es sofort. Beginnen Sie."
The player crawls under the aircraft with a Handleuchte (inspection lamp) and systematically inspects each area listed on the task card, checking boxes and writing brief German descriptions of findings.

Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — Brake Assembly Replacement on Main Landing Gear
Setup: Week 8. The player receives Arbeitskarte AW-320-2053: "Hauptfahrwerk links — Bremse erneuern. Referenz: AMM 32-41-11."

Beat 1 — AMM Procedure Retrieval:
The player accesses the digital AMM (Aircraft Maintenance Manual) on a rugged tablet, navigates to ATA Chapter 32 (Landing Gear), Section 41 (Wheels and Brakes), and reads the step-by-step procedure in technical German:
"Schritt 1: Fahrzeug aufbocken und sichern. Schritt 2: Rad demontieren. Schritt 3: Bremsscheiben auf Verschleiß prüfen (Mindestdicke: 8,2 mm). Schritt 4: Bremsbeläge ersetzen. Schritt 5: Drehmoment auf Radmuttern: 275 Nm ± 15 Nm. Schritt 6: Sicherungsdraht anbringen."

Beat 2 — Physical Execution:
Each step is performed in 3D with tool selection, precise torque application (a minigame requiring the player to stop a rotating gauge at the correct value), and safety wiring (a dexterity challenge where the player must twist the wire clockwise through two bolt heads in the correct pattern).

Beat 3 — Quality Inspection:
Herr Richter inspects the completed work. He checks the safety wire tension, the torque marking (a paint stripe across bolt and surface to visually verify no rotation has occurred), and the documentation. He asks:
"Welches Drehmoment haben Sie angewendet?"
Player: "Zweihundertfünfundsiebzig Newtonmeter, plus minus fünfzehn."
Richter: "Und der Sicherungsdraht? Rechtsherum oder linksherum gedrillt?"
Player: "Rechtsherum, in Anzugsrichtung."
Richter signs the Arbeitskarte with his Prüferstempel (inspector's stamp).

Beat 4 — Schichtübergabe (Shift Handover):
The player completes the paperwork and hands it to the incoming shift with a verbal summary:
"Hauptfahrwerk links, Bremse erneuert nach AMM 32-41-11. Bremsscheiben im Toleranzbereich. Alle Schrauben auf Drehmoment. Sicherungsdraht angebracht. Arbeitskarte unterzeichnet und gestempelt. Keine offenen Punkte."

Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise — Hydraulic Leak During Ground Inspection
Setup: Day 45. The player is conducting a pre-flight walkaround inspection. Under the left wing root, they notice a thin stream of purple-colored fluid dripping from a hydraulic line fitting onto the hangar floor. The fluid is Skydrol — toxic, corrosive to skin, and flammable in mist form.

Beat 1 — Recognition & Immediate Action:
The player must:

NOT touch the fluid with bare hands (Skydrol causes chemical burns).
Don chemical-resistant gloves (die Chemikalienschutzhandschuhe) from the emergency kit.
Place an absorbent mat (die Ölbindematte) under the drip.
Mark the area with a red caution cone (der Warnkegel).
Beat 2 — Reporting:
The player finds Herr Richter:
"Herr Richter, ich habe eine Leckage an der hydraulischen Leitung am linken Flügelwurzelbereich entdeckt. Es ist Skydrol. Die Leitung tropft aktiv. Ich habe Bindemittel untergelegt und den Bereich abgesperrt."
Richter's immediate response: "Gut. Hydraulik-System Eins unter Druck?"
Player must check the hydraulic pressure gauge: "Ja, das System steht noch unter Druck. Soll ich es drucklos machen?"
Richter: "Sofort. Drucklos schalten, absperren, Arbeitskarte erstellen. Dieses Flugzeug fliegt heute nicht."

Beat 3 — System Depressurization:
The player follows the AMM procedure to depressurize Hydraulic System 1: switching off the hydraulic pump in the cockpit, opening the pressure relief valve, and verifying zero pressure on the gauge. Each step demands correct verb usage: "Ich schalte die Hydraulikpumpe ab. Ich öffne das Druckentlastungsventil. Der Druck fällt. Null bar."

Beat 4 — Technical Investigation & Documentation:
The player and Richter inspect the fitting. A cracked B-nut (die Überwurfmutter) is the cause. The player documents: nature of defect, location (ATA 29-10-00), fluid type, estimated quantity lost, corrective action required. A Technical Log Entry (der Bordbucheintrag) is created. The aircraft is grounded (AOG — Aircraft on Ground) until the fitting is replaced and system tested.

5. The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)
Exam Structure:
The Fluggerätmechaniker/in completes a 3.5-year duale Ausbildung with IHK-Prüfung (Industrie- und Handelskammer). Additionally, for independent release authority, a technician must pursue the EASA Part-66 Lizenz (Cat A, B1, or B2) through the Luftfahrt-Bundesamt (LBA). The Industriemechaniker/in path follows a similar IHK structure without the aviation-specific licensing.

Specific Exam Vocabulary:

German Term	English	Context
die Gesellenprüfung / Abschlussprüfung (IHK)	final examination (IHK)	End of Ausbildung — practical and written
die EASA Part-66 Lizenz	EASA Part-66 License	European aviation maintenance license
das Luftfahrt-Bundesamt (LBA)	Federal Aviation Office	German aviation authority — issues licenses
die Arbeitskarte (Task Card)	task card	Must be correctly completed during practical exam
das Fachgespräch	technical oral discussion	Oral exam component
der Prüfungsausschuss	examination committee	Panel of IHK/LBA evaluators
die Fertigungstechnik (Prüfungsbereich)	manufacturing technology (exam area)	Written exam: machining, forming, joining
die Instandhaltungstechnik	maintenance technology	Core exam module — preventive and corrective maintenance
die Funktionsanalyse	functional analysis	Written exam: analyzing technical systems
die Qualitätssicherung (QS)	quality assurance (QA)	Exam section on ISO 9001, EN 9100 aviation QMS
das Human Factors Training	human factors training	Mandatory aviation psychology module (EASA Part-66 Module 9)
die zerstörungsfreie Prüfung (ZfP)	non-destructive testing (NDT)	Specialized inspection techniques (ultrasonic, dye penetrant)
die Freigabebescheinigung (EASA Form 1)	release certificate	Document confirming airworthiness after maintenance
der Ausbildungsnachweis	training record	Must be complete for exam admission
das Berichtsheft (wöchentlich)	weekly training logbook	Mandatory documentation of apprenticeship activities
Visa Stabilization Link:
Passing the IHK Abschlussprüfung yields the Facharbeiterbrief, enabling a §18a AufenthG Fachkräftevisum. Obtaining the EASA Part-66 license further qualifies the player for international mobility and higher salary bands — reflected in-game as improved housing options and social status.

PROFESSION 4: ELEKTRONIKER/IN FÜR ENERGIE- UND GEBÄUDETECHNIK (Building Electrician / Power Technician)
1. The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint
Visual Landscape:
The player works for a mid-sized Elektrofachbetrieb (electrical contracting company) called "Elektro Bergmann GmbH," based in a ground-floor workshop on a Neukölln side street. The Werkstatt has a front counter for Kundenberatung (customer consultation), a back workshop with a large workbench (die Werkbank) under LED strip lighting, a wire rack holding drums of NYM-J cable in grey, green-yellow, and blue, and wall-mounted panels of circuit breakers, RCDs, and fuse switches used for training and testing. Pegboards hold labeled bins of Lüsterklemmen (terminal strips), Aderendhülsen (wire ferrules), Abzweigdosen (junction boxes), Kabelkanäle (cable trunking), and Schalter/Steckdosen-Serien (switch/outlet series — Gira, Busch-Jaeger, Jung). A van (der Firmentransporter) parked outside is stocked with ladders (die Stehleitern), a portable Schlagbohrmaschine (hammer drill), and crates of sorted materials for each day's Baustelle (construction site). The majority of the player's workday, however, is NOT in this workshop — it is on-site: inside half-renovated Altbau apartments with exposed brick, dangling wires from gutted walls, dust sheets over furniture, and the constant noise of other trades. The player sees raw wall chases (die Schlitze) cut into plaster for cable routing, open Unterputzdosen (flush-mounted boxes) waiting for outlets, and a temporary Baustromverteiler (construction site power distribution board) clamped to a steel pole in the stairwell, feeding orange extension cables in every direction.

Specific Smells:
In the workshop: solder flux (sharp, rosin-scented), heated PVC from cable insulation during stripping, ozone from electrical arcing during testing, and the clean metallic scent of freshly cut copper conductor. On-site: plaster dust (chalky, throat-drying), Rigips (drywall compound — slightly sweet, mineral), sawdust from Trockenbau (dry construction) work happening simultaneously, the chemical sharpness of Kontaktspray (contact cleaner), and the acrid bite of freshly drilled concrete (hot masonry dust). In damp cellars (Kellerräume) during panel installations, there is the unmistakable musty smell of old Berlin basements — wet stone, old mortar, and sometimes mold.

Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:
On-site noise is relentless and layered: the Schlagbohrmaschine drilling into concrete registers at 100–110 dB (hearing protection mandatory). The Schlitzfräse (wall chaser) screams at 105 dB, producing a high-pitched grinding whine and clouds of dust. Angle grinders (der Winkelschleifer) used by other trades add piercing metallic shrieks. Between these bursts, the baseline is 70–80 dB — radio playing, workers calling across rooms, the rhythmic tap-tap of a colleague chiseling. In the workshop, the environment is quieter (55–65 dB): the hum of a soldering station, the click-click of wire strippers, the soft buzz of the test panel under load. Communication on-site often requires shouting or approaching within arm's length.

The Meister / Boss Persona:
Name: Meister Andreas Bergmann
Age: 46
Personality: Meister Bergmann is the owner-operator, a compact, energetic man with close-cropped dark hair, calloused hands, and a perpetual pencil behind his right ear. He earned his Meisterbrief at 28 and built the company from a one-van operation. He is technically brilliant, pedagogically impatient, and deeply safety-conscious — electricity kills silently, and he has seen the aftermath. He carries a well-worn DGUV Vorschrift 3 manual in his van. He oscillates between encouraging mentorship ("Das kriegst du hin, pass auf...") and explosive frustration when safety is compromised ("Bist du lebensmüde?! Der Strom ist noch drauf!"). He smokes hand-rolled cigarettes on breaks outside, where he becomes philosophical about the trade. He speaks rapid Berlinerisch with heavy consonant swallowing ("ick" for "ich," "det" for "das," "wa" as a tag), but switches to clean Hochdeutsch when explaining technical procedures or speaking to customers.
Speech Cadence: Staccato bursts when giving instructions on-site, often with directional gestures. Uses nicknames for tools ("Gib mal die Zwicke" for die Seitenschneiderzange). Ends explanations with "Klar soweit?" (Clear so far?).
Expectations: The golden rule: "Spannungsfreiheit feststellen — immer, bevor du irgendetwas anfasst." (Verify absence of voltage — always, before you touch anything.) Neat cable work is a matter of professional pride — no sagging cables, no uneven spacing. The player's Leitungsverlegung (cable routing) must be "im Lot und in der Waage" (plumb and level). Bergmann checks cable runs by eye and can spot a 2mm deviation.

2. Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology
20 Specific Tools / Instruments / Materials:

3. The Workplace Register & Jargon
5 Exact German Imperative Commands Shouted on the Job:

"Strom ist noch drauf — nicht anfassen!" — [ʃtʁoːm ɪst nɔx dʁaʊf — nɪçt ˈanfasn̩] — "Power is still on — don't touch!" (The most critical warning in the trade. Shouted when someone reaches toward a live circuit. Life-saving.)
"Sicherung raus!" — [ˈzɪçəʁʊŋ ʁaʊs] — "Breaker out!" (Command to trip/remove a circuit breaker before work begins.)
"Fünf Sicherheitsregeln beachten!" — [fʏnf ˈzɪçɐhaɪtsˌʁeːɡl̩n bəˈaxtn̩] — "Observe the five safety rules!" (Reference to the mandatory fünf Sicherheitsregeln der Elektrotechnik: 1. Freischalten, 2. Gegen Wiedereinschalten sichern, 3. Spannungsfreiheit feststellen, 4. Erden und Kurzschließen, 5. Benachbarte unter Spannung stehende Teile abdecken.)
"Prüf mal nach mit dem Duspol!" — [pʁyːf maːl naːx mɪt deːm ˈdʊspoːl] — "Check with the Duspol!" (Instruction to verify voltage-free status with the two-pole tester. "Duspol" is brand name used generically.)
"Klemm den PE zuerst an!" — [klɛm deːn peː eː tsueːɐst an] — "Connect the PE first!" (PE = Protective Earth / Schutzleiter. Always connected first, disconnected last — fundamental safety principle.)
3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:

"Strom macht klein, schwarz und hässlich." — "Electricity makes you small, black, and ugly." (Dark humor about electrocution. Said as a teaching warning to apprentices who take risks.)
"Erst messen, dann fassen — sonst lassen!" — "First measure, then touch — otherwise leave it!" (A rhyming safety mantra drilled into every electrical apprentice.)
"Das ist nicht VDE-konform." — "That's not VDE-compliant." (Said when any installation doesn't meet the DIN VDE standards — a serious professional criticism meaning the work must be redone entirely.)
4. Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)
Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Orientation, the Five Safety Rules & First Cable Run

Setup: 07:30 Monday. The player arrives at the Elektro Bergmann workshop. Meister Bergmann is loading the van (der Firmentransporter) with material crates for today's Baustelle — an Altbau apartment renovation on Weserstraße, third floor (dritter Obergeschoss / 3. OG).

Beat 1 — The Van & the Tool Check:
Bergmann tosses the player a set of keys: "Dein Werkzeugkoffer ist im Wagen. Kontrollier den Inhalt. Alles da, alles heil, alles VDE-geprüft?"
The player opens the orange-lidded Werkzeugkoffer (electrician's tool case) and must verify each tool against a laminated checklist glued inside the lid. Key items to identify and name in German:

die Abisolierzange ✓
der zweipolige Spannungsprüfer (Duspol) ✓
die Seitenschneiderzange (VDE) ✓
der Schraubendrehersatz (isoliert, Schlitz und Kreuz) ✓
die Crimpzange ✓
das Multimeter ✓
die Wasserwaage (spirit level — even electricians need level cable runs) ✓
If the player cannot name a tool when Bergmann points and asks "Was ist das?", the Meister corrects with a flash of impatience: "Das ist DIE Abisolierzange. Feminine. Merk dir das."

Beat 2 — Arrival at the Baustelle & the Five Safety Rules:
The apartment is gutted — bare brick, no flooring, plastic sheeting over window openings. A Baustromverteiler (temporary construction power board) is clamped in the hallway, humming faintly. Bergmann stops the player at the doorway.

"Bevor du hier irgendetwas anfasst, lernst du die fünf Sicherheitsregeln. Die wirst du im Schlaf aufsagen können, oder du arbeitest nicht an Strom. Niemals."

He holds up five fingers and counts:

"Freischalten." — Disconnect from the power supply.
"Gegen Wiedereinschalten sichern." — Secure against reconnection (lock-out/tag-out).
"Spannungsfreiheit feststellen." — Verify absence of voltage (with a Duspol, not a Phasenprüfer — Bergmann is emphatic about this).
"Erden und kurzschließen." — Ground and short-circuit (for high-voltage; in building electrics, steps 1–3 and 5 are most critical).
"Benachbarte, unter Spannung stehende Teile abdecken oder abschranken." — Cover or barricade adjacent live parts.
The player must repeat them back. In-game, this becomes a recurring quiz triggered at random moments — before every job, the system may flash: "Nenne die fünf Sicherheitsregeln" as a spaced-repetition check. Getting them wrong means the player cannot proceed.

Beat 3 — The First Assignment: Laying a Cable Run:
Bergmann points to a pre-cut wall chase running horizontally from a junction box position to a future outlet position, 1.2 meters from the floor:
"Dein erster Auftrag: Ein NYM-J 3x1,5 in den Schlitz verlegen, an beiden Enden zwanzig Zentimeter Überstand lassen, mit Gipsschellen fixieren, Abzweigdose setzen, abisolieren und die Aderenden mit Aderendhülsen vercrimpen. Farben: Braun ist Phase, Blau ist Nullleiter, Grün-Gelb ist Schutzleiter. Nicht verwechseln. Klar soweit?"

The player must:

Select the correct cable drum (NYM-J 3x1,5 mm²) from the van.
Measure the required length with a Zollstock (folding rule), adding 20 cm surplus at each end.
Cut the cable with the Seitenschneiderzange.
Lay the cable in the chase (der Schlitz), pressing it into the channel.
Fix it with Gipsschellen (plaster cable clips) every 30 cm.
At the Abzweigdose end: strip the outer sheath (der Mantel) back 8 cm, strip each individual wire (die Ader) 10 mm, identify colors (braun = L/Phase, blau = N/Neutralleiter, grün-gelb = PE/Schutzleiter), crimp Aderendhülsen onto each wire end.
Set the Abzweigdose into the wall.
Bergmann inspects: "Zeig mal die Aderendhülsen." He tugs each one. If any slips off: "Die sitzt nicht. Nochmal crimpen. Richtig fest."

Beat 4 — The Learning Moment:
As the player works, Bergmann asks casually: "Warum nehmen wir NYM-J und nicht NYY-J?"
The player must either know or learn: NYM-J is for indoor installation (Mantelleitung — sheathed cable, grey), while NYY-J is for underground/outdoor installation (Erdkabel — earth cable, black, moisture-resistant). Getting this wrong is not punished harshly — Bergmann uses it as a teaching moment: "NYM ist für innen, NYY ist für außen, im Erdreich. Verwechselst du das auf einer Prüfung, bist du durchgefallen."

Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — Wiring a Sub-Distribution Board (Unterverteilung)

Setup: Week 6. The player has earned basic trust. The Baustelle apartment is nearing completion. Today's Arbeitsauftrag: "Unterverteilung im Flur verdrahten und in Betrieb nehmen."

Beat 1 — Reading the Schaltplan (Wiring Diagram):
Bergmann hands the player a printed Schaltplan (circuit diagram) and an Installationsplan (installation plan showing outlet/switch positions on a floor plan). The Unterverteilung (sub-distribution board) must contain:

1x Hauptschalter (main switch) — 40A
1x FI-Schalter (RCD) — 40A / 30mA
6x Sicherungsautomaten (MCBs) — B16A for general circuits, B10A for lighting circuits
1x Klingeltransformator (doorbell transformer) — 8V AC
Hutschiene (DIN rail), Phasenschiene (busbar), Klemmleiste (terminal block)
The player must read the Schaltplan and identify which MCB protects which circuit:

Stromkreis 1: Küche Steckdosen (kitchen outlets) — B16A
Stromkreis 2: Wohnzimmer Steckdosen — B16A
Stromkreis 3: Bad Steckdosen (bathroom outlets) — B16A
Stromkreis 4: Beleuchtung (lighting) — B10A
Stromkreis 5: Herd (stove) — B16A, separate circuit
Stromkreis 6: Waschmaschine (washing machine) — B16A, separate circuit
Beat 2 — Physical Assembly:
The player works at the open Zählerschrank (meter cabinet), mounted on the hallway wall:

Snap the Hutschiene (DIN rail) into the cabinet.
Click in the components from left to right: Hauptschalter → FI-Schalter → Sicherungsautomaten (6 pieces) → Klingeltransformator.
Install the Phasenschiene (busbar/comb bar) connecting the FI output to all MCBs.
Wire the incoming supply: L (braun), N (blau), PE (grün-gelb) from the Hausanschluss (building connection) to the Hauptschalter.
Wire the outgoing circuits: each NYM-J cable from the apartment circuits connects to its designated MCB (L) and the N-Klemmleiste (neutral terminal block) and PE-Klemmleiste (earth terminal block).
Every connection must be tight. Bergmann's test: he tugs every wire. A loose connection is a fire hazard.
Beat 3 — Testing & Measurement (Erstprüfung nach VDE 0100-600):
Before the system can go live, the player must perform the Erstprüfung (initial verification) per VDE 0100 Teil 600:

Sichtprüfung (Visual inspection): Check for correct cable colors, tight connections, proper labeling of circuits on the MCB legend card (die Beschriftung), no visible damage.
Schutzleiterwiderstand messen (Protective conductor resistance): Using the Multimeter or a dedicated Installationstester, measure the PE continuity from each outlet back to the main earth bar. Must be < 1 Ω. Player states: "Schutzleiterwiderstand Stromkreis eins: null Komma drei Ohm. In Ordnung."
Isolationswiderstand messen (Insulation resistance): Between L and PE, N and PE, L and N. Must be > 1 MΩ (Megaohm). "Isolationswiderstand L gegen PE: zweihundertdreißig Megaohm. In Ordnung."
FI-Schalter prüfen (RCD test): Press the test button — the RCD must trip within 200 ms. Then test with the Installationstester at 30 mA: "Auslösezeit: fünfundzwanzig Millisekunden. Auslösestrom: zweiundzwanzig Milliampere. Bestanden."
Schleifenimpedanz messen (Loop impedance): Ensure fault current will trip the MCB within the required time. Player reads: "Schleifenimpedanz: null Komma achtundsiebzig Ohm."
All values are recorded on a Prüfprotokoll (test protocol / Erstprüfung report) — a standardized form with fields for each measurement, the tester's name, date, and signature.

Beat 4 — Reporting & Inbetriebnahme (Commissioning):
The player reports to Bergmann:
"Meister Bergmann, die Unterverteilung ist fertig verdrahtet und geprüft. Alle Messwerte sind im Protokoll. Schutzleiter, Isolation, FI und Schleifenimpedanz — alles in Ordnung. Kann ich einschalten?"
Bergmann reviews the Prüfprotokoll, checks one random connection himself, and nods:
"Gut. Schalte ein. Hauptschalter zuerst, dann FI, dann die Automaten einzeln von links nach rechts."
The player flips each switch. Lights come on. Outlets are tested with a Steckdosenprüfgerät (outlet tester) — green LEDs confirm correct wiring. A mis-wired outlet (e.g., swapped L and PE) would show a red LED error pattern — a teachable failure moment.

Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise — Electrical Short Circuit with Arc Flash

Setup: Day 50. The player is working alone in a Keller (basement) of an older Mehrfamilienhaus (apartment building), replacing an outdated Zählerkasten. Bergmann is upstairs in another apartment. The building's Hauptsicherung (main fuse) has been pulled, but an adjacent meter cabinet for another tenant's unit is still live.

Beat 1 — The Crisis Trigger:
The player is routing a new cable along the wall near the adjacent live cabinet. Their drill bit, boring through the wall to route a cable, breaches into the back of the adjacent cabinet and strikes a live busbar. There is a blinding white-blue arc flash (der Lichtbogen), a deafening BANG, a shower of molten copper sparks, and the basement plunges into darkness as the building's main RCD trips. The air smells of burned insulation — acrid, choking. The player's right hand is tingling from a secondary shock transmitted through the drill housing (the VDE insulation prevented full electrocution, but the transient voltage was felt).

Beat 2 — Immediate Response:
The player must:

Drop the drill immediately.
Assess themselves: "Kann ich meine Finger bewegen? Ja. Verbrennungen? Nein, aber Kribbeln im rechten Arm." (Can I move my fingers? Yes. Burns? No, but tingling in the right arm.)
NOT attempt to restore power — the arc may have damaged wiring.
Use a Taschenlampe (flashlight) from their tool belt to see.
Retreat from the area and secure it — no one else enters the Keller.
Beat 3 — Calling for Help:
The player calls Bergmann from the stairwell:
"Meister Bergmann! Kurzschluss im Keller! Lichtbogen! Ich habe beim Bohren eine unter Spannung stehende Sammelschiene im Nachbarschrank getroffen. Die Anlage hat ausgelöst. Ich habe einen leichten Schlag bekommen — rechte Hand kribbelt, aber ich bin bei Bewusstsein."

Bergmann's response is immediate, rapid, and intense:
"Bist du verletzt? Kannst du gehen? — Gut. Komm sofort raus aus dem Keller. Fass nichts mehr an. Gar nichts. Ich rufe den Netzbetreiber an, die müssen die Hauptleitung freischalten. Das ist jetzt ein Fall für den Bereitschaftsdienst. Hast du die Einschlagstelle gesehen? Welcher Schrank war das?"

Beat 4 — Post-Incident Protocol:
Bergmann arrives in the Keller with his own Duspol, inspects the damage (melted busbar, scorched cable insulation, drill bit welded into the copper bar), and initiates the mandatory protocol:

The player must be examined by a Durchgangsarzt (D-Arzt — occupational accident physician) because of the electrical contact — this is legally required, even without visible injury. A game sequence takes the player to the D-Arzt appointment, requiring medical vocabulary: "Ich habe einen Stromschlag erlitten. Kribbeln in der rechten Hand. Kein Bewusstseinsverlust."
An Unfallmeldung (accident report) is filed with the Berufsgenossenschaft BG ETEM (employers' liability insurance for the electrical trade).
Bergmann files a report and the player fills out their portion: date, time, location, description of events, injuries, witnesses.
The learning scar: Bergmann's debrief is quiet and serious. "Du hast Glück gehabt. Ein Lichtbogen kann dreitausend Grad heiß werden. Das nächste Mal: Leitungssucher benutzen, bevor du bohrst. Immer. Auch wenn du denkst, da ist nichts." The Leitungssucher (cable tracer) becomes a permanent gameplay requirement — forgetting it triggers an automatic warning prompt.
5. The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)
Exam Structure:
The Elektroniker/in für Energie- und Gebäudetechnik completes a 3.5-year duale Ausbildung, culminating in the Gesellenprüfung Teil 1 (gestreckte Abschlussprüfung, after 18 months) and Gesellenprüfung Teil 2 (at the end), administered by the Handwerkskammer (HWK) Berlin. The exam includes written, practical, and oral components.

Specific Exam Vocabulary:

German Term	English	Context
die Gesellenprüfung Teil 1 / Teil 2	journeyman's exam part 1 / part 2	Stretched final examination format
der Gesellenbrief	journeyman's certificate	Issued upon passing — key for visa
die Handwerkskammer (HWK)	Chamber of Skilled Crafts	Certifying body
die Kundenanlage (gemäß VDE 0100)	customer installation (per VDE 0100)	Practical exam: build and test a complete installation
die Installationsschaltung (Wechsel-, Kreuz-, Serienschaltung)	installation circuit types (two-way, intermediate, series)	Must be wired correctly in practical exam
die Wechselschaltung	two-way switching circuit	A core exam task — wiring a light controlled from two points
die Kreuzschaltung	intermediate switching circuit	Extension of Wechselschaltung — three or more control points
der Stromlaufplan (aufgelöste / zusammenhängende Darstellung)	circuit diagram (resolved / consolidated form)	Must be read and drawn in written exam
der Installationsplan	installation plan (floor-plan based)	Translating a circuit diagram to physical layout
das Prüfprotokoll nach VDE 0100-600	test protocol per VDE 0100-600	Must be completed during practical exam
die Erstprüfung / Wiederholungsprüfung	initial verification / periodic re-inspection	Exam knowledge: when each is required
die DGUV Vorschrift 3 (ehem. BGV A3)	DGUV regulation 3 (formerly BGV A3)	Workplace electrical safety regulation — written exam
die fünf Sicherheitsregeln	the five safety rules	Must be recited perfectly in oral exam
die VDE-Normenreihe	VDE standards series	Referenced throughout written exam (VDE 0100, 0105, 0701/0702)
das Fachgespräch	technical oral discussion	Oral defense of practical work before the Prüfungsausschuss
Visa Stabilization Link:
The Gesellenbrief enables the player's visa conversion to §18a AufenthG (Fachkräftevisum). Furthermore, the Elektroniker/in is a regulated trade — the player cannot legally perform independent electrical work without the Gesellenbrief or Meisterbrief, making this certification existentially critical for both legal residency and career progression.

PROFESSION 5: ANLAGENMECHANIKER/IN FÜR SANITÄR-, HEIZUNGS- UND KLIMATECHNIK (SHK) (Plumber / HVAC Heating & Air Conditioning Technician)
1. The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint
Visual Landscape:
The player works for "Haustechnik Krüger GmbH," a family-owned SHK-Betrieb (plumbing, heating, and air conditioning company) located in a courtyard workshop (die Hinterhofwerkstatt) accessed through a brick archway off Sonnenallee. The workshop is a converted ground-floor industrial space with concrete flooring, a pit area for cutting and threading pipes, and open metal shelving stacked with copper pipe bundles (sorted by diameter: 12mm, 15mm, 18mm, 22mm, 28mm), PVC drain pipes (HT-Rohr — grey, for wastewater), press-fit system components (Viega Profipress), and boxes of fittings — T-Stücke (tee fittings), Bögen (elbows — 90° and 45°), Muffen (couplings), Verschraubungen (threaded unions). A wall-mounted Gewindeschneidmaschine (pipe threading machine) sits in the corner, its cast-iron body oily and scarred from decades of use. Coils of copper pipe hang from ceiling hooks. A gas testing station with a Lecksuchgerät (leak detector) occupies a bench. The van — a white Mercedes Sprinter with the company logo — is the player's primary workplace, loaded daily with job-specific materials. Most work happens in Berlin apartments: cramped bathrooms being renovated, dusty boiler rooms (die Heizungskeller) in Altbau basements, and occasionally rooftop installations for solar thermal panels (die Solarthermieanlage).

Specific Smells:
Copper flux paste (leicht süßlich-metallisch — slightly sweet-metallic) used for soldering; the sharp tang of Lötfett (soldering flux) when heated; the warm, oily scent of Hanf und Fermit (hemp thread and pipe-sealing compound — traditional German pipe sealing method, distinctive earthy-chemical smell); the sulfurous whiff of gas (Erdgas is normally odorless, but the odorant Tetrahydrothiophen / THT is added — the player must learn to recognize this smell as a danger signal); damp basement air mixed with mold and rust from old heating systems; PVC primer/cement (aggressive chemical solvent smell); and the clean, mineral scent of fresh Trinkwasser (drinking water) flushing through newly installed copper pipes.

Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:
Workshop noise: the rhythmic chunk-chunk-chunk of the Gewindeschneidmaschine threading steel pipe at 85 dB; the whine of the Rohrabschneider (pipe cutter) on copper; the hiss-roar of the Hartlöt-Brenner (brazing torch) at 70 dB. On-site: the building's own acoustic environment — echoing stairwells, voices from neighboring apartments, the gurgle and hammer of old pipe systems (Wasserschlag — water hammer), the click-whoosh of a gas boiler igniting. In Heizungskeller: the low, constant rumble of the Heizkessel (boiler) and the ticking of expanding metal pipes. Communication is usually at normal volume except when using power tools.

The Meister / Boss Persona:
Name: Meister Jürgen Krüger
Age: 58
Personality: Meister Krüger is a bear of a man — 190 cm, broad-shouldered, with a grey beard, thick forearms, and hands the size of dinner plates, permanently calloused and crosshatched with small scars from decades of copper work. He inherited the Betrieb from his father and represents the second generation. He wears the traditional SHK Zunftkleidung (guild clothing) — black corduroy trousers and a blue Arbeitsjacke — on formal occasions but typically works in grey coveralls. He is a man of few words, but every word carries weight. He believes the SHK trade is noble and essential: "Ohne uns gibt es kein sauberes Wasser und keine warme Wohnung." (Without us, there is no clean water and no warm apartment.) He teaches by demonstration: he performs a task once, explains while doing it, then expects the player to replicate. He has zero patience for wasting materials — "Kupfer ist teuer" is his mantra. He speaks slow, deliberate Berlinisch with occasional Plattdeutsch expressions from his family's northern roots. He addresses apprentices with "du" from day one — in his workshop, formality is replaced by respect shown through competence.
Speech Cadence: Slow, deliberate, often pauses mid-sentence to demonstrate a hand movement. Uses "Guck mal" (Look here) frequently before demonstrations. Sighs audibly when displeased rather than shouting.
Expectations: Werkzeug pflegen (maintain your tools — clean, oil, and store correctly every day). Measure twice, cut once — literally: "Zweimal messen, einmal schneiden." Connections must be dicht (leak-tight) — he tests every joint and has no tolerance for even a Tropfen (a drip). Punctuality, sobriety, and a willingness to carry heavy materials up narrow Berlin Altbau stairwells without complaining.
