3. The Workplace Register & Jargon
5 Exact German Imperative Commands Shouted on the Job:

"Wasser abstellen — sofort!" — [ˈvasɐ ˈapʃtɛlən — zoˈfɔʁt] — "Turn off the water — now!" (Shouted during an unexpected leak or burst pipe. Urgent — every second means more water damage.)
"Halt dagegen, ich zieh an!" — [halt daˈɡeːɡn̩, ɪç tsiː an] — "Hold against it, I'm tightening!" (One person holds the pipe fitting with a wrench while the other tightens the nut. Requires coordinated effort.)
"Flamme weg vom Holz!" — [ˈflamə vɛk fɔm hɔlts] — "Flame away from the wood!" (Warning when brazing near combustible materials — wooden joists, insulation. Fire risk.)
"Riechst du Gas?!" — [ʁiːçst duː ɡaːs] — "Can you smell gas?!" (Critical safety question. If yes: immediate evacuation, no electrical switches, no open flame, call Gasnotruf / GASAG Störungsdienst.)
"Lass mal Druck drauf!" — [las maːl dʁʊk dʁaʊf] — "Put pressure on it!" (Command to activate the Abdrückpumpe for a pressure test on a newly installed system.)
3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:

"Dicht ist dicht — ein bisschen undicht gibt es nicht." — "Tight is tight — a little bit leaky doesn't exist." (Krüger's absolute standard for pipe connections. Either the joint holds or it's redone.)
"Wasser findet immer einen Weg." — "Water always finds a way." (Philosophical-practical warning that any imperfection in a system will eventually manifest as a leak — often in the worst possible place, like above someone's ceiling.)
"Der Hanf muss in Anzugsrichtung gewickelt werden." — "The hemp must be wrapped in the tightening direction." (Instructional proverb — if wound the wrong way, the hemp unravels when the fitting is screwed on, causing a leak. A classic apprentice mistake.)
4. Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)
Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Workshop Orientation, Material Identification & First Soldering Exercise

Setup: 07:15 Tuesday. The player arrives at the Hinterhof workshop. Meister Krüger is already at his workbench, fitting a press coupling onto a copper pipe. He looks up: "Morgen. Zieh dir 'ne Latzhose an und Sicherheitsschuhe. Dann kommst du her."

Beat 1 — The Material Tour:
Krüger walks the player through the workshop's Regalsystem (shelving system), pointing and naming:

"Das ist Kupferrohr, fünfzehn Millimeter Außendurchmesser. Das hier, zweiundzwanzig Millimeter. Für Trinkwasser, immer Kupfer oder Edelstahl." (This is copper pipe, 15mm OD. This, 22mm. For drinking water, always copper or stainless steel.)
"Das hier ist HT-Rohr — für Abwasser. Grau, Kunststoff. Verschiedene Durchmesser: DN 40 für Waschbecken, DN 50 für Dusche, DN 75 oder 100 für Toilette." (This is HT pipe — for wastewater. Grey, plastic. Various diameters.)
"Fittings. T-Stücke, Bögen neunzig Grad, Bögen fünfundvierzig Grad, Muffen, Verschraubungen, Reduzierungen." (Fittings. Tees, 90° elbows, 45° elbows, couplings, unions, reducers.)
He holds up a handful of small items: "Aderendhülsen sind beim Elektriker. Bei uns heißen die hier: Klemmringverschraubungen. Guck mal: der Klemmring, die Überwurfmutter, die Stützhülse." (Compression fittings. Look: the compression ring, the cap nut, the support sleeve.)
At each stop, the player must select the correct German noun with article from a radial menu. Krüger tests constantly: "Was ist der Unterschied zwischen einem Bogen und einem Winkel?" (A Bogen is a gradual, formed bend; a Winkel / Winkelstück is a sharp-angle fitting.)

Beat 2 — The Safety Briefing:
Krüger shows the player the Gasflasche (gas bottle — propane) used for the Lötbrenner:
"Das ist Propangas. Brennbar. Wenn du mit der Flamme arbeitest: immer eine Löschdecke bereithalten. Feuerlöscher steht da. Und: Hitzeschutzblech hinter das Rohr, wenn du in der Nähe von Holz oder Dämmmaterial lötest."
(That is propane gas. Flammable. When you work with the flame: always have a fire blanket ready. Fire extinguisher is there. And: heat shield plate behind the pipe when you solder near wood or insulation.)

The player must locate and physically pick up: die Löschdecke (fire blanket), der Feuerlöscher (fire extinguisher), das Hitzeschutzblech (heat protection plate). Each is named in German during the interaction.

Beat 3 — First Soldering Exercise (Weichlöten):
Krüger clamps a practice copper pipe (15mm) in a vice and demonstrates a capillary soldered joint step-by-step:

Rohr ablängen (Cut pipe to length with the Rohrschneider) — smooth, even rotation.
Rohr entgraten (Deburr pipe — both inside and outside with the Rohrentgrater).
Rohr und Fitting reinigen (Clean pipe end and fitting interior with Schmirgelvlies / abrasive pad — shiny copper surface required for solder flow).
Flussmittel auftragen (Apply flux paste thinly and evenly to the pipe end).
Fitting aufstecken (Push fitting onto pipe — twist to distribute flux).
Erwärmen (Heat the joint with the Lötbrenner — the flame circles the fitting, NOT directly on the solder point. The heat draws the solder in by capillary action).
Lötzinn zuführen (Feed solder wire to the joint — when the flux bubbles and the copper reaches the correct temperature, the solder flows into the gap by itself).
Abkühlen lassen und Flussmittelreste entfernen (Allow to cool and remove flux residue with a damp cloth).
"Jetzt du." Krüger hands the player a pipe, fittings, and the torch. The player must replicate the process. If the solder blobs on the outside instead of flowing in, Krüger sighs: "Das Rohr war nicht heiß genug. Das Lot muss reingezogen werden, nicht draufgeschmiert. Nochmal."

Beat 4 — Material Waste Lecture:
If the player wastes material (drops fittings, bends pipe incorrectly and discards it), Krüger delivers his signature line: "Kupfer ist teuer. Jedes Stück, das du in den Schrott wirfst, geht von meinem Gewinn ab. Und irgendwann von deinem Lohn." (Copper is expensive. Every piece you throw in the scrap comes off my profit. And eventually off your wages.)

Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — Radiator Installation in an Altbau Apartment

Setup: Week 5. Arbeitsauftrag: "Altbau Weserstraße 34, 2. OG links. Alten Gliederheizkörper entfernen, neuen Flachheizkörper (Typ 22, Buderus Logatrend) montieren. Thermostatventil erneuern."

Beat 1 — Arriving at the Customer's Apartment:
The player rings the doorbell. Frau Fischer, 67, opens. The player must greet professionally:
"Guten Tag, Frau Fischer. Mein Name ist [Playername], von Haustechnik Krüger. Wir kommen wegen des neuen Heizkörpers. Darf ich reinkommen?"
Frau Fischer lets them in, offers coffee (the player can accept or decline — accepting builds Kundenzufriedenheit / customer satisfaction). She shows the old Gliederheizkörper (sectional radiator) in the Wohnzimmer under the window: cast iron, painted over ten times, one valve dripping.

Beat 2 — Removing the Old Radiator:
The player must:

Heizung abstellen — Close the Vorlauf-Ventil (flow valve) and the Rücklauf-Verschraubung (return union) on the radiator.
Heizkörper entlüften — Open the Entlüftungsschraube to break the vacuum and allow draining.
Restwasser ablassen — Place a Auffangwanne underneath and open the Rücklaufverschraubung. Dirty, rusty water drains out.
Heizkörper abmontieren — Unscrew the unions and lift the radiator off its Wandkonsole (wall brackets). Old Gliederheizkörper are extremely heavy (~45 kg). The player must call for help (the colleague Azubi Marcel) or risk a back injury — a gameplay consequence.
Wandkonsolen entfernen — Remove old brackets, fill old Dübellöcher (anchor holes) with Spachtelmasse (filler).
Beat 3 — Mounting the New Radiator:

Neue Wandkonsolen montieren — Measure, mark, drill (die Schlagbohrmaschine + Steinbohrer), insert Dübel (wall anchors), screw in new brackets. Must be level (die Wasserwaage) and at the correct height: Oberkante Heizkörper (radiator top edge) typically 15 cm below the Fensterbank (window sill).
Neuen Heizkörper einhängen — Hang the Flachheizkörper on the brackets.
Thermostatventil einbauen — Thread the new Thermostatventil into the Vorlauf connection, seal with Hanf und Fermit, tighten with the Rohrzange. Attach the Thermostatkopf (thermostat head) — set to position 3 (≈ 20°C).
Rücklaufverschraubung anschließen — Connect the return pipe union.
System befüllen und entlüften — Open the valves, allow the system to fill, then bleed the radiator using the Entlüftungsschlüssel (bleed key). Air hisses out, then water flows — close the valve. "Kein Luftgeräusch mehr? Gut, der ist entlüftet."
Beat 4 — Pressure Test & Customer Handover:
The player checks all connections for leaks — running a finger along each joint, looking for moisture (Feuchtigkeit). Then checks the Manometer on the boiler: system pressure should be 1.2–1.8 bar. If pressure has dropped significantly, there is a leak somewhere.

Report to Frau Fischer:
"Frau Fischer, der neue Heizkörper ist montiert. Ich habe das Thermostatventil auch erneuert. Die Einstellung auf Stufe drei entspricht ungefähr zwanzig Grad. Wenn Sie es wärmer möchten, drehen Sie auf vier. Bitte prüfen Sie in den nächsten Tagen, ob irgendwo Wasser austritt, und rufen Sie uns dann an."

The player fills out a Arbeitsbericht (job report) with: Kundenname, Adresse, durchgeführte Arbeiten, verwendetes Material, Arbeitszeit, Unterschrift Kunde. Frau Fischer signs.

Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise — Gas Leak in a Basement Boiler Room

Setup: Day 40. The player is called to an emergency: "Gasgeruch im Heizungskeller, Sonnenallee 78. Hausverwaltung hat angerufen. Sofort hinfahren." Krüger and the player drive to the site.

Beat 1 — Arrival & Sensory Confirmation:
Entering the stairwell, the player detects the unmistakable rotten-egg smell of odorized Erdgas (natural gas / THT odorant). The smell intensifies near the Kellertür (basement door). Krüger stops immediately:
"Riechst du das? Gas. Keller nicht betreten. Kein Licht anschalten. Kein Klingelknopf drücken. Nichts, was einen Funken erzeugen kann."

Beat 2 — Emergency Protocol:
The player and Krüger must:

Alle Bewohner informieren — Ring doorbells (physical knocking, NOT electric bells) on each floor: "Bitte verlassen Sie sofort das Gebäude. Gasaustritt im Keller. Benutzen Sie nicht den Aufzug. Nicht rauchen. Kein offenes Feuer."
Gebäude räumen — Evacuate residents to the sidewalk, at least 10 meters from the building.
Gasnotdienst rufen — Call GASAG Störungsdienst (Berlin gas emergency service): "Gasgeruch in einem Mehrfamilienhaus. Adresse: Sonnenallee achtundsiebzig. Der Geruch kommt aus dem Heizungskeller. Wir haben das Gebäude geräumt. Bitte kommen Sie sofort."
Feuerwehr rufen — 112: "Gasaustritt, Sonnenallee achtundsiebzig, Berlin-Neukölln. Gebäude wird geräumt."
Wenn sicher erreichbar: Gaszähler-Haupthahn schließen — If the main gas shut-off valve (der Gas-Hauptabsperrhahn) is accessible WITHOUT entering the gas-filled Keller, close it. Krüger decides: "Der Haupthahn ist am Hauseingang im Zählerkasten. Ich mach den zu."
Beat 3 — GASAG and Feuerwehr Arrival:
The GASAG Störungsdienst-Techniker arrives with a gas detector (das Gasmessgerät / Explosimeter). He enters the Keller with respiratory protection and measures: "Dreizehn Prozent UEG. Unter der unteren Explosionsgrenze, aber knapp. Wir belüften jetzt." (13% LEL. Below the lower explosive limit, but close. We're ventilating now.)

The Feuerwehr (fire department) sets up a Lüfter (ventilation fan) at the Kellertür. After ventilation brings the gas level to zero, the GASAG technician identifies the source: a corroded Gasleitung (gas pipe) at a threaded joint on the Gaszuleitung (gas supply line) to the Heizkessel (boiler). A pin-hole leak (ein Nadelloch / Haarriss) in the corroded thread.

Beat 4 — Repair & Aftermath:
Krüger is authorized (as a SHK-Meister with Gasinstallateur-Zulassung from the DVGW / Netzgesellschaft) to perform the repair:

The gas supply remains off.
The corroded pipe section is cut out with a Rohrschneider.
A new pipe section is threaded (Gewindeschneidkluppe), sealed with Gewindedichtband (PTFE tape — for gas, yellow-coded, not white plumber's tape), and installed.
A Leckprüfung (leak test) is performed: Lecksuchspray (leak detection spray) applied to every joint. No bubbles = dicht. Additionally, the Gasleitung is pressure-tested with the Lecksuchgerät — audible alarm = Leck, silence = dicht.
The GASAG technician performs a final Druckprüfung (pressure test) on the entire gas installation before authorizing reopening: 150 mbar for 10 minutes, no pressure drop permitted.
The player documents: Gasgeruch gemeldet um [Uhrzeit], Gebäude geräumt, GASAG und Feuerwehr verständigt, Ursache: korrodierte Gewindverbindung an der Gaszuleitung, Reparatur durchgeführt, Dichtigkeitsprüfung bestanden, Gasversorgung wiederhergestellt um [Uhrzeit].

Krüger debriefs: "Gas ist kein Spaß. Wenn du Gas riechst, denkst du nicht nach — du handelst. Keine Diskussion, keine Experimente. Raus, Notruf, absperren. Alles andere kommt danach."

5. The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)
Exam Structure:
The Anlagenmechaniker/in SHK completes a 3.5-year duale Ausbildung with the Gesellenprüfung administered by the Handwerkskammer (HWK). Additionally, gas installation work requires a separate Eintragung in das Installateurverzeichnis (registration in the installer directory) of the local gas supplier (GASAG / Netzgesellschaft Berlin), based on passing the DVGW-TRGI examination (Technische Regel für Gasinstallationen).

Specific Exam Vocabulary:

German Term	English	Context
die Gesellenprüfung Teil 1 / Teil 2	journeyman's exam part 1 / part 2	Practical and theoretical components
die Kundenauftragsarbeit	customer order work piece	Practical exam: install a complete system from a work order
die Arbeitsplanung	work planning	Written exam: material lists, time estimates, procedures
die Rohrleitungstechnik	piping technology	Exam area: materials, joining methods, dimensioning
die Wärmelehre (Thermodynamik)	heat theory (thermodynamics)	Written exam: heat transfer, expansion, efficiency
die Trinkwasserverordnung (TrinkwV)	drinking water ordinance	Legal standards for potable water installations
die DIN 1988 (Trinkwasserinstallation)	DIN 1988 (drinking water installation)	Technical standard tested in written exam
die DVGW-TRGI (Technische Regel für Gasinstallationen)	technical rules for gas installations	Mandatory knowledge for gas work authorization
die Abgasmessung (nach BImSchV)	flue gas measurement (per emissions ordinance)	Testing combustion efficiency of heating systems
die Schornsteinfegerabnahme	chimney sweep inspection/acceptance	Legal inspection required before boiler commissioning
das Fachgespräch	technical oral discussion	Oral defense before the Prüfungsausschuss
die Druckprüfung (Dichtheitsprüfung)	pressure test (leak test)	Core practical exam task
die Rohrdimensionierung	pipe sizing / dimensioning	Written exam: calculating flow rates, pipe diameters
die Wärmebedarfsberechnung	heat demand calculation	Planning a heating system — written exam
der Gesellenbrief	journeyman's certificate	Issued upon passing
Visa Stabilization Link:
The Gesellenbrief, combined with the DVGW-TRGI certification for gas work, enables the §18a AufenthG Fachkräftevisum. The SHK trade is among Germany's most in-demand professions, and the player receives an in-game notification from the Ausländerbehörde recognizing the shortage occupation (Engpassberuf), which accelerates visa processing.

PROFESSION 6: KOCH / KÖCHIN (Professional Chef / Commercial Kitchen Cook)
1. The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint
Visual Landscape:
The player works in the kitchen of "Gasthaus zum Goldenen Löwen," a mid-scale German-Mediterranean restaurant on Weserstraße with 75 covers. The Küche (kitchen) is a long, narrow rectangle — 12 meters by 5 meters — with stainless steel everywhere: work surfaces (die Arbeitsflächen), shelving, the pass (die Durchreiche), and the hood extraction system (die Dunstabzugshaube) running the full length of the cooking line. The cooking line (die Kochzeile) consists of, from left to right: a six-burner gas range (der Gasherd, sechs Flammen) with a solid-top section (die Bratplatte), a Salamander grill (der Salamander — overhead radiant broiler) mounted above, a Friteuse (deep fryer — double basin, 2x 10 liters), a Kombidämpfer (combi steamer — Rational or equivalent, digital display showing temperature, humidity, and time), and a Bain-Marie (das Wasserbad) for holding sauces at temperature. The cold section (die kalte Küche) sits across from the hot line: a long prep counter with refrigerated drawers (die Kühlschubladen), a Aufschnittmaschine (meat slicer), and a Salatschleuder (salad spinner). The Spülküche (dishwashing area) is in the back, dominated by a Haubenspülmaschine (hood-type commercial dishwasher) that hisses and steams every 90 seconds. Walk-in coolers: a Kühlhaus (cold room, +2°C) and a Tiefkühlhaus (freezer room, -18°C). The dry store (das Trockenlager) holds canned goods, flour, oil, and spices on labeled metal shelving. The floor is red quarry tile with drains, perpetually wet and slippery despite non-slip mats. A whiteboard near the pass shows tonight's Mise en place list and 86'd items.

Specific Smells:
An overwhelming, layered olfactory environment: caramelizing onions (sweet, deep), searing beef on the Bratplatte (smoky, rich Maillard reaction), garlic hitting hot oil (pungent, sharp, then aromatic), the clean herbaceous burst of freshly chopped parsley and dill, the yeasty warmth of bread proofing, the fatty fog from the Friteuse (heavy, clinging), the acidic bite of a Weißweinreduktion (white wine reduction) boiling down, bleach and commercial floor cleaner (the Spülküche end), and — pervading everything — the thick, humid, greasy air that settles into clothing and hair. In the Kühlhaus: cold, mineral, slightly metallic, with the metallic-blood scent of raw meat.

Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:
The kitchen during service is a controlled chaos of sound at 85–95 dB. Gas burners roar continuously. The Salamander clicks and ticks. The Haubenspülmaschine cycles with a hissing-whooshing-beeping rhythm. Metal pans crash on metal surfaces. The extraction hood creates a constant low-frequency drone. Over all of this: shouted commands from the Küchenchef (head chef) calling orders from the pass, the kitchen team responding with "Ja, Chef!" or "Oui, Chef!" (French kitchen tradition persists in German professional kitchens). The ticket printer clacks and tears. During the 19:00–21:00 peak (die Stoßzeit / der Rush), the noise and pace are overwhelming — the player must parse urgent German commands from the auditory wall.

The Meister / Boss Persona:
Name: Küchenchef Stefan Vogt
Age: 41
Personality: Chef Vogt is a lean, intense man with a shaved head, a neck tattoo of a chef's knife, and burn scars on both forearms that he wears as badges of honor. He trained at a Sterne-Restaurant in Munich, worked in Lyon and Copenhagen, and returned to Berlin to open his own place. He runs the kitchen with iron discipline during service but is generous and encouraging during prep, when the pace allows teaching. He demands mise en place perfection: "Mise en place ist alles. Wenn dein Posten nicht vorbereitet ist, bist du um acht Uhr tot." (Mise en place is everything. If your station isn't prepped, you're dead by eight o'clock.) He speaks clipped, rapid Hochdeutsch during service, peppered with French kitchen terminology (la brigade, passe, deglacieren) and occasional profanity when a dish comes back. Outside service, he becomes surprisingly calm and articulate — he sees cooking as Handwerk (craftsmanship) and enjoys explaining technique.
Speech Cadence: During service: staccato, imperative, rhythmic. Each table's order is called out as a chant. "Zwei Rücken, ein Fisch, drei Salat — los!" Outside service: measured, detailed, pedagogical.
Expectations: Cleanliness above all — HACCP rules are absolute. Knife skills must be fast and precise. The player must learn to call back orders ("Ja, Chef!"), maintain their Posten (station) in immaculate order, and never, ever send a dish to the pass without it being perfect. Tasting everything, constantly — "Abschmecken!" is his most frequent command. Coming to work hungover, with dirty whites, or with unsharpened knives earns a withering stare and a public correction.

2. Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology
20 Specific Tools / Instruments / Materials:

#	German (with article)	Plural	English

10 Domain-Specific Action Verbs:

2	flambieren (flambiert, flambierte, flambiert)	to flambé	Die Sauce mit Cognac flambieren

4	glacieren (glaciert, glacierte, glaciert)	to glaze (vegetables, meat)	Die Karotten mit Butter und Zucker glacieren

3. The Workplace Register & Jargon
5 Exact German Imperative Commands Shouted on the Job:

"Hinter dir!" — [ˈhɪntɐ diːɐ] — "Behind you!" (Called when moving behind another cook, especially carrying hot liquids or knives. Failure to call this out results in collisions and burns.)
"Heiß durch!" — [haɪs dʊʁç] — "Hot, coming through!" (Carrying a boiling pot through the kitchen. Everyone must clear the path.)
"Marsch! Tisch vier!" — [maʁʃ | tɪʃ fiːɐ] — "Fire! Table four!" (The call from the pass to begin cooking a table's order. "Marsch" is the German adaptation of the French "marche.")
"Abschmecken!" — [ˈapʃmɛkn̩] — "Taste it!" (Chef Vogt's most frequent command. Every sauce, every soup, every component must be tasted before it leaves the station.)
"Mise en place auffüllen — sofort!" — [miːz‿ɑ̃ plas ˈaʊffʏlən — zoˈfɔʁt] — "Restock your mise en place — now!" (When a station is running low on prepped ingredients during service.)
3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:

"Der Gast merkt alles — auch was du nicht siehst." — "The guest notices everything — even what you don't see." (Vogt's reminder that a fingerprint on a plate rim, an uneven sauce line, or a wilted herb will be noticed.)
"Wer seine Messer nicht pflegt, ist kein Koch." — "Whoever doesn't care for their knives is no cook." (A sharp knife is safer and faster. Dull knives are a sign of laziness and a safety hazard.)
"Zu viele Köche verderben den Brei." — "Too many cooks spoil the broth." (Classic German proverb, used when too many people interfere with a dish or a process.)
4. Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)
Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Kitchen Orientation, HACCP Briefing & Knife Skills Test

Setup: 09:00 Monday. The player arrives at the restaurant's back entrance (der Personaleingang). They change in the Umkleide into kitchen whites: die Kochjacke (double-breasted, white), die Kochhose (checkered pattern — Pepita), die Kochschürze (apron), die Sicherheitsschuhe (closed, non-slip kitchen shoes), and the Kochmütze or Bandana (head covering — hair containment). No jewelry, no watch, nails short and unpolished.

Beat 1 — The HACCP Tour:
Chef Vogt meets the player in the kitchen, which is empty and spotless — the morning calm before the storm.
"In dieser Küche gelten zwei Gesetze: erstens, das Lebensmittelhygienegesetz, und zweitens, mein Wort. Beides ist nicht verhandelbar."

He walks through the HACCP (Hazard Analysis and Critical Control Points) stations:

Wareneingang (Goods receiving): "Jede Lieferung wird kontrolliert. Temperatur messen. Fleisch: unter sieben Grad. Tiefkühlware: unter minus achtzehn. MHD prüfen — abgelaufene Ware geht zurück." (Every delivery is inspected. Temperature check. Meat: below 7°C. Frozen goods: below -18°C. Check best-before dates — expired goods are refused.)
Lagerung (Storage): "Kühlhaus: Fleisch unten, Milchprodukte Mitte, Gemüse oben. Niemals rohes Fleisch über verzehrfertige Ware. Kreuzkontamination ist der Feind."
Schneidebretter (Cutting boards): "Rot ist Fleisch. Grün ist Gemüse. Blau ist Fisch. Weiß ist Brot. Verwechselst du das, hast du ein Problem."
Händewaschen (Handwashing): The player must physically wash hands at the dedicated Handwaschbecken — with Seifenspender and Einweghandtücher. This is required after every break, every raw-meat contact, and every toilet visit. A HACCP poster on the wall shows the 10-step hand-washing procedure.
Beat 2 — Knife Skills Test (Schneidetechnik):
Vogt places an onion, a carrot, and a zucchini on the player's cutting board:
"Zeig mir: Brunoise, Julienne, Chiffonade. Und erzähl mir, was du schneidest."

Brunoise: 2mm x 2mm x 2mm fine dice (die Brunoise). Player must say: "Ich schneide die Karotte in Brunoise. Zwei Millimeter Würfel."
Julienne: 2mm x 2mm x 5cm fine strips (die Julienne). "Ich schneide die Zucchini in Julienne. Zwei Millimeter Streifen."
Chiffonade: Fine ribbon-cut of herbs (die Chiffonade). The player rolls basil leaves and slices thinly.
Vogt watches the player's knife grip (der Klingengriff — blade grip, NOT handle grip), cutting hand (die Krallenhand — claw grip for safety), and speed. Corrections are immediate: "Deine Führungshand! Finger einrollen! Sonst fehlt dir bald einer."

Beat 3 — First Prep Assignment:
Vogt assigns: "Mise en place für heute Abend. Dein Posten: Gardemanger — kalte Küche. Du machst die Salate. Ich brauche: zwanzig Portionen gemischter Blattsalat, gewaschen, geschleudert, portioniert. Vinaigrette: drei Teile Olivenöl, ein Teil Weißweinessig, Senf, Salz, Pfeffer, Honig. Drei Liter insgesamt. Dressing separat in einem Gastronorm-Behälter. Bis fünfzehn Uhr."

The player must:

Locate the Blattsalate (mixed greens) in the Kühlhaus.
Wash in the Salatwaschbecken (three-compartment sink — wash, rinse, sanitize).
Spin in the Salatschleuder.
Portion into 20 individual containers.
Prepare vinaigrette: measure on the Küchenwaage, whisk in a Rührschüssel, taste and adjust (abschmecken — balance of Säure/Süße/Salz — acid/sweet/salt).
Store properly: Frischhaltefolie (plastic wrap) over the GN-Behälter, label with date and contents (Etikett: "Vinaigrette, Datum: [Datum], MHD: +3 Tage"), place in the Kühlschublade.
Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — Running the Hot Station During Evening Service (der Abendservice)

Setup: Week 4. The player has been promoted from Gardemanger to Entremetier (vegetable and side dish station). It is 19:15, the restaurant is full (ausgebucht). The printer is spitting tickets.

Beat 1 — The Order Call:
Chef Vogt reads from the pass:
"Achtung, neuer Bon! Tisch sieben: zweimal Rinderrücken medium, einmal Lachs, dreimal Kartoffelpüree, zweimal Ofengemüse, einmal Risotto! Entremetier — das Püree und das Gemüse sind bei dir! Marsch!"
Player responds: "Ja, Chef! Drei Püree, zwei Ofengemüse, ein Risotto — verstanden!"

Beat 2 — Station Execution Under Pressure:
The player must simultaneously:

Kartoffelpüree (mashed potatoes): Already prepped — warming in the Bain-Marie. Scoop three portions into Gastronorm-Schälchen, finish with a knob of butter (ein Stich Butter), keep warm.
Ofengemüse (roasted vegetables): Pull two portions from the Kombidämpfer (set at 200°C, Heißluft — convection), check for caramelization, season with fleur de sel and fresh thyme.
Risotto: This is the challenging one. The player must ladle hot Gemüsebrühe (vegetable stock) into the simmering Risottoreis, stir continuously (rühren — must not stop or the bottom burns), add a splash of white wine, finish with Parmesan and butter (mantecatura technique — "mantecieren" in kitchen German), taste and call "Risotto fertig!" when the texture is al dente and creamy.
Beat 3 — The Pass & Quality Check:
All components must arrive at the pass simultaneously. Vogt inspects:
"Püree — gut. Gemüse — das Zucchini-Stück ist verbrannt, tausch es aus. Risotto — schmecken. Salz fehlt. Nochmal abschmecken. Nicht rausschicken, bevor es perfekt ist."
The player corrects: replaces the burned zucchini piece, adds salt to the risotto, tastes again, calls "Fertig, Chef!"
Vogt wipes the plate rims with a damp cloth (das Passiertuch), garnishes with microgreens (die Mikrokräuter), and calls: "Tisch sieben — raus!"

Beat 4 — Post-Service Cleanup & Shift Report:
Service ends at 22:30. The kitchen enters the Reinigungsphase (cleaning phase):

Scrub all work surfaces with Flächendesinfektionsmittel.
Disassemble and clean the Aufschnittmaschine, the Friteuse (filter and replace oil if dark), and the Kombidämpfer (run the automatic cleaning cycle).
Mop the floors.
Check and record Kühlhaus-Temperaturen on the HACCP-Protokoll (temperature log — recorded twice daily).
Restock mise en place list for tomorrow: "Was brauchen wir morgen? Drei Kilo Karotten, fünf Kilo Kartoffeln, zehn Lachsfilets, zwei Liter Sahne. Schreib die Bestellliste."
Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise — Grease Fire in the Deep Fryer (Fettbrand)

Setup: Day 35. The dinner rush. The player is working the hot station. The Friteuse, set at 175°C for Pommes frites, has been left unattended by the player for 90 seconds while they dealt with the Risotto. The oil temperature has spiked — the thermostat malfunctioned. Smoke begins to rise from the Friteuse. Suddenly, the oil ignites — a column of orange-yellow flame erupts from the basin with a deep WHOMP.

Beat 1 — Immediate Danger:
A Fettbrand (grease fire) is one of the most dangerous kitchen emergencies. The player must:

NEVER use water — water on a grease fire causes a Fettexplosion (boiling steam explosion that sprays burning oil in all directions). This is drilled into every cook. If the player reaches for the water tap, Chef Vogt screams: "KEIN WASSER! BIST DU WAHNSINNIG?! NIEMALS WASSER AUF FETT!"
Turn off the gas supply to the Friteuse (den Gashahn zudrehen).
If safe to do so: place the Löschdecke (fire blanket — mounted on the wall, red container) over the fryer to smother the flame by cutting off oxygen.
If the fire is too large for the blanket: use the Fettbrandlöscher (Class F fire extinguisher — wet chemical, specifically for cooking oil fires) — NOT a standard CO2 or powder extinguisher.
Beat 2 — Evacuation Call:
Chef Vogt takes command:
"Alle raus aus der Küche! Sofort! [Playername], Feuerlöscher — JETZT! Service ist gestoppt! Lena, sag dem Service Bescheid — Gäste ruhig halten!"
The player grabs the Fettbrandlöscher (Class F — yellow band label), pulls the pin (Sicherungsstift ziehen), aims at the base of the fire (auf den Brandherd zielen, nicht auf die Flammen), and applies (auslösen). The wet chemical agent smothers the fire and creates a saponification layer that prevents re-ignition.

Beat 3 — Aftermath:
The fire is out. The kitchen is filled with smoke and chemical residue. The extraction hood has pulled most smoke out, but the area around the fryer is coated in extinguishing agent. The Feuerwehr (fire department — called automatically by the building's fire alarm system or manually at 112) arrives to inspect and clear the scene. The player must explain:
"Es gab einen Fettbrand in der Friteuse. Der Thermostat war defekt, das Öl hat sich überhitzt und entzündet. Wir haben den Brand mit dem Fettbrandlöscher Klasse F gelöscht. Keine Verletzten."

Beat 4 — HACCP Incident Report & Lesson:
The player must complete an HACCP-Störungsbericht (incident report): date, time, equipment involved, cause (thermostat malfunction, unattended fryer), actions taken, corrective measures (thermostat replacement, fryer inspection by the Gerätetechniker). Vogt's debrief:
"Die Friteuse wird nie — ich wiederhole: NIE — unbeaufsichtigt betrieben. Du bist verantwortlich für deinen Posten. Wenn du weggehst, stellst du sie aus. Punkt. Und: Wasser auf Fett ist ein Todesurteil. Das muss jeder Koch im Schlaf wissen."

5. The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)
Exam Structure:
The Koch/Köchin completes a 3-year duale Ausbildung with the Abschlussprüfung administered by the Industrie- und Handelskammer (IHK). The exam consists of a practical cooking test (praktische Prüfung — preparing a 3-course menu from a given warenkorb/market basket within a time limit), a written exam (Technologie, Warenkunde, Betriebswirtschaft), and an oral Fachgespräch.

Specific Exam Vocabulary:

German Term	English	Context
die Abschlussprüfung (IHK)	final examination (IHK)	End-of-training certification exam
die Zwischenprüfung	intermediate exam	Midpoint assessment
der Warenkorb	market basket	Practical exam: basket of ingredients from which the menu must be composed
die Speisenkarte (Menüfolge)	menu (course sequence)	Planning a menu: Vorspeise, Hauptgang, Dessert
die Warenkunde	food science / commodity knowledge	Written exam: identifying cuts of meat, fish species, vegetable varieties
die Garmethoden	cooking methods	Exam topic: braten, schmoren, dünsten, pochieren, gratinieren, blanchieren
die Nährwertberechnung	nutritional value calculation	Written exam component
die Kalkulation (Wareneinsatz)	food cost calculation	Written exam: calculating cost per portion
die Hygienevorschriften (HACCP / IfSG)	hygiene regulations (HACCP / Infection Protection Act)	Critical exam area
die Arbeitssicherheit in der Küche	workplace safety in the kitchen	Fire prevention, knife safety, slip/fall prevention
das Fachgespräch (über das praktische Menü)	technical discussion (about the practical menu)	Oral defense: explaining choices, techniques, and timing
die Menüplanung (saisonal, regional)	seasonal/regional menu planning	Written/oral exam: composing menus using seasonal ingredients
der Arbeitsablaufplan (für die praktische Prüfung)	workflow plan (for the practical exam)	Must be submitted before cooking — a timeline of all tasks
die Sensorik (Aussehen, Geruch, Geschmack, Textur)	sensory evaluation (appearance, aroma, taste, texture)	How examiners evaluate the finished dishes
die Ausbildungsordnung Koch/Köchin (2020)	training regulation for cooks (2020 revision)	Updated curriculum with sustainability and digitalization
Visa Stabilization Link:
The IHK Abschlussprüfung yields a Facharbeiterbrief, enabling the §18a AufenthG Fachkräftevisum. Cooks are designated as a Mangelberuf (shortage occupation) in Germany, giving the player an in-game advantage in visa processing speed. Additionally, the Koch/Köchin path opens an in-game side quest for the Küchenmeister/in title (advanced master chef qualification).

PROFESSION 7: FRISEUR/IN & BARBER (Hairdresser / Men's Barber)
1. The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint
Visual Landscape:
The player works at "Salon Schönschnitt," a modern-traditional Friseursalon on Sonnenallee with a separate Barber-Bereich (barber section) in the back. The salon has six styling stations (die Bedienplätze) along the left wall, each with a large mirror (der Friseurspiegel) bordered by warm LED lighting, a hydraulic styling chair (der Friseurstuhl) with chrome base and black leather, and a wheeled Friseurwagen (styling trolley) holding tools. The floor is polished dark wood, scattered with hair clippings despite the apprentice's regular sweeping (Haare zusammenfegen is the apprentice's constant task). A Waschbereich (wash area) with three reclining Rückwärts-Waschbecken (backwash basins) in matte black sits at the rear. A display shelf of Schwarzkopf and L'Oréal Professionnel products (Shampoos, Kuren, Stylingprodukte) lines the reception counter. The Barber-Bereich has two vintage-style Barbierstühle (barber chairs) with headrests, a hot-towel steamer (der Handtuchwärmer / der Kompressenwärmer), a leather strop (der Abziehlederriemen) mounted on the wall, and a shelf of Bartpflegeprodukte (beard care products — oils, balms, waxes). The atmosphere is urban-chic with exposed brick, pendant Edison bulbs, and a Spotify playlist of nu-jazz and lo-fi beats at comfortable volume.

Specific Smells:
Hair products dominate: the sweet, chemical-floral scent of Haarfärbemittel (hair color — ammonia-based oxidative color has a sharp, eye-watering ammonia bite during mixing; ammonia-free alternatives are milder, almost fruity), the clean botanical fragrance of professional shampoos (Argan oil, tea tree, keratin), the warm vanilla-caramel note of Haarspray fixation, the mentholated coolness of aftershave balm in the Barber section, the subtle sulfuric scent of Dauerwellenlösung (perming solution), and the hot-metal-and-keratin smell of a Glätteisen (flat iron) or Lockenstab (curling iron) pressing through damp hair — a slightly burnt-protein scent.

Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:
A comfortable 60–70 dB. Background music, the hum of conversation between stylists and customers (a crucial language environment — Smalltalk is a professional skill), the buzz of Haarschneidemaschinen (hair clippers) in the barber section, the click-click of scissors, the whoosh of Föhn (blow-dryer — up to 80 dB when in use near the ear). The Waschbereich has the soothing sound of running water. The phone rings intermittently — Terminvergabe (appointment scheduling) is a frequent language task.

The Meister / Boss Persona:
Name: Salonleiterin / Friseurmeisterin Nadine Hartmann
Age: 38
Personality: Frau Hartmann is a stylish, sharp-witted woman with an asymmetric platinum-blonde bob (her walking advertisement), perfectly manicured nails (though she wears gloves for chemical work), and a professional smile that can shift to a pointed correction in an instant. She completed her Friseurmeisterin at 26 and opened the salon at 30. She runs a tight ship but with warmth — clients are loyal because of her personal touch. She insists that the Friseurberuf is both Handwerk and Dienstleistung (craft and service): technical skill without customer rapport is worthless, and charm without technique is fraud. She speaks bright, clear Hochdeutsch with frequent use of du with colleagues and Sie with clients, and expects the player to observe this distinction absolutely. She is passionate about Farbberatung (color consultation) and considers a botched color job a personal affront.
Speech Cadence: Melodic, warm with clients ("Na, Frau Becker, was darf es heute sein?"). Direct and clipped with the team during busy periods ("Platz drei ist frei — nächster Kunde!"). Gives feedback as questions: "Findest du, dass die Übergänge sauber sind?" — forcing the player to self-evaluate.
Expectations: Flawless hygiene — fresh Umhang (cape) for every client, tools desinfiziert between clients, hair swept immediately. The player must master Kundenberatung (client consultation): listening, suggesting, and managing expectations in German. Timekeeping: a Damen-Haarschnitt (women's haircut) should take 30–40 minutes; going over time disrupts the entire Terminkalender (appointment calendar). The first months are heavy on Waschen, Föhnen, Kehren (washing, blow-drying, sweeping) — earning trust before touching scissors.

3. The Workplace Register & Jargon
5 Exact German Imperative Commands on the Job:

"Platz drei ist frei — nächster Kunde!" — [plats dʁaɪ ɪst fʁaɪ — ˈnɛːçstɐ ˈkʊndə] — "Chair three is open — next client!" (Called to the reception area to move the queue along.)
"Einwirkzeit ist um — auswaschen!" — [ˈaɪnvɪʁktsaɪt ɪst ʊm — ˈaʊsvaʃn̩] — "Processing time is up — wash it out!" (For hair color or perm — critical timing; over-processing causes damage.)
"Strähnchen kontrollieren — ist die Aufhellung schon durch?" — [ˈʃtʁɛːnçən kɔntʁoˈliːʁən — ɪst diː ˈaʊfhɛlʊŋ ʃoːn dʊʁç] — "Check the highlights — has the lightening process finished?" (Instruction to check foils for desired lift level.)
"Kehr bitte sofort den Platz!" — [keːɐ ˈbɪtə zoˈfɔʁt deːn plats] — "Sweep the station right now, please!" (To the apprentice after every haircut — the floor must be clear before the next client sits down.)
"Handschuhe anziehen — du arbeitest mit Farbe!" — [ˈhantʃuːə ˈantsiːən — duː ˈaʁbaɪtəst mɪt ˈfaʁbə] — "Put on gloves — you're working with color!" (Chemical contact protection — mandatory for all color and perm work.)
3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:

"Ein guter Schnitt verkauft sich selbst." — "A good cut sells itself." (Meaning: when the client loves the result, they rebook and recommend — no advertising needed.)
"Weniger ist mehr — lieber nochmal nachschneiden." — "Less is more — better to cut again." (Never cut too much on the first pass. Hair can be cut shorter but not glued back on. A fundamental cautionary principle.)
"Die Beratung ist die halbe Miete." — "The consultation is half the battle." (If the consultation is thorough and honest, the client's expectations are managed, and complaints are rare.)
4. Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)
Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Salon Orientation, Hygiene Routine & First Wash

Setup: 08:30 Tuesday. The salon opens at 09:00. The player arrives and is greeted by Friseurmeisterin Hartmann.

Beat 1 — The Tour & Hygiene Protocol:
Hartmann walks the player through:

Reception & Terminkalender (appointment book — digital, on a tablet): "Hier siehst du den Tagesplan. Jeder Kunde ist mit Uhrzeit, Name, und gewünschter Dienstleistung eingetragen. Damen-Haarschnitt, Herren-Schnitt, Färben, Strähnchen, Dauerwelle, Bartpflege. Deine Aufgabe: jeden Kunden begrüßen, Mantel abnehmen, Getränk anbieten."
Hygiene-Station: "Nach jedem Kunden: Schere, Kamm, und alle benutzten Werkzeuge in den Desinfektionsbehälter (Barbizid-Lösung). Frischer Umhang. Frisches Nackenpapier. Stuhl abwischen. Haare fegen. Erst dann darf der nächste Kunde Platz nehmen."
Farbbereich (color mixing station): "Farbe wird erst angerührt, wenn die Kundin sitzt. Nie auf Vorrat. Handschuhe immer an. Farbkarte (Farbring) bereithalten für die Beratung."
Beat 2 — The First Wash (Haarwäsche):
Hartmann demonstrates at the Rückwärtswaschbecken:
"Du testest die Wassertemperatur an deinem eigenen Handgelenk — nicht am Kopf der Kundin. Temperatur: angenehm warm, nie heiß. Du fragst: 'Ist die Temperatur angenehm für Sie?' Zweimal shampoonieren. Beim ersten Mal: Produkt verteilen, leicht einmassieren, ausspülen. Beim zweiten Mal: gründlich einmassieren — Kopfhaut, Haaransatz, Spitzen. Dann Pflegespülung — in die Längen und Spitzen, nicht auf die Kopfhaut. Zwei Minuten einwirken lassen. Ausspülen. Handtuch um den Kopf. Zurück zum Platz."

The player practices on a training head (der Übungskopf) first, then on a real customer (Frau Becker, a friendly regular).

Language demands:

"Guten Tag, Frau Becker. Kommen Sie bitte zum Waschbecken."
"Ist die Temperatur angenehm?"
"Ich trage jetzt das Shampoo auf."
"Möchten Sie eine Pflegekur oder die normale Spülung?"
"So, bitte — hier ist Ihr Platz."
Beat 3 — The Broom (das Kehren):
Between clients, the player's primary task is keeping the salon immaculate. Hartmann hands the player a Handfeger und Kehrblech (hand brush and dustpan): "Nach jedem Schnitt: fegen. Nicht warten, bis ich es sage." Hair on the floor is visually rendered in the game — missing it triggers Hartmann's pointed look and eventual verbal correction.

Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — Damen-Haarschnitt mit Färben (Women's Cut & Color)

Setup: Week 8. The player now performs supervised cuts and has begun color services. Today's client: Frau Yılmaz, 34, wants a Ansatzfärbung (root touch-up — natural hair color: dunkelbraun (dark brown), wants her roots touched up and a trim.

**Beat 1 — The Consultation (Kundenberatung):**
The player sits Frau Yılmaz at the styling station, drapes the Umhang, and conducts the consultation in German:
- "Frau Yılmaz, was darf es heute sein?"
- "Ich möchte den Ansatz nachfärben — meine Naturfarbe ist dunkelbraun, und die Blondierung ist herausgewachsen."
- The player must examine the hair, identify the regrowth line (der Ansatz), and select the correct color from the Farbkarte. The player mixes the color at the Farbbereich: "Ich nehme die 6/7 Dunkelblond mit 6% Oxidationsmittel im Verhältnis 1:1."
- Hartmann checks: "Hast du den Ansatz genau abgeteilt? Keine Überlappung auf die bereits blondierten Längen — das gibt Haarbruch!"

**Beat 2 — Application & Processing:**
The player applies the color with the Färbepinsel, section by section (Abteilung für Abteilung), starting at the nape (der Nacken) and working upward. Timer set for 35 minutes (die Einwirkzeit). During processing, the player offers Frau Yılmaz a coffee ("Möchten Sie einen Kaffee oder Tee?") and makes Smalltalk — a graded language task where the player must respond to questions about their life in Neukölln, the weather, weekend plans.

**Beat 3 — Rinse, Cut & Finish:**
After rinsing the color at the Waschbecken, the player performs a Damen-Haarschnitt: trimming the split ends (die Spliss-Enden), checking the layers (die Stufen) with the Stielkamm, and blow-drying with the Rundbürste for a smooth finish. Hartmann inspects: "Der Übergang am Oberkopf ist etwas kantig — nimm die Effilierschere und weich die Linie auf." The player corrects it. Final check in the mirror with the client: "Sind Sie zufrieden, Frau Yılmaz?"

**Language demands:**
- "Ich teile jetzt das Haar in vier Abteilungen."
- "Die Einwirkzeit beträgt fünfunddreißig Minuten."
- "Ich spüle die Farbe jetzt aus — bitte den Kopf leicht nach hinten."
- "Die Spitzen sind etwas trocken — ich empfehle eine Olaplex-Kur beim nächsten Termin."

**Scenario C: Der Notfall — Die verbrannte Kopfhaut (Chemical Burn from Bleach)**

**Setup:** Week 14. The player is performing a full Blondierung (full bleach) on a young client, Lena, 22, who wants platinum blonde from medium brown. The player used 12% Oxidationsmittel (40-volume developer) — too strong for an on-scalp application.

**Beat 1 — The Crisis Onset:**
Twenty minutes into processing, Lena suddenly flinches and grabs the armrest: "Aua! Das brennt extrem! Mein Kopf fühlt sich an wie Feuer!" The player sees redness along the parting lines (die Scheitel) and behind the ears. The sensory cue: the sharp ammonia smell intensifies, and Lena's eyes are watering.

**Beat 2 — Immediate Response:**
Hartmann rushes over: "Sofort auswaschen! Nicht warten! Ab zum Waschbecken — lauwarmes Wasser, kein Shampoo, nur spülen! Schnell!" The player must:
1. Physically guide Lena to the Waschbecken within 5 seconds (game timer).
2. Rinse thoroughly with cool-to-lukewarm water for a full 3 minutes.
3. Apply a soothing scalp treatment (die Kopfhautberuhigung — aloe-based).
4. Verbally manage the panicked client in German: "Es tut mir sehr leid, Frau Lena. Ich spüle jetzt alles sofort aus. Das Brennen wird gleich nachlassen. Bitte atmen Sie tief."

**Beat 3 — The Aftermath & Lesson:**
Hartmann takes over, assesses the damage (mild erythema, no blistering — Glück im Unglück). She speaks to the player in the back room, voice low but furious: "Zwölf Prozent auf die Kopfhaut? Bist du wahnsinnig? Zwölf Prozent ist nur für Foliensträhnen, nie auf die Haut! Du hast Glück, dass es keine Verbrennung zweiten Grades ist. Ab sofort: maximal sechs Prozent bei Ansatzfärbung. Verstanden?" The player must respond with a formal apology to the client and offer a complimentary treatment. The game logs the error in the player's vocational competency tracker — chemical safety is now flagged for re-examination.

**Language demands (crisis register — rapid, stressed):**
- "Sofort ausspülen!" (Rinse immediately!)
- "Brennt es noch?" (Is it still burning?)
- "Ich hole meine Chefin." (I'm getting my boss.)
- "Wir übernehmen selbstverständlich die Kosten für die Nachbehandlung." (We will of course cover the cost of follow-up treatment.)

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

The player must pass the **Gesellenprüfung Friseur/in** (Journeyman Examination) administered by the **Handwerkskammer (HWK) Berlin**. The exam consists of:

- **Teil 1 (Praktische Prüfung — 6 hours):** The player performs a complete Damen- und Herrenservice on a live model: consultation, cutting (Damen-Haarschnitt with Stufenschnitt, Herren-Schnitt with Maschinenübergang), coloring (Strähnentechnik or Ansatzfärbung), and styling (Föhnfrisur). The Prüfungsausschuss (exam board) evaluates precision, hygiene, timing, and client communication in German.
- **Teil 2 (Theoretische Prüfung — written, 4 hours):** Fachtheorie (hair structure, color chemistry, dermatology of the scalp), Fachrechnen (mixing ratios, pricing, VAT calculation), Wirtschafts- und Sozialkunde (labor law, salon management, UVV hygiene regulations).
- **Key Exam Vocabulary:** der Haarfollikel (hair follicle), die Haarstruktur (hair structure), der Haarschaft (hair shaft), die Melaninproduktion (melanin production), die Wasserstoffperoxid-Konzentration (hydrogen peroxide concentration), die pH-Wert-Regulierung (pH regulation), die Kontaktdermatitis (contact dermatitis), die Desinfektionsverordnung (disinfection ordinance), die Preisgestaltung (pricing), die Umsatzsteuer (VAT).
- **Visa Impact:** Passing the Gesellenprüfung grants the player a recognized German vocational qualification (anerkannter Berufsabschluss), which unlocks the pathway to a permanent residence permit (Niederlassungserlaubnis) and the option to pursue the Friseurmeister/in title for salon ownership.

---

### PROFESSION 8: HOTELFACHMANN / HOTELFACHFRAU (Hotel Operations & Receptionist)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works at "Hotel Neukölln Mitte," a boutique 4-star hotel on Weserstraße with 68 rooms across five floors. The lobby features polished concrete floors, a curved reception desk (die Rezeption) in brushed brass and dark walnut, a key-card encoding station (der Kartencodierer), two computer monitors running the Opera PMS (Property Management System), a telephone switchboard (die Telefonanlage) with blinking lines, and a concierge bell (die Rezeptionsglocke). Behind the desk: a wall of numbered room key slots (die Schlüsselfächer), a safe (der Hotelsafe) for guest valuables, and a printer for registration forms (der Meldeschein). The breakfast area (der Frühstücksraum) on the ground floor has buffet stations with chafing dishes, a commercial espresso machine (der Vollautomat), and linen-draped tables. The housekeeping corridor on each floor shows linen carts (der Etagenwagen) loaded with white towels, bed linens, and miniature toiletries.

**Specific Smells:**
The lobby smells of a signature hotel fragrance — white tea and bergamot diffused through the HVAC system. The breakfast area carries the warm aroma of fresh Brötchen (bread rolls), brewing coffee, and scrambled eggs. The housekeeping floors smell of industrial cleaning agents (der Allzweckreiniger — citrus-scented), fresh laundry (der Weichspüler — softener), and occasionally the faint musty odor of a room that hasn't been aired out (das Lüften vergessen). The back-of-house staff corridor smells of floor wax and staff canteen food.

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
Lobby: 50–60 dB — soft background jazz, the clack of keyboard typing, the murmur of guest conversations in multiple languages, the ding of the elevator (der Aufzug). Breakfast room at peak: 75 dB — clinking cutlery, coffee machine grinding, overlapping conversations. Housekeeping corridors: quieter, 40–50 dB, punctuated by the rumble of linen carts and the beep of electronic door locks. The phone at reception rings constantly during check-in hours (14:00–18:00) — a persistent, demanding trill.

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**Name:** Hoteldirektor Thomas Krüger
**Age:** 52
**Personality:** Herr Krüger is a tall, impeccably groomed man with silver hair combed back, wearing a tailored navy suit and a subtle lapel pin of the hotel's logo. He has 25 years in the hospitality industry, having worked his way up from Nachtportier (night porter) to Hoteldirektor. He is the embodiment of German hospitality precision: every detail matters, from the fold of the napkin to the exact phrasing of a welcome greeting. He is courteous but exacting — he will smile while correcting a subordinate's posture or grammar. He speaks formal Hochdeutsch with guests and a slightly more relaxed but still professional register with staff. He expects the player to master the art of anticipating guest needs before they are voiced.
**Speech Cadence:** Measured, calm, authoritative. With guests: "Herzlich willkommen im Hotel Neukölln Mitte. Darf ich Ihnen behilflich sein?" With staff: "Die Suite 412 muss bis vierzehn Uhr bezugsfertig sein — keine Ausnahmen."
**Expectations:** The player must maintain a professional appearance (gepflegtes Äußeres — no visible tattoos, hair tied back, name badge straight). Multitasking is non-negotiable: answering the phone while checking in a guest while resolving a complaint. The player must learn the Opera PMS system, handle cash and card transactions, and communicate fluently in German and at least basic English. Complaints must be handled with the LEARN method (Listen, Empathize, Apologize, React, Notify) — entirely in German.

2. **Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology**

**20 Specific Tools / Instruments / Materials:**

| 10 | abrechnen (rechnet ab, rechnete ab, abgerechnet) | to settle the bill | Die Rechnung beim Check-out abrechnen |

3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Exact German Imperative Commands on the Job:**

1. **"Zimmer 305 sofort fertig machen — Early-Check-in!"** — [ˈtsɪmɐ dʁaɪ hʊndɐt fʏnf zoˈfɔʁt ˈfɛʁtɪç ˈmaxən — ˈɛːɐli ˈtʃɛk ɪn] — "Get room 305 ready immediately — early check-in!" (Urgent housekeeping order when a guest arrives before standard 15:00 check-in.)
2. **"Telefon — Leitung zwei, Herr Müller aus Suite 412!"** — [teleˈfoːn — ˈlaɪtʊŋ tsvaɪ — hɛʁ ˈmʏlɐ aʊs sviːt fɪɐ tsvœlf] — "Phone — line two, Mr. Müller from Suite 412!" (Reception alert to answer a guest call.)
3. **"Meldeschein nicht vergessen — Ausweis kopieren!"** — [ˈmɛldəʃaɪn nɪçt fɛɐˈɡɛsn̩ — ˈaʊsvaɪs koˈpiːʁən] — "Don't forget the registration form — copy the ID!" (Legal requirement under German Meldegesetz: every guest must register with ID.)
4. **"Die Rechnung stimmt nicht — Korrektur sofort!"** — [diː ˈʁɛçnʊŋ ʃtɪmt nɪçt — kɔʁɛkˈtuːɐ zoˈfɔʁt] — "The bill is wrong — correct it immediately!" (Billing error during check-out — guest is waiting.)
5. **"Schichtübergabe in zehn Minuten — Protokoll schreiben!"** — [ˈʃɪçtʔyːbɐɡaːbə ɪn tseːn miˈnuːtn̩ — pʁotoˈkɔl ˈʃʁaɪbn̩] — "Shift handover in ten minutes — write the report!" (End-of-shift documentation.)

**3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:**

1. **"Der Gast ist König."** — "The guest is king." (The foundational hospitality principle. Even when a guest is unreasonable, the staff must maintain composure and find a solution.)
2. **"Lächeln kostet nichts, bringt aber alles."** — "Smiling costs nothing but brings everything." (A reminder from the Hoteldirektor during morning briefings — attitude is as important as competence.)
3. **"Kein Zimmer ist sauber, bis die Hausdame es abgenommen hat."** — "No room is clean until the head housekeeper has inspected it." (Quality control standard — the player's work is always subject to a second check.)

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Orientation, PMS Training & First Check-In**

**Setup:** 07:00 Monday. The player arrives for the Frühschicht (early shift) at Hotel Neukölln Mitte. Herr Krüger meets them in the lobby.

**Beat 1 — The Tour & Systems Introduction:**
Herr Krüger walks the player through the hotel:
- "Das ist die Rezeption — Ihr Arbeitsplatz. Links das PMS-System, rechts die Telefonanlage. Dahinter die Schlüsselfächer. Der Tresor für Wertsachen ist dort — den Code bekommen Sie erst nach der Probezeit."
- Housekeeping floors: "Jedes Zimmer hat einen Status im System: belegt (occupied), abgereist (checked out), in Reinigung (being cleaned), sauber (clean), inspiziert (inspected and ready). Sie dürfen keinen Gast in ein Zimmer einchecken, das nicht den Status 'inspiziert' hat."
- Breakfast area: "Frühstückszeit: sechs Uhr dreißig bis zehn Uhr. Buffet auffüllen, Kaffeemaschine warten, Tische abräumen. Die Gäste erwarten frische Brötchen um sieben — nicht um halb acht."

**Beat 2 — PMS Training (Opera System):**
The player sits at the reception computer. Herr Krüger demonstrates:
- "Neue Buchung anlegen: Datum, Zimmertyp, Gastname, Zahlungsart. Kreditkarte vorautorisieren — das ist Pflicht. Meldeschein ausdrucken, Gast unterschreiben lassen, Ausweis scannen. Das ist Gesetz — das Bundesmeldegesetz. Keine Ausnahme."
- The player practices creating a dummy reservation, checking in a virtual guest, and processing a check-out with invoice generation.

**Beat 3 — First Live Check-In:**
A real guest arrives: Herr Schulz, a business traveler from München. The player must:
- Greet: "Guten Tag, Herr Schulz. Herzlich willkommen im Hotel Neukölln Mitte. Haben Sie eine Reservierung?"
- Process: Find the booking, verify ID, scan the Personalausweis, print the Meldeschein, encode the Zimmerkarte, explain breakfast times and Wi-Fi password.
- Close: "Ihr Zimmer ist die 308, dritter Stock, Aufzug links. Frühstück von halb sieben bis zehn im Erdgeschoss. Wenn Sie etwas brauchen, wählen Sie die Null an der Rezeption. Einen angenehmen Aufenthalt!"

**Language demands:**
- "Darf ich Ihren Ausweis sehen?"
- "Möchten Sie mit Karte oder bar bezahlen?"
- "Die Kreditkarte wird vorautorisiert — der Betrag wird erst beim Check-out abgebucht."
- "Hier ist Ihre Zimmerkarte. Der Aufzug befindet sich links."

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — Die Beschwerdebehandlung (Handling a Guest Complaint)**

**Setup:** Week 10. The player is working the Spätschicht (late shift, 14:00–22:00). At 19:30, a guest, Frau Hoffmann from Zimmer 415, storms to the reception, visibly angry.

**Beat 1 — The Complaint:**
Frau Hoffmann slams her key card on the counter: "Das ist eine Zumutung! Die Klimaanlage in meinem Zimmer funktioniert nicht, es ist fünfundzwanzig Grad, ich kann nicht schlafen! Und das Badezimmer stinkt nach Abfluss! Ich verlange sofort ein anderes Zimmer!"

**Beat 2 — The LEARN Protocol (in German):**
The player must execute the complaint-handling sequence:
- **L**isten (Listen): "Frau Hoffmann, ich verstehe Ihren Ärger. Bitte erzählen Sie mir genau, was passiert ist." (Maintain eye contact, nod, do not interrupt.)
- **E**mpathize: "Das tut mir wirklich leid. Bei dieser Hitze ist eine defekte Klimaanlage absolut inakzeptabel."
- **A**pologize: "Ich entschuldige mich aufrichtig für die Unannehmlichkeiten."
- **R**eact: "Ich schaue sofort, ob wir ein gleichwertiges Zimmer frei haben. Einen Moment, bitte." The player checks the PMS — Zimmer 418 (same category, Klimaanlage functional) is available. "Ich kann Ihnen sofort Zimmer 418 anbieten, gleiche Kategorie, klimatisiert. Ich lasse Ihr Gepäck umziehen."
- **N**otify: The player logs the complaint in the Beschwerdeliste, notifies the Haustechnik (maintenance) to repair Zimmer 415's AC, and informs Herr Krüger via the Übergabeprotokoll.

**Beat 3 — Resolution & Follow-Up:**
The player personally escorts Frau Hoffmann to Zimmer 418, ensures the AC is working, and offers a complimentary drink voucher for the hotel bar: "Als kleine Wiedergutmachung laden wir Sie auf ein Getränk an der Bar ein. Nochmals Entschuldigung, Frau Hoffmann." The guest calms down. The game rewards the player with +15 Kompetenzpunkte in "Kundenkommunikation."

**Language demands:**
- "Ich kümmere mich sofort darum."
- "Darf ich Ihnen ein Upgrade in ein Superior-Zimmer anbieten?"
- "Der Techniker wird das Problem morgen früh beheben."
- "Wir nehmen Ihre Beschwerde sehr ernst."

**Scenario C: Der Notfall — Der Feueralarm nachts (Night Fire Alarm)**

**Setup:** Week 18. The player is working the Nachtschicht (night shift, 22:00–06:00) alone at reception. At 02:47, the Brandmeldeanlage (fire alarm system) triggers — a piercing, continuous alarm (95 dB) echoes through all floors. The PMS shows 42 occupied rooms with approximately 60 guests.

**Beat 1 — The Alarm:**
The fire panel (die Brandmeldetafel) behind reception flashes red: "Melder Gruppe 3, Etage 2, Zimmer 210." The player must immediately:
1. Call the Feuerwehr (112) and report: "Hotel Neukölln Mitte, Weserstraße 47, Feueralarm im zweiten Obergeschoss, Zimmer 210. Circa sechzig Gäste im Haus."
2. Activate the Evakuierungsplan (evacuation plan) — press the Evakuierungstaste on the alarm panel.
3. Grab the Notfallliste (emergency guest list) and the master key card (die Generalkarte).

**Beat 2 — Guest Evacuation:**
The player runs to the stairwell, shouting in German and English: "Feueralarm! Bitte verlassen Sie das Gebäude sofort über die Treppe! Nicht den Aufzug benutzen! Sammelpunkt ist draußen vor dem Hoteleingang!" The player must knock on doors on the second floor, checking rooms: "Feueralarm! Bitte sofort rauskommen!" The sensory environment is chaotic — strobe lights flashing, alarm deafening, guests in pajamas stumbling into the corridor, a baby crying. The player identifies the source: Zimmer 210 — a guest fell asleep with a lit cigarette, triggering the smoke detector. The guest is disoriented but unharmed. The player guides them out.

**Beat 3 — All-Clear & Documentation:**
The Feuerwehr arrives within 6 minutes, confirms no actual fire (false alarm from smoke), and ventilates the floor. The player must account for all 60 guests at the Sammelpunkt (assembly point) using the Notfallliste. Herr Krüger arrives at 03:30, takes over, and the player writes the detailed Vorfallbericht (incident report): time of alarm, actions taken, Feuerwehr arrival time, guest count, damage assessment. The game evaluates the player's speed, accuracy of the 112 call, and completeness of the evacuation.

**Language demands (crisis register):**
- "Feueralarm! Alle Gäste sofort nach draußen!"
- "Benutzen Sie nicht den Aufzug — nur die Treppe!"
- "Bleiben Sie ruhig und folgen Sie mir!"
- "Die Feuerwehr ist unterwegs — bitte warten Sie am Sammelpunkt."

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

The player must pass the **IHK-Abschlussprüfung Hotelfachmann/-frau** administered by the **Industrie- und Handelskammer (IHK) Berlin**.

- **Teil 1 (Praktische Prüfung — 4 hours):** The player performs a complete hotel service cycle: checking in a guest with special requests (allergies, late check-out, room upgrade), handling a complaint scenario, setting up a conference room (Tagungsraum), and conducting a check-out with invoice correction. The examiners role-play as difficult guests.
- **Teil 2 (Schriftliche Prüfung — 3 subjects, 5 hours total):**
  - **Hotelorganisation & Rezeption:** Room categories, PMS operations, Meldegesetz, Datenschutz (GDPR), Stornobedingungen.
  - **Wirtschafts- und Sozialkunde:** Arbeitsrecht (Arbeitszeitgesetz, Mindestlohn), Tarifverträge (DEHOGA), Umsatzsteuer, Kalkulation.
  - **Warenwirtschaft & Gastronomie:** Food safety (HACCP), beverage service, buffet planning, inventory management.
- **Key Exam Vocabulary:** die Beherbergungsvertrag (accommodation contract), die Stornogebühr (cancellation fee), die Kurtaxe (tourist tax), der Belegungsplan (occupancy plan), die Auslastung (occupancy rate), der RevPAR (Revenue per Available Room), die Nachtwache (night watch), das Brandschutzkonzept (fire safety concept), die Verkehrssicherungspflicht (duty of care), das Beschwerdemanagement (complaint management).
- **Visa Impact:** The IHK-Abschluss is a nationally recognized qualification (anerkannter Berufsabschluss nach BBiG). Passing it grants the player eligibility for a Fachkräftevisum (skilled worker visa) under §18a AufenthG and opens the path to Hotelbetriebswirt (hotel management degree).

---

### PROFESSION 9: FACHKRAFT FÜR LAGERLOGISTIK (Warehouse Logistics & Forklift Specialist)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works at "Logistikzentrum Berlin-Süd," a massive 12,000 m² distribution warehouse in the industrial zone near Neukölln's southern edge (close to the A100 Autobahn). The interior is cavernous: 10-meter-high Hochregale (high-bay racking systems) stretch in parallel rows labeled A through H, each shelf stacked with Euro-Paletten (EUR-pallets) wrapped in black stretch foil. Yellow safety lines (die Bodenmarkierungen) mark pedestrian walkways and forklift lanes. Fluorescent LED panels cast harsh, shadowless white light across the concrete floor, which is scarred with tire marks and scuffs. The Warenausgang (shipping dock) has four Laderampen (loading docks) with roller shutters, where LKWs (trucks) back in for loading. The Wareneingang (receiving dock) has a scanning station with barcode readers, a digital scale (die Industriewaage), and a quality inspection table. The Leitstand (control room) is a glass-walled office elevated above the floor, where the Schichtleiter monitors the Lagerverwaltungssystem (WMS — Warehouse Management System) on multiple screens. Forklifts (die Gabelstapler) zip between aisles with beeping reverse alarms.

**Specific Smells:**
Cardboard dust (trocken, papieren), diesel exhaust from the LKWs at the loading docks, the sharp chemical tang of shrink-wrap plastic being heated, the rubbery smell of forklift tires on concrete, the faint metallic odor of steel racking, and in the refrigerated section (das Kühllager) a cold, sterile smell of refrigerant and condensation.

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
80–90 dB — a constant industrial roar. The whine of electric forklift motors, the beep-beep-beep of reversing alarms, the hydraulic hiss of pallet jacks lifting loads, the crash of pallets being set down, the crackle of stretch wrap being torn from rolls, the rumble of LKW engines at the docks, and the PA system announcements: "Achtung, Staplerverkehr in Gang C!" Hearing protection (der Gehörschutz) is mandatory in certain zones.

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**Name:** Schichtleiter Markus Weber
**Age:** 45
**Personality:** Herr Weber is a stocky, broad-shouldered man with a shaved head, a thick mustache, and forearms covered in faded tattoos. He has worked in logistics for 22 years, starting as a Kommissionierer (order picker) and rising to Schichtleiter. He is blunt, loud, and impatient with laziness, but fair and protective of his team. He speaks in a rough Berliner Schnauze (Berlin dialect) mixed with logistics jargon: "Mach ma' hinne, die Tour muss raus!" He has zero tolerance for safety violations — one wrong move with a forklift and someone dies. He expects the player to earn their Gabelstaplerschein (forklift license) within the first three months.
**Speech Cadence:** Rapid, clipped, loud enough to be heard over the warehouse noise. Sentences are short and imperative. Uses "du" with everyone. Rarely says "please."
**Expectations:** Speed and accuracy in Kommissionierung (order picking) — the player must hit a pick rate of 120 Positionen per hour. Zero tolerance for damaged goods (Beschädigung). Safety first: Stahlkappenschuhe (steel-toe boots), Warnweste (high-vis vest), and Helm (hard hat) at all times. The player must learn the WMS scanner system and master forklift operation.

2. **Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology**

**20 Specific Tools / Instruments / Materials:**
