3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Exact German Imperative Commands on the Job:**

1. **"Tour 47 muss bis vierzehn Uhr raus sein — Beeilung!"** — [tuːɐ zɪbn̩ ʊnt fɪʁtsɪç mʊs bɪs fɪɐˈtseːn uːɐ ʁaʊs zaɪn — bəˈaɪlʊŋ] — "Route 47 must ship by 2 PM — hurry up!" (Urgent dispatch deadline.)
2. **"Stapler frei in Gang C — Fußgänger raus!"** — [ˈʃtaplɐ fʁaɪ ɪn ɡaŋ tseː — ˈfuːsɡɛŋɐ ʁaʊs] — "Forklift active in aisle C — pedestrians out!" (Safety warning over PA system.)
3. **"Lieferschein prüfen — die Menge stimmt nicht!"** — [ˈliːfɐʃaɪn ˈpʁyːfn̩ — diː ˈmɛŋə ʃtɪmt nɪçt] — "Check the delivery note — the quantity is wrong!" (Receiving discrepancy.)
4. **"Palette kippt — sofort absetzen!"** — [paˈlɛtə kɪpt — zoˈfɔʁt ˈapzɛtsn̩] — "Pallet is tipping — set it down immediately!" (Critical safety command during forklift operation.)
5. **"Schichtende — Übergabeprotokoll schreiben!"** — [ˈʃɪçtʔɛndə — ˈyːbɐɡaːbəpʁotoˌkɔl ˈʃʁaɪbn̩] — "End of shift — write the handover report!" (Documentation before leaving.)

**3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:**

1. **"Erst die Sicherheit, dann die Geschwindigkeit."** — "Safety first, then speed." (Herr Weber's mantra. Speed matters, but never at the cost of safety.)
2. **"Was nicht gescannt ist, existiert nicht."** — "What isn't scanned doesn't exist." (If the WMS doesn't record it, the inventory is wrong. Every single item must be scanned.)
3. **"Die Tour wartet nicht."** — "The route doesn't wait." (The LKW has a fixed departure time. If the order isn't picked and loaded, the delivery fails.)

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Safety Briefing, UVV & First Pick**

**Setup:** 06:00 Monday. The player arrives at Logistikzentrum Berlin-Süd for the Frühschicht (6:00–14:00). Herr Weber meets them at the Zeiterfassungsterminal (time clock).

**Beat 1 — UVV Safety Briefing (Unfallverhütungsvorschrift):**
Herr Weber leads the player to the Schulungsraum (training room):
- "PSA anziehen: Stahlkappenschuhe, Warnweste, Helm. Ohne PSA betrittst du nicht die Lagerfläche. Verstanden?"
- "Fußgängerwege sind gelb markiert. Du gehst nur auf den gelben Linien. Stapler haben immer Vorfahrt. Wenn du einen Stapler hörst, bleibst du stehen und machst Blickkontakt mit dem Fahrer."
- "Notausgänge sind grün beleuchtet. Im Brandfall: alles stehen lassen, raus über den nächsten Notausgang, Sammelpunkt auf dem Parkplatz. Nicht dein Handy retten, nicht deine Jacke — raus."
- "Gefahrgut (dangerous goods) ist in Zone F. Da gehst du nicht rein ohne Schulung. Die Symbole auf den Kartons musst du kennen: entzündlich, ätzend, giftig."

**Beat 2 — WMS Scanner Training:**
Herr Weber hands the player an MDE-Gerät (mobile data entry scanner):
- "Jeder Lagerplatz hat einen Barcode: Gang-Regal-Ebene-Platz. Beispiel: C-04-03-02 bedeutet Gang C, Regal 4, Ebene 3, Platz 2. Du scannst den Platz, dann den Artikel, dann die Menge. Wenn der Scanner rot blinkt, stimmt etwas nicht — dann sofort mich rufen."
- The player practices scanning 10 dummy locations and matching them to a pick list on the scanner screen.

**Beat 3 — First Live Kommissionierung:**
The player receives their first real pick list: 15 Positionen for Tour 47. Herr Weber assigns them a Kommissionierwagen:
- "Du hast eine Stunde. Gang A bis D, hauptsächlich Kleinteile. Scanner an, Liste abarbeiten, Karton auf den Wagen, am Ende zur Packstation bringen."
- The player navigates the aisles, scans locations, counts quantities, and places items in the carton. Herr Weber checks the completed order: "Zwei Artikel fehlen — du hast die Mengenangabe nicht genau gelesen. Nochmal."

**Language demands:**
- "Wo finde ich den Artikel mit der Nummer 4782?"
- "Ist dieser Lagerplatz korrekt? Der Scanner zeigt einen Fehler."
- "Die Palette ist beschädigt — soll ich sie trotzdem einlagern?"
- "Wie viel Stück soll ich kommissionieren?"

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — LKW-Entladung und Einlagerung (Truck Unloading & Put-Away)**

**Setup:** Week 9. The player is now a competent Kommissionierer and has begun working the Wareneingang (receiving dock). A LKW from a supplier arrives at Rampe 2 with 22 Paletten of mixed goods.

**Beat 1 — Receiving & Inspection:**
The LKW driver hands the player the Lieferschein and Frachtbrief. The player must:
- Verify the Lieferschein against the Bestellung (purchase order) in the WMS: "Lieferschein 2024-8847, Lieferant Müller GmbH, 22 Paletten, Artikelnummern 1001 bis 1045."
- Inspect each pallet for damage: "Palette 7 hat einen Wasserschaden — die Kartons sind aufgeweicht. Das protokolliere ich als Mangel." The player photographs the damage with the MDE-Gerät and notes it on the Lieferschein.
- Count the pallets and confirm the quantity: "Zweiundzwanzig Paletten, Menge stimmt. Unterschrift auf dem Frachtbrief."

**Beat 2 — Einlagerung (Put-Away):**
The WMS assigns storage locations for each pallet. The player operates the Gabelstapler (having earned their Staplerschein in Week 6):
- "Palette 1 bis 5: Gang A, Regal 1 bis 3, Ebene 1. Palette 6 bis 12: Gang B, Hochregal, Ebene 4." The player drives the forklift, lifts the pallets to the correct height, and slides them into the racking. The sensory experience: the forklift's hydraulic whine, the slight sway of the mast at full height, the precision required to slot a 1,000 kg pallet into a space with 5 cm clearance on each side.
- The player scans each location and each pallet barcode to confirm put-away in the WMS.

**Beat 3 — Documentation:**
The player completes the Wareneingangsprotokoll (receiving log): time of arrival, LKW license plate, driver name, number of pallets, damage notes, storage locations. Herr Weber signs off: "Sauber gearbeitet. Die beschädigte Palette geht in die Quarantänezone — nicht in den regulären Bestand."

**Language demands:**
- "Der Lieferschein weicht von der Bestellung ab — ich melde das dem Einkauf."
- "Die Palette ist zu schwer für das Hochregal — maximal 800 Kilo auf Ebene 5."
- "Rampe 2 ist frei — der nächste LKW kann andocken."
- "Die Ware ist temperaturgeführt — sofort ins Kühllager!"

**Scenario C: Der Notfall — Der Staplerunfall (Forklift Collision)**

**Setup:** Week 16. Friday afternoon, 13:30. The warehouse is at peak activity — three LKWs being loaded simultaneously, all Kommissionierer rushing to meet the 14:00 dispatch deadline. The noise level is at 90 dB.

**Beat 1 — The Collision:**
A fellow worker, Tom, is driving a Gabelstapler around the blind corner at the intersection of Gang D and Gang E at excessive speed. He doesn't see a pedestrian — a new temp worker (Leiharbeiter) — stepping out from behind a stack of pallets. The forklift's front corner clips the worker's leg. The worker screams and falls. The sound: a sickening thud of metal on bone, followed by a sharp cry of pain. The forklift's load — a pallet of glass bottles — shifts and crashes to the floor, shattering hundreds of bottles. Glass shards scatter across the concrete. The smell of spilled liquid (Fruchtsaft — fruit juice) mixes with the metallic tang.

**Beat 2 — Emergency Response:**
The player is 10 meters away and must act immediately:
1. **Shout the warning:** "Unfall! Alle stehen bleiben! Stapler aus! Gang D und E sperren!"
2. **Call for help:** Use the warehouse emergency phone to call the Rettungsdienst (112): "Notruf, Logistikzentrum Berlin-Süd, Weserstraße 200. Arbeitsunfall, Beinverletzung, eine Person am Boden, bei Bewusstsein. Zugang über Tor 3."
3. **First Aid:** The player runs to the Verletzte (injured person), applies pressure to the bleeding wound with a clean cloth from the Erste-Hilfe-Kasten (first aid kit), and keeps the worker calm: "Bleib ruhig, der Krankenwagen kommt gleich. Beweg das Bein nicht."
4. **Secure the area:** The player uses Absperrband (barrier tape) to cordon off the glass-covered zone and prevents other forklifts from entering.

**Beat 3 — The Aftermath:**
The Rettungsdienst arrives in 8 minutes and transports the injured worker. Herr Weber is furious — at Tom for speeding, at the system for allowing a blind corner without a Spiegel (convex mirror). The player must write a detailed Unfallbericht (accident report) for the Berufsgenossenschaft (BG — the German occupational insurance authority): time, location, persons involved, sequence of events, injuries, first aid measures, witnesses. The game evaluates the player's speed of response, accuracy of the 112 call, quality of first aid, and completeness of the Unfallbericht.

**Language demands (crisis register):**
- "Unfall! Sofort den Notarzt rufen!"
- "Niemand darf den Bereich betreten!"
- "Wo ist der Erste-Hilfe-Kasten?"
- "Der Verletzte ist ansprechbar — Beinverletzung, starke Blutung."

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

The player must pass the **IHK-Abschlussprüfung Fachkraft für Lagerlogistik**.

- **Teil 1 (Praktische Prüfung — 5 hours):** The player performs a complete logistics cycle: receiving a delivery, inspecting for damage, entering goods into the WMS, storing in the correct location, picking a complex multi-item order, packing and labeling for shipment, loading a LKW, and completing all documentation (Lieferschein, Frachtbrief, Ladungssicherungsprotokoll). Forklift operation is tested separately.
- **Teil 2 (Schriftliche Prüfung — 3 subjects):**
  - **Lagerprozesse:** Güterannahme, Lagerorganisation, Kommissionierverfahren, Verpackungsverordnung, Gefahrgutvorschriften (ADR).
  - **Güterversand:** Frachtrecht, Incoterms, Zollabwicklung, Ladungssicherung (VDI 2700), Transportversicherungen.
  - **Wirtschafts- und Sozialkunde:** Arbeitsrecht, Berufsgenossenschaft, UVV, Mitbestimmung, Umweltschutz im Lager.
- **Key Exam Vocabulary:** die Bestandsführung (inventory management), der Mindestbestand (minimum stock level), der Meldebestand (reorder point), die FIFO-Methode (First In, First Out), die Chargennummer (batch number), die Ladungssicherung (load securing), der Gefahrgutschein (hazardous materials certificate), die Zollanmeldung (customs declaration), der Frachtführer (carrier), die Mautgebühr (toll fee).
- **Visa Impact:** The IHK-Abschluss qualifies the player as a skilled logistics worker (anerkannte Fachkraft), enabling a Fachkräftevisum under §18a AufenthG. The Gabelstaplerschein (forklift license) is an additional certified qualification that increases employability across all German logistics hubs.

---

### PROFESSION 10: TISCHLER/IN / SCHREINER/IN (Carpenter / Fine Cabinet Maker)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works at "Tischlerei Holz & Form," a medium-sized Schreinerei (joinery workshop) in a converted industrial building off Sonnenallee. The workshop (die Werkstatt) is approximately 400 m² with high ceilings, exposed steel beams, and large north-facing windows that flood the space with even, natural light. The floor is solid concrete, covered in a fine layer of sawdust (die Sägespäne) that crunches underfoot. The machinery zone contains a Formatkreissäge (panel saw), a Dickenhobel (thickness planer), eine Abrichthobelmaschine (jointer), a Tischfräse (spindle moulder), a Langlochbohrmaschine (mortiser), and a Breitbandschleifmaschine (wide-belt sander) — each surrounded by yellow safety zones painted on the floor. The Handarbeitsbereich (handwork area) has six massive Hobelbänke (workbenches) of scarred beech wood, each fitted with a Vorderzange (front vise) and a Hinterzange (rear vise). The Holzllager (timber store) in the back is stacked floor-to-ceiling with planks of oak (Eiche), walnut (Nussbaum), beech (Buche), maple (Ahorn), and pine (Kiefer), organized by species and thickness. The Lackierraum (finishing room) is a sealed, ventilated booth with spray equipment.

**Specific Smells:**
The dominant scent is fresh-cut wood — each species has its own aroma: the sweet, honey-like warmth of Eiche (oak), the rich chocolate-bitter note of Nussbaum (walnut), the clean, slightly sour tang of Buche (beech), the resinous pine-needle scent of Kiefer (pine). Beneath this: the sharp, solvent-heavy smell of Holzleim (wood glue — PVA glue has a mild vinegar note; PU glue is almost odorless but toxic), the pungent chemical bite of Lack (varnish/lacquer) and Beize (wood stain) from the finishing room, the hot-metal smell of saw blades cutting through dense hardwood, and the dusty, dry scent of sawdust accumulation.

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
85–95 dB when machinery is running — the scream of the Formatkreissäge ripping through oak (95 dB), the deep rumble of the Dickenhobel (88 dB), the high-pitched whine of the Oberfräse (router, 92 dB), the rhythmic thud of the Hammer und Meißel (hammer and chisel) in the handwork area. Gehörschutz (hearing protection) is mandatory during machine operation. When machines are off, the workshop drops to 50–60 dB — the scrape of a Handhobel (hand plane), the tap of a wooden mallet, the hiss of sandpaper.

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**Name:** Tischlermeister Friedrich Braun
**Age:** 58
**Personality:** Meister Braun is a lean, weathered man with calloused hands, sawdust permanently embedded in the creases of his flannel shirt, and reading glasses perched on his forehead. He is a third-generation Tischler who apprenticed under his grandfather. He is a purist: he believes in the integrity of solid wood (Massivholz) and views MDF and particle board (Spanplatte) with barely concealed contempt. He is patient with genuine learners but has no tolerance for carelessness — a millimeter of error in a mortise-and-tenon joint (Zapfenverbindung) is unacceptable. He speaks slowly, deliberately, in a warm but firm Schwäbisch-tinged Hochdeutsch (he moved to Berlin from Stuttgart decades ago). He measures everything twice and cuts once.
**Speech Cadence:** Slow, methodical, with long pauses. "Du misst... du prüfst... und dann erst schneidest du." He often demonstrates silently, letting his hands do the teaching.
**Expectations:** Absolute precision (Passgenauigkeit). The player must learn to read technical drawings (die technische Zeichnung), operate all stationary machines safely (with the Maschinenführerschein), and master hand-tool joinery. Cleanliness: the workshop is swept every evening, and every tool is returned to its designated shadow board (die Werkzeugwand).

2. **Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology**

**20 Specific Tools / Instruments / Materials:**

|---|---|---|---|

| 10 | lackieren (lackiert, lackierte, lackiert) | to varnish / lacquer | Den Tisch mit Klarlack lackieren |

3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Exact German Imperative Commands on the Job:**

1. **"Schutzbrille auf — die Kreissäge läuft!"** — [ˈʃʊtsbʁɪlə aʊf — diː ˈkʁaɪsˌzɛːɡə ˈlɔɪft] — "Safety glasses on — the circular saw is running!" (Mandatory PPE before any machine operation.)
2. **"Schiebeschild benutzen — nicht die Finger!"** — [ˈʃiːbəʃɪlt bəˈnʊtsn̩ — nɪçt diː ˈfɪŋɐ] — "Use the push stick — not your fingers!" (Critical safety command when using the table saw for narrow cuts.)
3. **"Absaugung einschalten — der Staub fliegt!"** — [ˈapzaʊɡʊŋ ˈaɪnʃaltn̩ — deːɐ ʃtaʊp ˈfliːkt] — "Turn on the dust extraction — the dust is flying!" (Dust extraction system must be on before any sanding or sawing.)
4. **"Erst anreißen, dann schneiden!"** — [eːɐst ˈanʁaɪsn̩ — dan ˈʃnaɪdn̩] — "Mark first, then cut!" (Meister Braun's golden rule: never cut without marking the cut line with a pencil and square.)
5. **"Werkzeug aufräumen — Feierabend!"** — [ˈvɛʁktsɔɪk ˈaʊfʁɔɪmən — ˈfaɪɐˌaːbn̩t] — "Clean up the tools — end of shift!" (Every tool back on the shadow board, machines cleaned, floor swept.)

**3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:**

1. **"Zweimal messen, einmal schneiden."** — "Measure twice, cut once." (The universal carpenter's proverb. Meister Braun repeats this daily.)
2. **"Holz arbeitet."** — "Wood works." (Wood expands and contracts with humidity. A Tischler must account for wood movement in every joint design.)
3. **"Das Auge isst mit, aber die Hand fühlt den Unterschied."** — "The eye eats first, but the hand feels the difference." (A surface may look smooth, but a true craftsman runs their hand over it to detect imperfections.)

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag — Werkstatt-Einweisung, Maschinensicherheit & First Cut**

**Setup:** 07:00 Monday. The player arrives at Tischlerei Holz & Form. Meister Braun greets them at the Werkstatttor (workshop gate), wearing a leather apron and holding a cup of filter coffee.

**Beat 1 — Werkstatt-Tour & UVV:**
Meister Braun walks the player through:
- "Das ist die Maschinenzone. Jede Maschine hat einen gelben Sicherheitskreis. Betrittst du den Kreis nicht, wenn die Maschine läuft, es sei denn, du bist der Bediener. Gehörschutz, Schutzbrille, eng anliegende Kleidung — keine weiten Ärmel, keine Ketten, keine langen Haare offen. Die Kreissäge zieht alles ein."
- "Not-Aus-Schalter (emergency stop) — hier, hier, und hier. Rote Pilzknöpfe. Im Notfall: draufschlagen. Die Maschine stoppt sofort."
- "Absauganlage (dust extraction): vor jeder Maschine einschalten. Holzstaub ist krebserregend — das ist kein Witz. Die Absaugung läuft immer."
- "Feuerlöscher: hier, hier, und hier. Holzstaub ist hochexplosiv. Kein offenes Feuer, kein Rauchen in der Werkstatt."

**Beat 2 — Hand Tool Introduction:**
Meister Braun hands the player a Handhobel and a piece of Buche:
- "Bevor du an die Maschinen gehst, lernst du das Handwerk von Hand. Hobel einstellen: das Eisen (blade) darf nur einen Haarbreit über den Hobelsohle stehen. Du hobelst mit dem Faserverlauf (with the grain), nie dagegen. Fühlst du den Widerstand? Das ist das Holz, das dir sagt, wie du arbeiten musst."
- The player practices planing the rough board until one face is perfectly flat (planparallel). Meister Braun checks with the Winkel: "Noch ein halber Millimeter Luft auf der linken Seite. Nochmal."

**Beat 3 — First Machine Cut (Formatkreissäge):**
Under direct supervision, the player makes their first cut on the panel saw:
- "Werkstück auf den Schiebetisch legen. Anschlag einstellen auf 400 Millimeter. Schutzhaube runter. Schiebeschild in die rechte Hand. Einschalten. Warten, bis das Sägeblatt volle Drehzahl hat. Dann schieben — gleichmäßig, nicht zu schnell, nicht zu langsam. Finger weg vom Blatt."
- The player makes the cut. The sensory experience: the scream of the blade, the spray of fine sawdust, the clean separation of the wood, the sweet smell of fresh-cut Buche. Meister Braun measures: "400,2 Millimeter. Für den Anfang akzeptabel. Aber wir wollen 400,0."

**Language demands:**
- "Welche Drehzahl brauche ich für Eichenholz?"
- "Ist das Sägeblatt scharf genug? Es brennt im Schnitt."
- "Wie stelle ich den Parallelanschlag ein?"
- "Darf ich die Oberfräse benutzen?"

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag — Bau eines Massivholztisches (Building a Solid Wood Table)**

**Setup:** Week 12. The player is now competent with all stationary machines and hand tools. Meister Braun assigns them their first independent project: a dining table (Esstisch) from massive Eiche, 180 × 90 cm, with a traditional Zapfenverbindung (mortise-and-tenon joint) frame.

**Beat 1 — Technical Drawing & Material Selection:**
The player reads the technische Zeichnung (technical drawing): top view, side view, detail of the joint. They select the timber from the Holzlager — four Eiche planks for the tabletop (Tischplatte), four legs (Tischbeine), and four aprons (Zargen). Meister Braun checks: "Das Holz muss akklimatisiert sein — mindestens zwei Wochen in der Werkstatt gelagert, damit es sich an die Luftfeuchtigkeit anpasst. Sonst verzieht es sich später."

**Beat 2 — Cutting & Joinery:**
The player cuts all components to size on the Formatkreissäge, planes them to thickness on the Dickenhobel, and then begins the joinery:
- "Die Zapfen (tenons) an den Zargen: 40 mm lang, 10 mm dick. Die Zapfenlöcher (mortises) in den Tischbeinen: exakt passend. Du stemmst die Löcher mit dem Stemmeisen und dem Holzhammer — vorsichtig, millimetergenau."
- The player dry-fits the joints (die Trockenprobe): "Der Zapfen muss mit leichtem Druck ins Zapfenloch gehen — nicht zu locker, nicht zu fest. Wenn du den Hammer brauchst, ist es zu eng. Wenn er wackelt, ist es zu weit."

**Beat 3 — Gluing, Clamping & Finishing:**
The player applies Holzleim to all joints, assembles the frame, and clamps it with Schraubzwingen: "Die Zwingen gleichmäßig anziehen — nicht zu fest, sonst drückst du den Leim raus. Leimfugen müssen dünn sein." After 24 hours of drying, the player sands the entire table (Körnung 80 → 120 → 180 → 240), applies a natural oil finish (das Hartöl), and presents the finished table to Meister Braun. He runs his hand over the surface: "Gut. Die Oberfläche ist glatt, die Fugen sind dicht. Die Beine stehen plan. Das ist ein Tisch, der hundert Jahre hält."

**Language demands:**
- "Ich brauche noch zwei Schraubzwingen — 800 Millimeter Spannweite."
- "Der Zapfen passt nicht — ich muss das Zapfenloch nachstemmen."
- "Wie lange muss der Leim trocknen?"
- "Soll ich das Holz ölen oder lackieren?"

**Scenario C: Der Notfall — Der Rückschlag an der Kreissäge (Kickback on the Table Saw)**

**Setup:** Week 20. The player is cutting a narrow strip of Nussbaum on the Formatkreissäge. The workshop is busy — three other Tischler are working on machines simultaneously. The noise level is 90 dB.

**Beat 1 — The Kickback:**
The player is ripping a 30 mm wide strip from a thick Nussbaum plank. The wood has internal tension (Spannung im Holz) — a hidden knot (der Ast) causes the kerf to close and pinch the spinning blade. The workpiece is violently thrown backward (der Rückschlag) at approximately 100 km/h, striking the player in the torso. The impact knocks the wind out of them. The sensory experience: a sudden, explosive crack like a gunshot, the blur of the wood projectile, the sharp pain in the ribs, the smell of burnt wood from the blade friction.

**Beat 2 — Immediate Response:**
The player must:
1. Hit the Not-Aus-Schalter (emergency stop) on the saw — the blade spins down.
2. Assess their own injuries: bruised ribs, no broken skin. "Ich bin okay — nur eine Prellung."
3. Alert Meister Braun: "Rückschlag an der Kreissäge! Das Brett hat verklemmt!"
4. Secure the area: turn off the machine, remove the damaged workpiece, inspect the blade for damage.

**Beat 3 — Investigation & Prevention:**
Meister Braun examines the workpiece and identifies the cause: "Das Holz hat einen Spannungsast — das hättest du vor dem Schnitt prüfen müssen. Und du hast den Spaltkeil (riving knife) nicht kontrolliert. Der Spaltkeil verhindert genau das — er hält den Schnitt offen. Ohne Spaltkeil ist die Kreissäge eine Waffe." The player must write a Beinahe-Unfall-Bericht (near-miss report) and undergo a refresher training on kickback prevention. The game logs the error and adds a permanent safety note to the player's competency tracker.

**Language demands (crisis register):**
- "Rückschlag! Maschine aus!"
- "Ich bin verletzt — ich brauche den Erste-Hilfe-Kasten."
- "Das Sägeblatt ist beschädigt — nicht weiterbenutzen!"
- "Der Spaltkeil war nicht richtig eingestellt."

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

The player must pass the **Gesellenprüfung Tischler/in** administered by the **Handwerkskammer (HWK) Berlin**.

- **Teil 1 (Praktische Prüfung — 16 hours over 2 days):** The player builds a complex furniture piece from a technical drawing — typically a cabinet (Schrank) or table with at least two types of joinery (e.g., Schwalbenschwanzverbindung / dovetail joint and Zapfenverbindung), a veneered surface (Furnier), and a finished surface treatment. The examiners evaluate dimensional accuracy (±0.5 mm), joint quality, surface finish, and adherence to the drawing.
- **Teil 2 (Theoretische Prüfung — written, 4 hours):** Fachtechnologie (wood species, wood technology, machine operation, joinery types), Fachmathematik (material calculations, cutting lists, cost estimation), Technische Mathematik (reading and interpreting technical drawings), Wirtschafts- und Sozialkunde (Auftragsabwicklung, Kalkulation, Gewährleistung).
- **Key Exam Vocabulary:** die Holzfeuchtigkeit (wood moisture content), der Faserverlauf (grain direction), die Schwindung (shrinkage), das Quellen (swelling), die Furniertechnik (veneer technique), die Oberflächenbehandlung (surface treatment), die Holzschutzverordnung (wood preservation ordinance), die CE-Kennzeichnung (CE marking), die DIN-Norm (DIN standard), die Materialliste (cutting list / bill of materials).
- **Visa Impact:** The Gesellenbrief (journeyman certificate) is a fully recognized German vocational qualification under the Handwerksordnung (HwO). It grants the player the right to work as a qualified Tischler/in throughout Germany and the EU, and opens the path to the Tischlermeister/in title (required for self-employment in the craft).

---

### PROFESSION 11: MAURER/IN / BAUHANDWERKER/IN (Bricklayer / Construction Craftsman)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works on a Rohbau (structural shell) construction site (die Baustelle) for a new six-story Wohngebäude (residential building) on a side street off Sonnenallee. The site is a raw, exposed skeleton of reinforced concrete (Stahlbeton) and Kalksandstein (sand-lime brick) walls. Scaffolding (das Baugerüst) wraps the exterior in a grid of steel tubes and wooden planks (Gerüstbohlen). The ground is uneven, muddy in patches, littered with broken bricks, mortar splatter, and offcuts of rebar (der Bewehrungsstahl). A Baukran (tower crane) dominates the skyline, its hook swinging loads of palletized bricks to the upper floors. A Betonmischer (concrete mixer truck) is parked at the site entrance, its drum rotating. The Bauleiter's container (der Baucontainer) sits near the gate, covered in blueprints and safety signs. The player's workspace shifts daily — today the third floor, tomorrow the ground floor — as the building rises.

**Specific Smells:**
Wet cement and mortar (feucht, alkalisch, mineralisch — a chalky, caustic smell that dries the nostrils), the metallic tang of cut rebar, the diesel exhaust from the Betonmischer and Bagger (excavator), the earthy smell of damp soil from the foundation excavation (die Baugrube), the chemical odor of Baukleber (construction adhesive) and Dichtmittel (sealant), and in summer, the hot-tar smell of Bitumen from the waterproofing layer (Abdichtung).

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
85–100 dB — one of the loudest work environments. The rhythmic clack-clack-clack of the Maurerhammer (brick hammer) on bricks, the grinding roar of the Trennschleifer (angle grinder) cutting through Kalksandstein (95 dB), the deep rumble of the Betonmischer, the metallic clang of scaffolding poles being assembled, the shout of the Kranführer (crane operator) directing loads, the thud of heavy pallets being set down, the hiss of pneumatic nail guns, and the constant beeping of reversing vehicles (Rückfahrwarner). Echoes bounce off unfinished concrete walls, making speech difficult at distance.

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**Meister Horst Krüger**, 54, a weathered, broad-shouldered Maurermeister from Brandenburg with 35 years on the Bau. His face is permanently dusted with a fine layer of cement powder, his hands are calloused into leather. He speaks in a clipped, gruff Brandenburg dialect, dropping word endings ("hamma" instead of "haben wir," "nächste" instead of "nächsten"). His speech cadence is staccato and loud — he never whispers, even up close, because years on the Baustelle have half-deafened him. He expects absolute precision in Lot (plumb) and Flucht (alignment) and will tear down an entire wall if the joints are uneven. He respects hard work and punctuality above all. His signature phrase: *"Mauerwerk lügt nicht — es steht oder es fällt."* (Masonry doesn't lie — it stands or it falls.)

---

2. **Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology**

**20 Nouns (Tools, Instruments, Materials):**

| # | German (Article + Noun) | Plural | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | die Maurerkelle | die Maurerkellen | bricklaying trowel |
| 2 | der Maurerhammer | die Maurerhämmer | brick hammer |
| 3 | die Wasserwaage | die Wasserwaagen | spirit level |
| 4 | das Lot (Senklot) | die Lote | plumb bob |
| 5 | die Richtschnur | die Richtschnüre | mason's line / string line |
| 6 | der Mörteltrog | die Mörteltröge | mortar trough |
| 7 | die Fugenkelle | die Fugenkellen | pointing trowel / jointing trowel |
| 8 | der Kalksandstein | die Kalksandsteine | sand-lime brick |
| 9 | der Ziegelstein | die Ziegelsteine | clay brick |
| 10 | der Mörtel | die Mörtel (sorts) | mortar |
| 11 | der Beton | die Betone (sorts) | concrete |
| 12 | die Schalung | die Schalungen | formwork |

**10 Domain-Specific Action Verbs:**

| # | German Verb | English | Example Context |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | mauern | to lay bricks / to build a wall | *Wir mauern heute die Außenwand.* |
| 2 | verfugen | to point (mortar joints) | *Die Fugen müssen sauber verfugt werden.* |
| 3 | anmischen | to mix (mortar/concrete) | *Misch den Mörtel neu an.* |
| 4 | ausloten | to plumb / to check vertical alignment | *Lot die Ecke aus!* |
| 5 | fluchten | to align (horizontally) | *Die Wand fluchtet nicht.* |
| 6 | schalen | to build formwork | *Wir schalen morgen die Decke.* |
| 7 | bewehren | to reinforce (with rebar) | *Die Stütze wird bewehrt.* |
| 8 | abbinden | to set / to cure (concrete/mortar) | *Der Beton muss 28 Tage abbinden.* |
| 9 | verdichten | to compact / to vibrate (concrete) | *Den Beton mit dem Rüttler verdichten.* |
| 10 | abziehen | to screed / to level off | *Die Decke sauber abziehen.* |

---

3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Imperative Commands Shouted on the Job:**

1. **"Mörtel her! ZACK!"** — /ˈmœʁtl̩ heːɐ̯ tsak/ — "Mortar here! NOW!" (Urgent demand for mortar supply to keep the laying pace.)
2. **"Lot prüfen! Die Wand kippt!"** — /loːt ˈpʁyːfən diː vant ˈkɪpt/ — "Check plumb! The wall is tilting!" (Warning that the wall is leaning out of vertical.)
3. **"Fuge sauber! Nicht schmieren!"** — /ˈfuːɡə ˈzaʊbɐ nɪçt ˈʃmiːʁən/ — "Clean joint! Don't smear it!" (Criticism of sloppy pointing work.)
4. **"Stein drehen! Läufer, kein Binder!"** — /ʃtaɪ̯n ˈdʁeːən ˈlɔɪ̯fɐ kaɪ̯n ˈbɪndɐ/ — "Turn the brick! Stretcher, not header!" (Correcting the bond pattern in the wall.)
5. **"Schalung fest! Beton kommt in zehn!"** — /ˈʃaːlʊŋ fɛst bəˈtɔŋ kɔmt ɪn tseːn/ — "Formwork tight! Concrete arrives in ten minutes!" (Urgent preparation order before the mixer truck pours.)

**3 Common Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:**

1. **"Das ist schief wie der Turm von Pisa."** — "That's as crooked as the Tower of Pisa." (Said when a wall or column is visibly out of plumb. A classic Maurer insult.)
2. **"Mörtel ist kein Nutella."** — "Mortar isn't Nutella." (Said when an apprentice spreads mortar too thick and sloppily, like a child spreading chocolate on bread.)
3. **"Auf dem Bau gibt's kein schlechtes Wetter, nur schlechte Kleidung."** — "On the construction site there's no bad weather, only bad clothing." (The universal Bauarbeiter motto, usually said in freezing rain.)

---

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag (Orientation & Safety Briefing — UVV)**

*Beat 1 — Arrival at the Baustelle (06:45):* The player arrives at the construction gate on a cold Monday morning. The site is already active — the Betonmischer is idling, workers in high-vis vests (Warnwesten) and hard hats (Bauhelm) cross the muddy yard. Meister Krüger meets the player at the Baucontainer. He points at the player's feet: *"Wo sind deine Sicherheitsschuhe? S3, Stahlkappe! Ohne die gehst du hier nicht rein."* (Where are your safety shoes? S3, steel toe! Without them you're not coming in here.) The player must select the correct PSA from a gear rack: **der Bauhelm** (hard hat), **die Sicherheitsschuhe S3** (steel-toe boots), **die Warnweste** (hi-vis vest), **die Arbeitshandschuhe** (work gloves), **die Schutzbrille** (safety goggles), and **der Gehörschutz** (ear protection).

*Beat 2 — UVV Safety Walk (07:15):* Krüger walks the player through the site, pointing out danger zones with rapid, loud German: *"Absturzkante — nicht ohne Gurt!"* (Fall edge — not without a harness!), *"Kranbereich — nie drunter stehen!"* (Crane zone — never stand underneath!), *"Stolperfallen — Kabel aufräumen!"* (Trip hazards — tidy up cables!). The player must correctly label five hazard signs (Gefahrenzeichen): **Absturzgefahr** (fall hazard), **Helm tragen** (wear hard hat), **Gehörschutz tragen** (wear ear protection), **Feuergefährlich** (flammable), and **Baustellenverkehr** (construction vehicle traffic). Each sign requires the player to read the German text and match it to the pictogram.

*Beat 3 — First Assignment (08:00):* Krüger assigns the player to a simple task: mixing a batch of mortar in the Mörteltrog and carrying it to the Maurer on the second floor. The player must follow the mixing ratio (*"Drei Teile Sand, ein Teil Zement, Wasser nach Gefühl — aber nicht zu dünn!"* — Three parts sand, one part cement, water by feel — but not too thin!). The player mixes, loads the mortar onto a **Mörtelbrett** (mortar board), and carries it up the scaffolding stairs. The physical action requires careful balance — the game simulates the weight and slosh of wet mortar. If the player spills, Krüger shouts: *"Saubermachen! Sofort! Mörtel ist kein Geschenk!"*

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag (The Standard Routine Task)**

*Beat 1 — Morning Briefing (07:00):* The team gathers at the Baucontainer. Krüger unrolls the **Bauzeichnung** (construction drawing) and assigns the day's task: building a 4-meter section of a Kalksandstein interior wall (Innenwand) on the third floor, 17.5 cm thick, with a **Türöffnung** (door opening) at the 1-meter mark. The player must read the drawing, identify the wall dimensions, the bond pattern (**Läuferverband** — stretcher bond), and the door opening position. German vocabulary demand: *"Die Wand ist 4 Meter lang, 2,50 hoch, 17,5 Zentimeter dick. Türöffnung bei Meter 1, Breite 87,5 Zentimeter."*

*Beat 2 — Execution (08:00–12:00):* The player sets the **Richtschnur** at the correct height, checks the first course (erste Schicht) with the **Wasserwaage** and **Lot**, and begins laying bricks. Each brick placement is a micro-event: scoop mortar with the **Maurerkelle**, butter the brick end (Stoßfuge), place the brick, tap it level with the **Maurerhammer** handle, check with the level, scrape excess mortar with the **Fugenkelle**. The game demands the player verbalize each step in German to the Geselle working alongside: *"Mörtel auftragen, Stein setzen, ausrichten, Fuge abziehen."* After every fifth course, the player must re-check plumb and alignment — if the wall drifts more than 3 mm, Krüger will notice and order a partial tear-down.

*Beat 3 — Documentation (15:30):* At the end of the shift, the player fills out the **Bautagesbericht** (daily construction report). Fields include: **Datum** (date), **Wetterbedingungen** (weather conditions), **Anzahl der Arbeitsstunden** (number of work hours), **ausgeführte Arbeiten** (work performed — e.g., *"Innenwand EG, Achse 3–5, 12 m² Kalksandsteinmauerwerk erstellt"*), **verwendete Materialien** (materials used), and **besondere Vorkommnisse** (special incidents). The player must write complete German sentences in the technical register. Krüger signs off with a grunt: *"Passt. Morgen weiter."*

**Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise (The Workplace Crisis)**

*Beat 1 — The Incident (10:15):* A sudden loud crack echoes across the site. The player looks up — a section of the **Schalung** (formwork) on the fourth floor has bulged outward under the pressure of wet concrete. Concrete is leaking through a gap in the formwork panels, pouring down onto the scaffolding below where two workers are standing. The **Polier** (foreman) screams from above: *"SCHALUNG PLATZT! ALLE WEG DA UNTEN! SOFORT! BETON STOPPEN!"* (Formwork is bursting! Everyone get away down there! NOW! Stop the concrete!)

*Beat 2 — Rapid Response (10:16–10:20):* The player must process the shouted commands in real-time and take three immediate actions: (1) shout a warning to the workers below — *"ACHTUNG! GEHT WEG! BETON KOMMT RUNTER!"* (Watch out! Move! Concrete is coming down!), (2) grab the **Not-Aus-Schalter** (emergency stop switch) on the concrete pump to halt the pour, and (3) help the Polier brace the bulging formwork with **Schalungsstützen** (formwork props). The game tests the player's ability to understand rapid, panicked, non-scaffolded German under extreme stress. Every second of hesitation causes more concrete to spill, increasing the consequence score.

*Beat 3 — Aftermath & Reporting (11:00):* The crisis is contained. The **Sicherheitsbeauftragter** (safety officer) arrives and demands a **Unfallbericht** (accident report). The player must verbally explain what happened in German: *"Die Schalung hat dem Betondruck nicht standgehalten. Die Spannstäbe waren nicht fest genug angezogen."* (The formwork couldn't withstand the concrete pressure. The tie rods weren't tightened enough.) The player fills out the incident form with fields for **Unfallzeit** (time of accident), **Unfallort** (location), **Unfallhergang** (sequence of events), **Verletzte** (injured persons), and **Sofortmaßnahmen** (immediate measures taken). Krüger is shaken but controlled: *"Das hätte tödlich enden können. Ab morgen doppelte Schalungskontrolle."* (That could have ended fatally. From tomorrow, double formwork inspection.)

---

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

**IHK / HWK Exam Structure:** The Maurer/in Ausbildung concludes with the **Gesellenprüfung** (journeyman's exam) administered by the **Handwerkskammer (HWK)**. The exam has two parts:

- **Gesellenprüfung Teil 1** (after 2nd year): A practical exam (6 hours) where the candidate must build a complex masonry structure from a technical drawing — including a corner, a door opening, a window opening, and a decorative bond pattern (e.g., **Kreuzverband** or **Gotischer Verband**). Theoretical written exam covers **Baustoffkunde** (building materials science), **Bauphysik** (building physics — heat, moisture, sound insulation), and **Technische Mathematik** (calculating volumes of concrete, brick counts, mortar quantities).

- **Gesellenprüfung Teil 2** (final): A comprehensive practical project (8 hours) and an oral examination (**Fachgespräch**) where the candidate must explain construction details, safety regulations (**UVV Bau**, **BG BAU Vorschriften**), and read complex **Bauzeichnungen** with **Schnittdarstellungen** (cross-sections) and **Bewehrungspläne** (reinforcement plans).

**Key Exam Vocabulary:**
- **die Rohbauabnahme** — structural shell inspection
- **die Wärmedämmung** — thermal insulation
- **die Feuchtigkeitsabdichtung** — moisture waterproofing
- **der Wärmebrückenberechnung** — thermal bridge calculation
- **die DIN-Norm (z.B. DIN 1053)** — German industrial standard for masonry
- **die VOB (Vergabe- und Vertragsordnung für Bauleistungen)** — construction contract regulations
- **die Statik** — structural engineering / statics
- **das Aufmaß** — measurement of completed work for billing

---

### PROFESSION 12: ZAHNMEDIZINISCHE/R FACHANGESTELLTE/R (ZFA) (Dental Assistant)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works in a modern **Zahnarztpraxis** (dental practice) on the second floor of an Altbau on Weserstraße. The reception area (**Empfang**) has a clean, minimalist design — white Corian counter, a computer screen running dental software (Dampsoft or Charly), a waiting area with magazines and a water dispenser. Behind the reception, a narrow hallway leads to three **Behandlungszimmer** (treatment rooms), each containing a **Behandlungseinheit** (dental chair unit) with its overhead lamp (**OP-Leuchte**), instrument tray (**Instrumententablett**), suction system (**Absauganlage**), and water/air syringe (**Spritzpistole**). A separate **Sterilisationsraum** (sterilization room) houses the **Thermodesinfektor** and **Autoklav**. The **Röntgenraum** (X-ray room) has lead-lined walls and a digital OPG machine. The **Labor** (small in-house lab) has a plaster model trimmer and a vacuum former. Everything is white, chrome, and clinical — the visual language of sterility.

**Specific Smells:**
The sharp, antiseptic bite of **Desinfektionsmittel** (disinfectant — usually a quaternary ammonium compound), the faint metallic-chemical smell of **Formaldehyd** from sterilization, the distinctive acrid odor of **Zahnbohren** (dental drilling) — burning tooth enamel and dentin (a smell patients dread), the sweet-minty scent of **Prophylaxepaste** (polishing paste), the rubbery smell of **Latexhandschuhe** (latex gloves), and the faint clove-like smell of **Eugenol** (temporary filling material).

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
50–70 dB — deceptively quiet until the drill starts. The high-pitched whine of the **Turbine** (high-speed dental drill) at 400,000 RPM is piercing and anxiety-inducing (the single most recognizable dental sound). The lower hum of the **Winkelstück** (low-speed handpiece), the wet sucking gurgle of the **Speichelsauger** (saliva ejector), the beep of the **Röntgengerät** (X-ray machine), the hiss of the **Druckluft** (compressed air) from the compressor in the basement, and the soft murmur of the dentist speaking to the patient through a mask. The acoustics are deadened by acoustic ceiling tiles and soft-close cabinetry.

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**Frau Dr. med. dent. Sabine Hartmann**, 47, the **Praxisinhaberin** (practice owner). She is precise, calm, and methodical — a perfectionist who speaks in a soft but firm Hochdeutsch. She never raises her voice but her disapproval is conveyed through a slight narrowing of her eyes and a clipped *"Noch einmal, bitte"* (Once more, please). She expects the ZFA to anticipate her instrument needs before she asks — a silent choreography of **Vier-Hand-Behandlung** (four-handed dentistry). Her speech cadence is measured and technical: she uses full Latin dental terminology mixed with German explanations for patients. Her signature phrase to the player: *"Der Patient spürt Ihre Nervosität. Atmen Sie, lächeln Sie, arbeiten Sie."* (The patient senses your nervousness. Breathe, smile, work.)

---

2. **Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology**

---

3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Imperative Commands Shouted (or Firmly Spoken) on the Job:**

1. **"Absaugen! Der Patient schluckt!"** — /ˈapzaʊ̯ɡən deːɐ̯ paˈtsi̯ɛnt ˈʃlʊkt/ — "Suction! The patient is swallowing!" (Urgent command during a procedure when saliva or water is pooling.)
2. **"Spiegel anreichen! Und die Sonde!"** — /ˈʃpiːɡəl ˈanʁaɪ̯çən ʊnt diː ˈzɔndə/ — "Hand me the mirror! And the explorer!" (Standard four-handed dentistry command during examination.)
3. **"Röntgen OPG, bitte! Bleischürze anlegen!"** — /ˈʁœntɡən oːpeːˈɡeː bɪtə ˈblaɪ̯ʃyːʁtsə ˈanleːɡən/ — "OPG X-ray, please! Put on the lead apron!" (Instruction for panoramic X-ray with radiation protection.)
4. **"Instrumente in den Thermo! Sofort!"** — /ɪnstʁuˈmɛntə ɪn deːn ˈtɛʁmo zɔˈfɔʁt/ — "Instruments into the thermowasher! Immediately!" (Post-treatment instrument reprocessing command.)
5. **"Patient hat Angst — erklären Sie den Ablauf!"** — /paˈtsi̯ɛnt hat aŋst ɛɐ̯ˈklɛːʁən ziː deːn ˈaplaʊ̯f/ — "The patient is anxious — explain the procedure!" (The dentist delegates patient communication to the ZFA.)

**3 Common Workplace Idioms / Expressions:**

1. **"Vier Hände sind besser als zwei."** — "Four hands are better than two." (The foundational principle of four-handed dentistry — the ZFA and dentist work as a synchronized team.)
2. **"Der Zahn ist nicht mehr zu retten."** — "The tooth can't be saved." (Said when a tooth is too decayed for a filling or crown and must be extracted. Also used metaphorically for hopeless situations.)
3. **"Lächeln ist die beste Betäubung."** — "A smile is the best anesthetic." (A common saying in dental practices about the power of a calm, friendly demeanor in reducing patient anxiety.)

---

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag (Orientation & Safety Briefing — UVV)**

*Beat 1 — Arrival & Hygiene Protocol (07:30):* The player arrives at the Praxis before opening hours. **Frau Dr. Hartmann** and the senior ZFA, **Lena Vogt** (28, efficient, warm but no-nonsense), greet the player. The first task is the **Hygieneplan** (hygiene protocol). Lena walks the player through the morning setup: wiping down all surfaces with **Flächendesinfektionsmittel** (surface disinfectant), checking the **Autoklav** cycle log, ensuring all instrument cassettes are sterile and sealed, and filling the **Desinfektionswanne** (disinfection tray) with fresh solution. The player must correctly identify and sort instruments into categories: **unkritisch** (non-critical — e.g., mouth mirror handle), **semikritisch** (semi-critical — e.g., mouth mirror, explorer), and **kritisch** (critical — e.g., surgical instruments that penetrate tissue). German demand: *"Kritische Instrumente müssen steril sein — Autoklav, 134 Grad, 5 Minuten Haltezeit."*

*Beat 2 — UVV & Strahlenschutz (08:30):* Dr. Hartmann conducts the **Strahlenschutzunterweisung** (radiation protection briefing). The player learns the rules of the **Röntgenraum**: only the patient stays inside during exposure, the ZFA stands behind the **Bleiwand** (lead wall) or wears a **Bleischürze** (lead apron) with **Schilddrüsenschutz** (thyroid collar). The player must read and acknowledge the **Röntgenverordnung (RöV)** compliance checklist in German. Vocabulary: **die Strahlenexposition** (radiation exposure), **der Dosisgrenzwert** (dose limit), **das Röntgenpass** (X-ray record card).

*Beat 3 — First Patient Interaction (09:15):* The first patient, **Herr Müller** (62, nervous, hates the dentist), arrives for a routine **Kontrolluntersuchung** (check-up). The player must greet him at the reception, verify his **Versichertenkarte** (insurance card), update his **Patientenakte** (patient file) in the software, and escort him to the Behandlungszimmer. The player must make small talk in German to calm him: *"Haben Sie Beschwerden? Tut etwas weh? Keine Sorge, heute machen wir nur eine Kontrolle."* (Do you have any complaints? Does anything hurt? Don't worry, today we're just doing a check-up.)

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag (The Standard Routine Task)**

*Beat 1 — Treatment Preparation (09:00):* The schedule shows a **Füllungstherapie** (filling treatment) for **Frau Schneider** (35) on tooth 36 (lower left first molar, **Zahn sechsunddreißig** — the ZFA must know the **FDI-Zahnschema** numbering system). The player prepares the treatment room: lays out the instrument set (**Untersuchungsbesteck**: Spiegel, Sonde, Pinzette), loads the **Turbine** and **Winkelstück** with the correct burs (**Diamantbohrer** for enamel, **Rosenbohrer** for dentin), prepares the **Kofferdam** (rubber dam) set, and mixes the **Komposit** shades (A2, A3) on the **Anmischblock**.

*Beat 2 — Four-Handed Treatment (09:15–10:00):* During the procedure, the player performs the ZFA's role in **Vier-Hand-Behandlung**: operating the **Absaugung** (suction) with the left hand while using the right hand to **anreichen** (hand) instruments to Dr. Hartmann in the correct sequence. The dentist calls out instrument names rapidly: *"Spiegel — Sonde — Turbine, diamant, grob — Winkelstück, rund — Ätzgel — Adhäsiv — Komposit A3 — Polymerisationslampe."* The player must anticipate each step and hand the correct instrument within 2 seconds. The game simulates the wet, confined space of the oral cavity — the player must keep the mirror fog-free with a quick blast of air and maintain suction without touching the patient's tongue or cheek.

*Beat 3 — Post-Treatment & Abrechnung (10:05):* After the filling, the player gives Frau Schneider post-treatment instructions in German: *"Bitte eine Stunde nichts essen. Die Betäubung wirkt noch — vorsichtig beim Kauen, nicht auf die Lippe beißen."* (Please don't eat for an hour. The anesthesia is still active — be careful chewing, don't bite your lip.) The player then processes the **Abrechnung** (billing) in the software, entering the correct **BEMA-Nummern** (statutory dental billing codes) or **GOZ-Ziffern** (private billing codes): e.g., **BEMA 13** (filling), **BEMA 15** (local anesthesia). The player prints the **Heil- und Kostenplan** (treatment and cost plan) for the next appointment.

**Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise (The Workplace Crisis)**

*Beat 1 — The Emergency (14:30):* During a routine **Zahnextraktion** (tooth extraction) on a patient, **Herr Weber** (58, overweight, known hypertension), the patient suddenly turns pale, starts sweating profusely, and clutches his chest. He gasps: *"Mir ist schlecht… mein Brustkorb… ich krieg keine Luft…"* (I feel sick… my chest… I can't breathe…). The **Blutdruckmessgerät** (blood pressure monitor) on the treatment unit shows 190/110 — a **hypertensive crisis** possibly escalating to a **Herzinfarkt** (heart attack).

*Beat 2 — Emergency Response (14:31–14:35):* Dr. Hartmann shouts: *"Notfall! Legen Sie ihn flach! Beine hoch! Notarzt rufen! 112!"* (Emergency! Lay him flat! Legs up! Call the emergency doctor! 112!) The player must: (1) immediately recline the **Behandlungsstuhl** to a flat position and elevate the legs, (2) grab the **Notfallkoffer** (emergency kit) and hand Dr. Hartmann the **Nitroglycerin-Spray** and **Aspirin 300mg**, (3) dial **112** and communicate the emergency in German to the **Rettungsleitstelle**: *"Zahnarztpraxis Hartmann, Weserstraße 42, 12045 Berlin. Patient, männlich, 58 Jahre, Verdacht auf Herzinfarkt, Brustschmerzen, Bluthochdruck. Wir leisten Erste Hilfe."* The game tests the player's ability to deliver precise, calm German under extreme pressure.

*Beat 3 — Handover to Rettungsdienst (14:40):* The **Rettungswagen** (ambulance) arrives. The player must hand over the patient's **Patientenakte** and verbally brief the **Notfallsanitäter** (paramedic): *"Der Patient hat Nitroglycerin und Aspirin bekommen. Blutdruck war 190 zu 110. Bekannte Hypertonie. Allergien: keine bekannt."* The player then completes the **Notfallprotokoll** (emergency protocol) for the Praxis records, documenting the exact timeline, medications administered, and vital signs. Dr. Hartmann is composed but serious: *"Gut reagiert. Das war knapp. Morgen machen wir eine Notfallübung mit dem ganzen Team."*

---

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

**IHK Exam Structure:** The ZFA Ausbildung (3 years) concludes with the **Abschlussprüfung** administered by the **Zahnärztekammer** (Dental Chamber). The exam has two parts:

- **Schriftliche Prüfung** (Written): Covers **Behandlungsassistenz** (treatment assistance), **Praxisorganisation und -verwaltung** (practice administration), **Wirtschafts- und Sozialkunde** (economics and social studies), and **Abrechnungswesen** (billing — BEMA and GOZ). Questions include case studies on patient management, hygiene protocols, and insurance law.

- **Praktische Prüfung** (Practical): The candidate performs a simulated four-handed treatment scenario, demonstrates instrument reprocessing (**Aufbereitung nach RKI-Richtlinie**), takes a dental X-ray with proper radiation protection, and conducts a patient consultation in German.

**Key Exam Vocabulary:**
- **die Prophylaxe** — preventive dental care
- **die Parodontitisbehandlung (PA-Behandlung)** — periodontal treatment
- **die Kieferorthopädie** — orthodontics
- **die Implantologie** — implantology
- **die Datenschutz-Grundverordnung (DSGVO)** — GDPR (patient data protection)
- **die Medizinprodukteaufbereitung** — medical device reprocessing
- **das Qualitätsmanagement (QM)** — quality management
- **die Hygieneverordnung** — hygiene regulation

---

### PROFESSION 13: ERZIEHER/IN (Kindergarten / Early Childhood Educator)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works in a **Kita** (Kindertagesstätte — daycare center) called "Sonnenkäfer" on a quiet side street off Sonnenallee. The building is a converted Altbau ground floor with high ceilings and large windows. The **Gruppenraum** (group room) for the player's group (the "Bärengruppe," 20 children aged 3–6) is a riot of color: low wooden shelves stocked with **Bauklötze** (building blocks), **Puzzles**, **Bilderbücher** (picture books), and **Kuscheltiere** (stuffed animals). A **Maltisch** (art table) is covered in wax crayons, finger paints, and construction paper. The **Teppichecke** (carpet corner) has a reading nook with cushions. The **Waschraum** (washroom) has child-sized toilets and sinks. The **Garderobe** (cloakroom) has hooks labeled with each child's name and a symbol (a star, a fish, a sun). Outside, the **Außengelände** (outdoor play area) has a sandbox, a climbing frame, and a small garden patch. The walls are covered in children's artwork, seasonal decorations, and the **Tagesplan** (daily schedule) written in large, colorful letters.

**Specific Smells:**
The warm, yeasty smell of **Bastelkleber** (craft glue — UHU or Pritt), the waxy scent of **Buntstifte** (colored pencils) and **Knetmasse** (playdough), the faint sour-milky smell of spilled **Kindermilch** (children's milk) and **Apfelschorle** (apple spritzer), the earthy smell of wet sand and mud from the Außengelände, the sharp tang of **Desinfektionsmittel** on the changing tables, and at lunchtime, the hearty smell of **Kinderessen** (children's meals — usually Nudeln mit Tomatensoße or Kartoffelsuppe from the caterer).

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
65–85 dB — a constant, cheerful cacophony. The high-pitched chatter and shrieking of 20 children, the clatter of wooden blocks being dumped, the squeak of rubber soles on linoleum, the sing-song voice of the Erzieher/in leading a **Morgenkreis** (morning circle) song, the crash of a toy shelf being knocked over, and the occasional wail of a child who has fallen or had a toy taken. The acoustics are echoey in the Gruppenraum despite acoustic panels — sound bounces off the high Altbau ceiling.

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**Frau Anke Bergmann**, 52, the **Kitaleitung** (daycare director). She is warm, maternal, and endlessly patient with the children but administratively sharp and demanding with her staff. She speaks in a clear, melodic Hochdeutsch that she modulates depending on her audience — high and sing-song for the children, direct and professional for the staff. She is a stickler for **Bildungsdokumentation** (educational documentation) and **Beobachtungsbögen** (observation forms). She expects every Erzieher/in to know each child's developmental milestones, allergies, and family situation by heart. Her signature phrase: *"Jedes Kind hat ein Recht auf Bindung. Seien Sie die sichere Basis."* (Every child has a right to attachment. Be the secure base.)

3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Imperative Commands Used on the Job:**

1. **"Alle in den Morgenkreis! Aufräumen, bitte!"** — /ˈalə ɪn deːn ˈmɔʁɡn̩kʁaɪ̯s ˈaʊ̯fʁɔɪ̯mən bɪtə/ — "Everyone into the morning circle! Tidy up, please!" (The daily transition signal.)
2. **"Hände waschen! Vor dem Essen immer!"** — /ˈhɛndə ˈvaʃən foːɐ̯ deːm ˈɛsn̩ ˈɪmɐ/ — "Wash hands! Always before eating!" (Hygiene command repeated dozens of times daily.)
3. **"Nicht schubsen! Wir benutzen unsere Worte!"** — /nɪçt ˈʃʊpsən viːɐ̯ bəˈnʊtsən ˈʊnzəʁə ˈvɔʁtə/ — "No pushing! We use our words!" (Conflict resolution phrase for toddlers.)
4. **"Jacke an, Mütze auf! Wir gehen raus!"** — /ˈjakə an ˈmʏtsə aʊ̯f viːɐ̯ ˈɡeːən ʁaʊ̯s/ — "Jacket on, hat on! We're going outside!" (Transition to outdoor play.)
5. **"Leise, leise! Die Kleinen schlafen!"** — /ˈlaɪ̯zə ˈlaɪ̯zə diː ˈklaɪ̯nən ˈʃlaːfən/ — "Quiet, quiet! The little ones are sleeping!" (During Mittagsruhe — nap time.)

**3 Common Workplace Idioms / Expressions:**

1. **"Das ist ein Selbstläufer."** — "That runs itself." (Said about a well-designed activity or routine that the children engage with independently, requiring minimal Erzieher intervention.)
2. **"Die Kinder haben heute Hummeln im Hintern."** — "The children have bumblebees in their butts today." (Said when the group is hyperactive and restless, usually before a weather change or after too much sugar.)
3. **"Eingewöhnung nach dem Berliner Modell."** — "Settling-in according to the Berlin Model." (The standard German daycare adaptation protocol — a gradual, parent-accompanied introduction over 2–4 weeks. Used as shorthand for any careful, phased transition.)

---

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag (Orientation & Safety Briefing — UVV)**

*Beat 1 — Arrival & Team Introduction (07:30):* The player arrives at the Kita before the children. Frau Bergmann introduces the player to the team: **Lena** (co-Erzieherin, 26, energetic), **Tom** (Erzieher, 30, the only male staff member, specializes in outdoor pedagogy), and **Frau Kowalski** (Kinderpflegerin, 45, handles the kitchen and cleaning). The player receives the **Dienstplan** (duty roster) and the **Gruppenliste** (group list) with 20 children's names, ages, allergies, and emergency contacts. The player must memorize five key children immediately: **Emil** (4, severe peanut allergy — **Erdnussallergie, Anaphylaxie-Gefahr!**), **Mia** (3, still in diapers), **Liam** (5, speech delay — **Sprachverzögerung, Logopädie**), **Sofia** (4, new, still in **Eingewöhnung**), and **Yusuf** (6, bilingual Turkish-German, starts school next year).

*Beat 2 — Safety Walk & UVV (08:00):* Frau Bergmann walks the player through the **Unfallverhütungsvorschriften** (accident prevention regulations) specific to Kitas: **Kindersichere Steckdosen** (child-proof outlets), **Fingerschutz an Türen** (finger guards on doors), **Giftige Pflanzen** (toxic plants — none allowed on the Außengelände), **Aufsichtspflicht** (duty of supervision — the player must always have visual contact with all children in their care), and the **Notfallplan** (emergency plan — fire evacuation route, assembly point at the corner of Weserstraße). The player must locate the **Erste-Hilfe-Koffer**, the **Brandmelder** (fire alarm), the **Notausgang** (emergency exit), and the **EpiPen** (adrenaline auto-injector for Emil's allergy) in the staff room.

*Beat 3 — First Morgenkreis (09:00):* The children arrive and the player participates in their first **Morgenkreis**. The ritual: all children sit in a circle on the carpet, the **Kerze** (candle) is lit, and the **Begrüßungslied** (welcome song) is sung: *"Guten Morgen, guten Morgen, schön, dass du da bist!"* The player must learn the song in German, clap the rhythm, and greet each child by name. Then the **Wochentag** (day of the week) and **Datum** are discussed: *"Welcher Tag ist heute? Montag! Und welches Datum? Der dritte März!"* The player is asked to lead the **Wetterlied** (weather song) — a simple call-and-response in German that the children correct enthusiastically if the player makes a mistake.

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag (The Standard Routine Task)**

*Beat 1 — Activity Planning (08:00):* The player plans the morning **Angebot** (structured activity) for the week's theme: **"Frühling"** (Spring). The player must write a **Angebotsplanung** (activity plan) in German with the following fields: **Thema** (topic — "Frühlingsblumen basteln"), **Bildungsbereich** (educational domain — "Ästhetische Bildung / Feinmotorik"), **Zielgruppe** (target group — "4–6 Jahre"), **Material** (materials — "Tonpapier, Scheren, Kleber, Krepppapier"), **Ablauf** (procedure — step-by-step instructions), and **Bildungsziel** (educational goal — "Die Kinder üben den Scherenschnitt und benennen Farben auf Deutsch"). Frau Bergmann reviews the plan and gives feedback: *"Gut, aber vergessen Sie nicht die Sprachförderung — lassen Sie die Kinder die Farben laut sagen."*

*Beat 2 — Activity Execution (10:00):* The player leads the **Bastelangebot** at the Maltisch with 8 children. The player must give clear, simple German instructions: *"Nehmt das grüne Papier. Schneidet einen langen Streifen — das ist der Stiel. Jetzt das rote Krepppapier — knüllt es zu einer Kugel — das ist die Blüte. Klebt die Blüte auf den Stiel."* The game simulates the chaos: **Mia** eats the glue stick, **Emil** cuts his finger slightly with the scissors (the player must apply a **Pflaster** — band-aid — and document the **Unfall** in the **Unfallbuch**), and **Liam** refuses to participate because he can't say "Krepppapier" (the player must gently scaffold: *"Krepp-pa-pier. Sag es mit mir. Krepp."*).

*Beat 3 — Documentation & Elterngespräch (14:00):* During **Mittagsruhe** (nap time), the player documents the morning in the **Tagesbericht** (daily report) for the parents' app: *"Heute haben wir Frühlingsblumen gebastelt. Die Kinder haben eifrig geschnitten und geklebt. Besonders schön war die Sonnenblume von Sofia!"* Then the player conducts a brief **Tür-und-Angel-Gespräch** (doorstep conversation) with **Emil's mother** at pickup time: *"Emil hat heute toll mitgemacht. Wir haben darauf geachtet, dass kein Erdnussbrot in der Nähe war. Sein EpiPen liegt griffbereit."* The mother asks a question in rapid German about the upcoming **Elternabend** (parents' evening), and the player must respond accurately.

**Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise (The Workplace Crisis)**

*Beat 1 — The Emergency (11:30):* During outdoor play on the Außengelände, **Emil** (4, peanut allergy) suddenly starts coughing violently, his face swells, and he develops **Nesselsucht** (hives) on his arms. He gasps: *"Ich krieg keine Luft… Hals tut weh…"* Another child, **Noah**, confesses in tears: *"Ich hab Emil von meinem Brot gegeben… mit Erdnussbutter…"* (I gave Emil some of my bread… with peanut butter…). This is an **anaphylaktischer Schock** (anaphylactic shock) — a life-threatening emergency.

*Beat 2 — Immediate Response (11:31–11:35):* The player must act within seconds. Lena shouts: *"EpiPen! Sofort! Und 112 anrufen!"* The player must: (1) grab the **EpiPen** from the emergency pouch (the player practiced this on Day 1), remove the safety cap, and press it firmly against Emil's outer thigh — *"Eins, zwei, drei… zehn Sekunden halten!"* (2) Call **112** and state: *"Kita Sonnenkäfer, Weserstraße 15, 12045 Berlin. Kind, 4 Jahre, anaphylaktischer Schock nach Erdnusskontakt. EpiPen wurde verabreicht. Kind ist bei Bewusstsein, aber Atemnot."* (3) Lay Emil on his side in the **stabile Seitenlage** (recovery position), keep him calm, and monitor his breathing. The game tests the player's ability to deliver precise German medical information under extreme emotional stress while 19 other children are screaming and crying around them.

*Beat 3 — Aftermath (12:00):* The **Notarzt** (emergency doctor) arrives and takes over. Emil is stabilized and transported to the **Krankenhaus** (hospital). Frau Bergmann arrives from her office, pale but composed. The player must complete the **Unfallbericht** (accident report) for the **Unfallkasse** (accident insurance), documenting the exact time, the allergen, the EpiPen administration, and the emergency call. Frau Bergmann then calls Emil's parents. The player must also debrief the other children in age-appropriate German: *"Emil ist krank geworden, weil er etwas gegessen hat, das er nicht verträgt. Der Doktor kümmert sich um ihn. Ihm geht es bald besser."* The next day, a **Teamgespräch** (team meeting) is held to review the **Allergieprotokoll** and prevent future incidents.

---

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

**Exam Structure:** The Erzieher/in Ausbildung (typically 3–5 years depending on the Bundesland) concludes with the **Staatliche Anerkennung** (state recognition) exam. In Berlin, this is administered by the **Senatsverwaltung für Bildung**. The exam includes:

- **Kolloquium** (oral examination, 45–60 minutes): The candidate presents a **pädagogische Facharbeit** (pedagogical thesis) on a self-chosen topic (e.g., *"Sprachförderung bei mehrsprachigen Kindern im Kita-Alltag"*) and defends it before a panel. Questions cover **Entwicklungspsychologie** (developmental psychology — Piaget, Erikson, Vygotsky), **Pädagogische Konzepte** (Montessori, Reggio, Situationsansatz), and **Rechtliche Grundlagen** (SGB VIII — Kinder- und Jugendhilfe, Kita-Gesetz Berlin).

- **Praktische Prüfung**: The candidate leads a documented **pädagogisches Angebot** with a group of children, observed by the examiners, followed by a **Reflexionsgespräch** (reflection discussion).

**Key Exam Vocabulary:**
- **der Situationsansatz** — the situational approach (dominant Berlin Kita pedagogy)
- **die Partizipation** — child participation / co-determination
- **die Inklusion** — inclusion (of children with disabilities)
- **das Berliner Bildungsprogramm** — the Berlin educational curriculum framework
- **die Sprachstandsfeststellung** — language proficiency assessment
- **der Schutzauftrag bei Kindeswohlgefährdung (§ 8a SGB VIII)** — child protection mandate
- **die Bildungsdokumentation** — educational documentation
- **die Eingewöhnung** — settling-in / adaptation period

---

### PROFESSION 14: BÄCKER/IN & KONDITOR/IN (Artisan Bread Baker & Pastry Chef)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works in a traditional **Bäckerei-Konditorei** (bakery-pastry shop) called "Goldene Ähre" on Sonnenallee, with the **Backstube** (bakehouse) in the basement and the **Verkaufsraum** (sales floor) at street level. The Backstube is a hot, flour-dusted industrial space: a massive **Etagenofen** (deck oven) with four stone hearths dominates one wall, its glass doors glowing orange. A **Knetmaschine** (spiral dough mixer) the size of a washing machine stands in the center, its steel hook rotating. Stainless steel **Arbeitstische** (work tables) are covered in flour, dough scraps, and **Teigkarten** (bench scrapers). Wooden **Gärkörbe** (proofing baskets) line the shelves, filled with rising **Sauerteig** (sourdough). A **Kühlschrank** (walk-in cooler) hums in the corner, storing butter, cream, and **Marzipan**. The Konditorei section has a marble-topped table for **Torten** (layer cakes), a **Temperiermaschine** (chocolate tempering machine), and shelves of **Spritzbeutel** (piping bags) and **Tüllen** (piping tips). The Verkaufsraum above is warm and golden, with glass display cases full of **Brötchen**, **Brezeln**, **Schwarzwälder Kirschtorte**, and **Berliner** (jam doughnuts).

**Specific Smells:**
The overwhelming, intoxicating aroma of freshly baked bread — the caramelized crust of **Roggenmischbrot** (rye-wheat bread), the buttery sweetness of **Croissants** and **Butterbrezeln**, the yeasty tang of rising **Sauerteig** (sour, fermented, almost beer-like), the rich vanilla-and-cream scent of **Vanillecreme** and **Sahne** (whipped cream) from the Konditorei, the dark bitterness of melting **Zartbitterschokolade** (dark chocolate), the nutty warmth of **geröstete Mandeln** (roasted almonds), and underneath it all, the dry, powdery smell of **Mehlstaub** (flour dust) that coats every surface and fills the lungs.

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
70–85 dB. The rhythmic thumping of the Knetmaschine kneading 30 kg of dough, the crackling hiss of the Etagenofen as steam is injected (**Schwaden geben**), the sharp clang of **Backbleche** (baking trays) being slammed onto racks, the whir of the **Teigteilmaschine** (dough divider), the sizzle of butter in the **Plundergebäck** (Danish pastry) lamination, and the baker's shouted time calls: *"Zehn Minuten! Brötchen raus!"* The acoustics are hard and echoey — tile walls, steel surfaces, and the low ceiling of the basement amplify every sound.

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**Meister Klaus-Dieter "Kalle" Brenner**, 58, a third-generation **Bäckermeister** with forearms like tree trunks and a permanent dusting of flour in his eyebrows. He wakes at 2:00 AM every day and has done so for 40 years. His speech is a rumbling, jovial Berlin dialect, full of baker's slang and dark humor. He is generous with praise when the bread is perfect and devastatingly blunt when it's not: *"Das ist kein Brot, das ist ein Ziegelstein! Nochmal!"* He treats baking as a sacred craft and despises industrial **Aufbackware** (par-baked factory bread). His signature phrase: *"Brot braucht Zeit, Wärme und Liebe. Die Maschine macht den Teig, aber du machst das Brot."* (Bread needs time, warmth, and love. The machine makes the dough, but you make the bread.)

---

2. **Core Technical Tool & Action Ontology**

**20 Nouns (Tools, Instruments, Materials):**

| # | German (Article + Noun) | Plural | English |
|---|---|---|---|

**10 Domain-Specific Action Verbs:**

| # | German Verb | English | Example Context |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | kneten | to knead | *Den Teig 15 Minuten kneten.* |
| 2 | gehen lassen (aufgehen) | to proof / to let rise | *Den Teig 60 Minuten gehen lassen.* |
