| 8 | räuchern | to smoke | *Den Kasseler über Buchenholz räuchern.* |
| 9 | vakuumieren | to vacuum-seal | *Die Steaks portionsweise vakuumieren.* |
| 10 | wiegen | to weigh | *Die Bestellung auf der Ladenwaage wiegen.* |

---

3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Imperative Commands Shouted on the Job:**

1. **"Messer schärfen! Das Ausbeinmesser ist stumpf wie ein Löffel!"** — /ˈmɛsɐ ˈʃɛʁfn̩ das ˈaʊsbaɪnˌmɛsɐ ɪst ʃtʊmpf viː aɪn ˈlœfl̩/ — "Sharpen the knife! The boning knife is dull as a spoon!" (Shouted when the apprentice is mangling a cut instead of making clean slices.)

2. **"Kühlkette einhalten! Das Hackfleisch darf nicht über vier Grad!"** — /ˈkyːlˌkɛtə ˈaɪnhaltn̩ das ˈhakflaɪʃ daʁf nɪçt ˈyːbɐ fiːɐ̯ ɡʁaːt/ — "Maintain the cold chain! The ground meat must not exceed four degrees!" (Critical food safety command — **HACCP** compliance.)

3. **"Kettenhandschuh an! Du schneidest dir noch die Finger ab!"** — /ˈkɛtn̩ˌhantʃuː an duː ˈʃnaɪdəst diːɐ̯ nɔx diː ˈfɪŋɐ ap/ — "Chainmail glove on! You'll cut your fingers off yet!" (Safety imperative when the apprentice is using the **Ausbeinmesser** near bone.)

4. **"Brättemperatur prüfen! Über zwölf Grad gerinnt das Eiweiß!"** — /ˈbʁɛːtˌtɛmpəʁaˌtuːɐ̯ ˈpʁyːfn̩ ˈyːbɐ tsvœlf ɡʁaːt ɡəˈʁɪnt das ˈaɪvaɪs/ — "Check the emulsion temperature! Above twelve degrees the protein coagulates!" (Critical during **Kuttern** — if the **Brät** gets too warm, the sausage texture is ruined.)

5. **"Theke putzen! Der Kunde sieht jeden Blutfleck!"** — /ˈteːkə ˈpʊtsn̩ deːɐ̯ ˈkʊndə ziːt ˈjeːdn̩ ˈbluːtflɛk/ — "Clean the counter! The customer sees every bloodstain!" (Hygiene command — the **Verkaufsraum** must be immaculate at all times.)

**3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:**

1. **"Das geht auf die Wurst."** — "That's going onto the sausage." (Meaning: The mistake is being hidden or repurposed — trimmings and imperfect cuts get ground into sausage. Also used metaphorically for covering up errors.)

2. **"Fleisch ist Vertrauenssache."** — "Meat is a matter of trust." (A common **Metzger** saying emphasizing that customers rely on the butcher's honesty about origin, freshness, and quality. Used when discussing **Herkunftskennzeichnung** — origin labeling.)

3. **"Wer die Wurst nicht ehrt, ist das Steak nicht wert."** — "Who doesn't honor the sausage isn't worth the steak." (A humorous play on the German proverb "Wer den Pfennig nicht ehrt, ist des Talers nicht wert." Meaning: Respect every part of the animal, from the cheapest sausage to the most expensive cut.)

---

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag (Orientation & Safety Briefing — UVV)**

*Beat 1 — Arrival at the Metzgerei (05:00):* The player arrives at **Metzgerei Krüger** before dawn. The street is dark and quiet, but the shop's back entrance is lit with harsh fluorescent light. Meister Krüger opens the steel door, and a blast of cold air hits the player's face. The smell of raw meat and disinfectant is overwhelming. Krüger hands the player a **weiße Fleischerschürze** (white butcher's apron), **Gummistiefel** (rubber boots), a **Haarnetz** (hairnet), and a **Kettenhandschuh** (chainmail glove) for the left hand: *"Der Kettenhandschuh ist dein bester Freund. Das Ausbeinmesser ist scharf wie eine Rasierklinge, und wenn du abrutschst, schneidest du dir die Sehne durch. Dann war's das mit der Lehre."* The player must equip all **PSA** items in the correct order. Forgetting the **Haarnetz** triggers: *"Haarnetz! Willst du Haare in der Leberwurst? Das ist ein Hygieneverstoß nach der Lebensmittelhygieneverordnung!"* (Hairnet! Do you want hair in the liverwurst? That's a hygiene violation under the Food Hygiene Regulation!)

*Beat 2 — Hygiene-Unterweisung (05:20):* Krüger leads the player to the **Waschbecken** (sink) and demonstrates the **Händewaschprotokoll** (hand-washing protocol): 30 seconds with **antibakterieller Seife** (antibacterial soap), scrubbing under nails, between fingers, up to the wrists. *"Vor jedem Arbeitsgang, nach jedem Toilettengang, nach jeder Pause, nach dem Naseputzen. Immer."* He then explains the **HACCP-Konzept** (Hazard Analysis Critical Control Points): *"Wir dokumentieren jede Kühlhaustemperatur dreimal täglich. Wir dokumentieren jede Wareneingangskontrolle. Wir dokumentieren jede Reinigung. Wenn das Gesundheitsamt kommt und die Dokumentation fehlt, schließen die uns den Laden. Verstanden?"* The player must interact with the **Temperaturlogbuch** (temperature logbook) and enter the current **Kühlhaus** reading (3.2°C) using the correct German format.

*Beat 3 — First Assignment: Wurstproduktion (06:00):* The player assists in the morning **Wurstproduktion**. Krüger demonstrates how to prepare **Bratwurst-Brät**: 60% pork shoulder, 30% pork belly, 10% ice (for temperature control). The player must weigh each ingredient on the **Ladenwaage**, reading the display in grams and kilograms. Krüger feeds the meat into the **Wolff**, and the player catches the ground meat in a stainless steel **Wanne** (tub). Then the **Kutter** phase: the ground meat goes into the spinning bowl, and the player adds **Pökelsalz**, **Pfeffer** (pepper), **Majoran**, and **Kümmel** while the blades spin at 3,000 RPM. The noise is deafening — the player must wear **Gehörschutz**. Krüger shouts over the noise: *"Brättemperatur messen! Das Thermometer rein! Unter zwölf Grad bleiben!"* The player inserts the **Kerntemperaturthermometer** and reads 9.7°C — safe. Krüger nods: *"Gut. Jetzt füllen."* The player loads the **Brät** into the **Wurstfüllmaschine**, threads a **Naturdarm** onto the nozzle, and cranks the handle, watching the sausage fill and twist into links. The sensory detail is extreme: the cold, wet texture of the **Brät**, the slippery stretch of the **Naturdarm**, the smell of raw pork and spices, the mechanical roar of the **Kutter**.

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag (The Standard Routine Task)**

*Beat 1 — Morning Zerlegung (06:30):* A delivery of a whole **Schweinehälfte** (pork half) arrives from the **Schlachthof** (abattoir). The player must perform the **Grober Zerlegung** (primary breakdown) under Krüger's supervision: separating the **Schulter** (shoulder), **Rücken** (loin), **Bauch** (belly), **Keule** (leg/ham), and **Kopf** (head). The player uses the **Fleischerbeil** for the major bone cuts and the **Ausbeinmesser** for the fine separation. Each cut must follow the natural **Muskelfaserverlauf** (muscle fiber direction). Krüger corrects: *"Nicht gegen die Faser schneiden! Du zerreißt das Fleisch! Folge der Anatomie!"* (Don't cut against the grain! You're tearing the meat! Follow the anatomy!) The player must identify each primal cut by its German name and place it on the correct labeled shelf in the **Kühlhaus**.

*Beat 2 — Theke-Verkauf (10:00–13:00):* The player rotates to the **Verkaufsraum** to serve customers. This is a language-intensive segment. A typical interaction:

**Customer (Frau Yilmaz, a regular):** *"Guten Morgen! Ich brauche 500 Gramm gemischtes Hack, 4 Schweineschnitzel — dünn geschnitten, bitte — und ein Stück Leberkäse, so 300 Gramm."*

The player must: (1) Greet correctly: *"Guten Morgen, Frau Yilmaz! Gerne."* (2) Weigh the **Hackfleisch** on the **Ladenwaage** and confirm: *"500 Gramm gemischtes Hack, das macht 5,45 Euro."* (3) Slice the **Schnitzel** on the **Aufschnittmaschine** to the correct thickness (the player adjusts the dial — too thick triggers a complaint: *"Das ist ja ein Schuhsohle, kein Schnitzel!"*). (4) Cut the **Leberkäse** by hand with the **Aufschnittmesser**. (5) Calculate the total and process payment. (6) Wrap each item in **Metzgerpapier** (butcher paper) and label it. The player must handle variations: *"Haben Sie auch Lamm?"* (Do you have lamb?) — requiring the response: *"Lamm haben wir nur freitags, aber ich kann es für nächste Woche vorbestellen."* (We only have lamb on Fridays, but I can pre-order it for next week.)

*Beat 3 — Tagesabschluss & Reinigung (16:00):* The shop closes, and the **Reinigungsprotokoll** (cleaning protocol) begins. The player must: (1) Disassemble and clean the **Wolff**, **Kutter**, and **Aufschnittmaschine** — every blade, every plate, every gasket. (2) Scrub the **Zerlegeraum** floor with **Heißwasser** (hot water) and **Desinfektionsmittel** (disinfectant). (3) Wipe down the **Theke** glass with **Glasreiniger** (glass cleaner). (4) Check and record the **Kühlhaus** temperature one final time. (5) Fill out the **Tagesbericht** with production quantities, sales figures, and any **Reklamationen** (complaints). Krüger inspects the cleaning: *"Der Kutter ist noch nicht sauber. Da klebt noch Brät unter der Dichtung. Nochmal."* (The bowl cutter isn't clean yet. There's still emulsion under the gasket. Again.)

**Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise (The Workplace Crisis)**

*Beat 1 — The Incident (11:30, Saturday morning rush):* The shop is packed with weekend customers. The player is slicing **Schinken** on the **Aufschnittmaschine** when the machine suddenly jams — a piece of bone fragment (missed during **Zerlegung**) has lodged in the blade mechanism. The motor makes a terrible grinding noise, then stops. Smoke begins to rise from the motor housing. Simultaneously, a customer at the counter shouts: *"Entschuldigung! In meiner Leberwurst ist ein Stück Plastik! Das ist doch eklig!"* (Excuse me! There's a piece of plastic in my liverwurst! That's disgusting!) Two crises at once: a mechanical failure and a food safety contamination.

*Beat 2 — Rapid-Fire Commands Under Stress:* Krüger bursts out of the **Zerlegeraum**: *"Maschine aus! Stecker ziehen! SOFORT!"* (Machine off! Pull the plug! NOW!) *"Niemand fasst die Säge an, bis der Elektriker da war!"* (Nobody touches the saw until the electrician is here!) *"Du — nimm die Leberwurst von der Kundin zurück, entschuldige dich, und schreib eine Reklamation auf! Dann sperre die gesamte Charge im Kühlhaus — wir prüfen alles!"* (You — take the liverwurst back from the customer, apologize, and write up a complaint! Then quarantine the entire batch in the cold storage — we're checking everything!) The player must: (1) Unplug the **Aufschnittmaschine** (not just turn it off — the motor is smoking). (2) Apologize to the customer in formal, empathetic German: *"Es tut mir sehr leid, das darf nicht passieren. Ich ersetze Ihnen die Ware sofort und Sie bekommen eine Gutschrift."* (I'm very sorry, this must not happen. I'll replace the goods immediately and you'll receive a credit.) (3) Locate the **Chargennummer** (batch number) on the **Leberwurst** label and quarantine all remaining units from that batch in the **Kühlhaus**, marking them with a red **"GESPERRT"** (QUARANTINED) tag.

*Beat 3 — Resolution & Documentation:* The **Elektriker** arrives and replaces the **Aufschnittmaschine** motor. The **Leberwurst** investigation reveals that a piece of **Vakuumierfolie** (vacuum film) was accidentally ground into the **Brät** during production — a catastrophic **Hygienefehler**. Krüger is devastated: *"Das ist das Schlimmste, was einem Metzger passieren kann. Fremdkörper im Produkt. Wenn das Gesundheitsamt das erfährt, sind wir dran."* The player must help write the **Rückrufbericht** (recall report) and the **Abweichungsbericht** (deviation report) for the **HACCP-Dokumentation**, including: **Fehlerursache** (root cause), **betroffene Chargen** (affected batches), **Korrekturmaßnahmen** (corrective actions), and **Präventivmaßnahmen** (preventive measures). The vocabulary is formal and regulatory: *"Gemäß §17 LFGB (Lebensmittel- und Futtermittelgesetzbuch) ist die Charge sofort aus dem Verkehr zu ziehen."* (According to §17 of the Food and Feed Code, the batch must be immediately withdrawn from circulation.)

---

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

**Certification Path:** The player must pass the **HWK-Gesellenprüfung** for **Fleischer/in** (Butcher). The exam consists of:

- **Schriftliche Prüfung (Written Exam):** Three sections — **Fleischtechnologie** (meat technology — animal anatomy, cuts, aging processes, curing chemistry), **Lebensmittelhygiene & HACCP** (food hygiene — temperature control, cross-contamination prevention, legal regulations under the **LMHV** and **EU-Verordnung 852/2004**), and **Wirtschafts- und Sozialkunde** (business studies — pricing, inventory management, customer service law).

- **Praktische Prüfung (Practical Exam):** Over two days, the player must: (1) **Zerlegen** a whole **Schweinehälfte** and a **Rinderviertel** (beef quarter) into all standard **Teilstücke** (primal and retail cuts) within a time limit. (2) Produce three types of **Wurst** — a **Brühwurst** (scalded sausage, e.g., **Bockwurst**), a **Rohwurst** (raw cured sausage, e.g., **Mettwurst**), and a **Kochwurst** (cooked sausage, e.g., **Leberwurst**) — from scratch, including **Brät** preparation, **Füllen**, **Brühen/Räuchern**, and **Kühlen**. (3) Prepare a **Verkaufsplatte** (display platter) with artistic arrangement of **Aufschnitt**, **Salate** (deli salads), and **Garnitur** (garnish). (4) Conduct a simulated **Verkaufsgespräch** (sales conversation) with a role-playing customer, including allergy inquiries (**Allergenkennzeichnung** — the 14 EU allergens).

- **Key Exam Vocabulary:** die Teilstückzerlegung (primal cut breakdown), die Fleischreifung (meat aging), der pH-Wert (pH value — critical for meat quality), die Kerntemperatur (core temperature), die Brühwurst (scalded sausage), die Rohwurst (raw cured sausage), die Kochwurst (cooked sausage), das Kutterhilfsmittel (cutter aid / phosphate), die Nitritpökelsalz-Konzentration (nitrite curing salt concentration), die Kühlkette (cold chain), die Kreuzkontamination (cross-contamination), die Allergenkennzeichnung (allergen labeling).

**Visa Impact:** Passing the **Gesellenprüfung** grants the player the **Facharbeiterbrief** (skilled worker certificate) recognized across the EU, enabling a **Fachkräftevisum** under §18a AufenthG and opening the path to **Fleischermeister** (master butcher) qualification, which allows the player to open their own **Metzgerei** and apply for a **Niederlassungserlaubnis** (permanent residence permit).

---

### PROFESSION 20: BUSFAHRER/IN / TRIEBFAHRZEUGFÜHRER/IN (BVG) (Berlin Public Bus / U-Bahn Transit Driver)

1. **The 3D Workplace Environment & Sensory Blueprint**

**Visual Landscape:**
The player works for the **BVG (Berliner Verkehrsbetriebe)**, Berlin's public transit authority, assigned to the **Betriebshof Britz** (Britz depot) in southern Neukölln. The **Betriebshof** is a massive industrial complex with rows of yellow **Doppeldeckerbusse** (double-decker buses), **Gelenkbusse** (articulated buses), and **E-Busse** (electric buses) parked in long lanes under floodlights. The **Fahrerkabine** (driver's cabin) of the player's assigned **MAN Lion's City 12** bus is a compact, ergonomic cockpit: a large **Lenkrad** (steering wheel), a digital **Fahrscheindrucker** (ticket printer), a **Fahrgastinformationssystem** (passenger information display), a **Rückspiegel-Array** (multi-mirror array — 6 mirrors covering all blind spots), a **Funkgerät** (radio) tuned to the **BVG-Leitstelle** (control center), a **Notrufknopf** (emergency button), and a **Fahrscheinautomat** (ticket vending machine) visible through the glass partition. The bus route takes the player through Neukölln's streets — **Karl-Marx-Straße**, **Hermannplatz**, **Sonnenallee**, **Grenzallee** — navigating tight turns, double-parked delivery vans, cyclists in the **Radweg** (bike lane), and pedestrians crossing at **Zebrastreifen** (crosswalks). For the **U-Bahn** variant, the player drives a **Typ H** train on the **U7 line** (Rathaus Spandau → Rudow), sitting in the **Führerstand** (driver's cab) at the front of the train, staring into the dark tunnel illuminated by the **Scheinwerfer** (headlights), with the **Fahrsignal** (signal lights) glowing red, yellow, and green along the tunnel walls.

**Specific Smells:**
The diesel exhaust fumes from the **Busbetriebshof** (bus depot), the rubbery smell of hot **Bremsbeläge** (brake pads) after a hard stop, the stale mix of **Schweiß** (sweat), **Parfüm**, **Döner** (kebab), and **Regenkleidung** (rain clothing) inside the crowded bus during rush hour, the ozone-like smell of the **U-Bahn-Stromschiene** (third rail) in the tunnels, the metallic scent of **Stahlschienen** (steel rails) and **Bremsstaub** (brake dust) in the underground stations, and the faint smell of **Desinfektionsmittel** (disinfectant) from the nightly **Fahrzeugreinigung** (vehicle cleaning).

**Ambient Decibels & Acoustics:**
60–95 dB inside the bus. The deep rumble of the **Dieselmotor** (diesel engine) at idle (65 dB), the pneumatic **hiss** of the **Türöffnung** (door opening — *pshhhht*), the electronic **ding-dong** of the **Haltestellenansage** (stop announcement), the roar of acceleration (80 dB), the screech of **Bremsen** (brakes) on wet asphalt (90 dB), the constant chatter of passengers, the crackle of the **Funkgerät** (radio), and the occasional blare of a **Hupe** (horn) at a cyclist running a red light. In the **U-Bahn**, the soundscape is dominated by the electric whine of the **Fahrmotoren** (traction motors), the rhythmic **clack-clack** of the wheels on rail joints, the whoosh of air pressure in the tunnels, and the automated **Stimmenansage**: *"Nächste Station: Hermannplatz. Übergang zur U8."*

**The Meister / Boss Persona:**
**BVG-Fahrdienstleiter (Shift Supervisor) Thomas "Tommy" Brandt**, 51, a lanky Berliner with a shaved head, a **BVG-Dienstuniform** (navy blue uniform with yellow reflective stripes), and a **Dienstausweis** (ID badge) on a lanyard. He has been driving buses and trains for 28 years and knows every pothole, every traffic light timing, and every problematic intersection in Neukölln. His speech is calm, laconic, and dryly humorous — the opposite of the chaotic environment: *"Du fährst keinen Formel-1-Wagen, du fährst 80 Menschen zur Arbeit. Deine einzige Aufgabe ist: lebendig ankommen, pünktlich ankommen, und niemanden umfahren. In der Reihenfolge."* (You're not driving a Formula 1 car, you're driving 80 people to work. Your only job is: arrive alive, arrive on time, and don't run anyone over. In that order.) He communicates primarily via **Funk** (radio) during shifts, and his voice is a constant presence in the player's earpiece: *"Bus 171, hier Leitstelle. Verspätung 4 Minuten an der Hermannstraße. Zieh das am S-Bahnhof wieder rein."* (Bus 171, control center here. 4 minutes delay at Hermannstraße. Make it up at the S-Bahn station.)

---

**20 Nouns (Tools, Instruments, Materials):**

---

3. **The Workplace Register & Jargon**

**5 Imperative Commands Shouted on the Job:**

1. **"Türen schließen! Abfahrt!"** — /ˈtyːʁən ˈʃliːsn̩ apˈfaːɐ̯t/ — "Doors closing! Departing!" (The driver's standard announcement before pulling away from a **Haltestelle**, spoken into the **Mikrofon** and echoed through the bus speakers.)

2. **"Zurückbleiben, bitte! Türen schließen automatisch!"** — /tsuˈʁʏkˌblaɪbn̩ ˈbɪtə ˈtyːʁən ˈʃliːsn̩ autoˈmaːtɪʃ/ — "Stand back, please! Doors close automatically!" (Shouted when passengers try to force the doors open or block them during closing.)

3. **"Leitstelle, hier Bus 171! Notfall an der Sonnenallee! Rettungsdienst anfordern!"** — /ˈlaɪtˌʃtɛlə hiːɐ̯ bʊs aɪn zɛbn̩ aɪns ˈnoːtfal an deːɐ̯ ˈzɔnənˌaleː ˈʁɛtʊŋsˌdiːnst ˈanfɔʁdɐn/ — "Control center, this is Bus 171! Emergency at Sonnenallee! Request ambulance!" (Emergency radio call — must be clear, calm, and precise.)

4. **"Fahrschein vorzeigen! Schwarzfahren kostet 60 Euro!"** — /ˈfaːɐ̯ʃaɪn ˈfoːɐ̯tsaɪɡn̩ ˈʃvaʁtsfaːʁən ˈkɔstət zɛçtsɪç ˈoɪʁo/ — "Show your ticket! Fare evasion costs 60 euros!" (Used when the player, acting as driver, must assist **Kontrolleure** (ticket inspectors) during a fare check.)

5. **"Vorfahrt beachten! Der Radfahrer hat Grün!"** — /ˈfoːɐ̯faːɐ̯t bəˈaχtn̩ deːɐ̯ ˈʁaːtfaːʁɐ hat ɡʁyːn/ — "Yield right of way! The cyclist has green!" (Critical safety command from the **Fahrlehrer** (driving instructor) during training when the player fails to check the **toter Winkel** (blind spot) before turning right.)

**3 Common German Workplace Idioms / Shop-Floor Expressions:**

1. **"Der Bus fährt auch ohne dich."** — "The bus runs without you too." (A humbling reminder from veteran drivers that the schedule is sacred and no single driver is indispensable. Used when an apprentice is late for their **Schicht** (shift).)

2. **"Im toten Winkel ist der Tod."** — "In the blind spot is death." (A grim but essential safety saying about the **toter Winkel** (blind spot) on the right side of the bus, where cyclists and pedestrians are invisible to the driver. Used during **Sicherheitsschulung** (safety training).)

3. **"Pünktlichkeit ist die Höflichkeit der Könige — und der BVG."** — "Punctuality is the politeness of kings — and of the BVG." (A sarcastic twist on the famous French proverb, used by drivers when the **Leitstelle** pressures them to make up time on a delayed route.)

---

4. **Three Detailed Micro-Level Scenarios (Beat-by-Beat)**

**Scenario A: Der erste Tag (Orientation & Safety Briefing — UVV)**

*Beat 1 — Arrival at the Betriebshof Britz (04:30):* The player arrives at the **BVG-Betriebshof** in the pre-dawn darkness. The yard is a sea of yellow buses under sodium floodlights, their engines rumbling as drivers perform **Fahrzeugchecks**. Fahrdienstleiter Brandt meets the player at the **Dienstgebäude** (staff building) and hands them a **BVG-Dienstuniform** (navy jacket, black trousers, reflective vest), a **Dienstausweis** (ID badge), and a **Fahrplanheft** (schedule booklet): *"Willkommen bei der BVG. Bevor du auch nur einen Bus anfasst, lernst du die Regeln. Regel eins: Du bist für 80 Leben verantwortlich. Regel zwei: Der Fahrplan ist dein Gott. Regel drei: Die Sicherheit schlägt den Fahrplan, immer."* He leads the player through the **Einweisung** (induction): the **Fahrdienstvorschrift** (driving regulations), the **Arbeitszeitgesetz** (working hours law — maximum 9 hours driving per shift, mandatory 45-minute break after 4.5 hours), and the **Alkohol- und Drogenverbot** (zero-tolerance alcohol and drug policy — *"0,0 Promille, keine Ausnahme"*).

*Beat 2 — Fahrzeugcheck (05:00):* Brandt walks the player around a **MAN Lion's City** bus and demonstrates the **tägliche Fahrzeugkontrolle** (daily vehicle inspection). The player must physically inspect and check off each item on a digital tablet: **Reifenprofil** (tire tread depth — minimum 3 mm), **Reifendruck** (tire pressure), **Bremslichter** (brake lights), **Blinker** (turn signals), **Scheinwerfer** (headlights), **Spiegel** (mirrors — all 6 adjusted), **Türmechanik** (door mechanism — open/close test), **Rollstuhlrampe** (wheelchair ramp — deploy and retract), **Feuerlöscher** (fire extinguisher — pressure gauge in the green zone), **Verbandkasten** (first aid kit — seal intact), **Warnwesten** (warning vests — 2 present), and **Nothammer** (emergency hammers — 4 present, glass unbroken). Missing any item triggers a penalty: *"Der Feuerlöscher hat keinen Druck! Wenn der Bus brennt, willst du mit einem leeren Löscher dastehen? Melden und tauschen, SOFORT!"* (The fire extinguisher has no pressure! If the bus catches fire, do you want to stand there with an empty extinguisher? Report and replace, NOW!)

*Beat 3 — First Driving Assignment: Linie 171 (06:00):* The player's first supervised drive is on **Buslinie 171** (Hermannplatz → Flughafen Schönefeld). The **Fahrlehrer** (driving instructor), a patient woman named **Frau Özdemir**, sits in the front passenger seat. The player must: (1) Start the engine and wait for the **Druckluftsystem** (air brake system) to build pressure to 8 bar. (2) Adjust all **Spiegel** and the **Fahrersitz** (driver's seat). (3) Pull out of the **Betriebshof** and merge into morning traffic on **Karl-Marx-Straße**. (4) Stop at the first **Haltestelle** (Hermannplatz), open the doors with the **Türknopf**, announce the stop via **Mikrofon**: *"Hermannplatz. Übergang zur U7 und U8."*, wait for passengers to board and alight, check the **Spiegel** for cyclists, close the doors, and pull away smoothly. The sensory experience is immersive: the heavy vibration of the diesel engine through the seat, the pneumatic hiss of the doors, the cacophony of the **Karl-Marx-Straße** traffic, the smell of exhaust and wet pavement, and the constant mental calculation of distances, speeds, and blind spots. Frau Özdemir corrects: *"Rechts abbiegen — Schulterblick! Der Radfahrer ist im toten Winkel! Nochmal!"* (Turning right — shoulder check! The cyclist is in the blind spot! Again!)

**Scenario B: Der Berufsalltag (The Standard Routine Task)**

*Beat 1 — Morning Rush Hour (07:30):* The player is driving **Linie M41** (Sonnenallee → Hauptbahnhof) during **Berufsverkehr** (rush hour). The bus is packed — 90 passengers, standing room only. The player must navigate the **Sonnenallee** corridor, which is congested with double-parked cars, delivery trucks, and cyclists. At each **Haltestelle**, the player performs the routine: brake smoothly, align the bus parallel to the **Bordstein** (curb) within 20 cm (for wheelchair access), open doors, announce the stop, monitor the **Fahrgastspiegel** (passenger mirror) for boarding/alighting, close doors, check mirrors, signal, and merge back into traffic. The **Fahrgastinformationssystem** displays the next stop and the **Anschlussverbindungen** (connecting services). The player must also handle **Sonderhaltestellen** (request stops) — pressing the **Stopp-Taste** (stop button) when a passenger signals.

*Beat 2 — Ticket Inspection & Difficult Passenger (09:15):* A **Kontrollteam** (ticket inspection team) boards the bus at **Hermannplatz**. The player must press the **Türverriegelung** (door lock) to prevent fare evaders from fleeing. A confrontation erupts: a passenger without a ticket becomes aggressive, shouting: *"Ich habe meinen Fahrschein zu Hause vergessen! Das ist Schikane!"* (I forgot my ticket at home! This is harassment!) The player must de-escalate using calm, formal German: *"Bitte beruhigen Sie sich. Die Kontrolleure machen nur ihre Arbeit. Sie können ein erhöhtes Beförderungsentgelt zahlen oder Ihren Ausweis vorzeigen."* (Please calm down. The inspectors are just doing their job. You can pay the increased fare or show your ID.) If the situation escalates, the player must press the **Notrufknopf** and radio the **Leitstelle**: *"Leitstelle, hier M41, Position Hermannplatz. Aggressiver Fahrgast, bitte Polizei schicken."* (Control center, this is M41, position Hermannplatz. Aggressive passenger, please send police.)

*Beat 3 — Schichtende & Übergabeprotokoll (14:30):* The player returns to the **Betriebshof** at the end of the shift. The **Übergabeprotokoll** (handover report) requires the player to: (1) Record the **Kilometerstand** (odometer reading). (2) Report any **Fahrzeugmängel** (vehicle defects) — e.g., *"Spiegel links außen verstellt, Tür 2 schließt verzögert."* (Left exterior mirror misaligned, door 2 closes with delay.) (3) Log any **Vorkommnisse** (incidents) — e.g., *"09:15 — Fahrgast ohne Fahrschein, aggressiv, Polizei gerufen."* (4) Hand over the **Dienstschlüssel** (service key) and **Funkgerät** to the next shift driver. (5) Clock out at the **Stechuhr** (time clock). Brandt reviews the report: *"Tür 2? Das melde ich der Werkstatt. Gut, dass du es aufgeschrieben hast. Wenn der nächste Fahrer das nicht weiß und ein Kind einklemmt, ist das dein Fehler."* (Door 2? I'll report that to the workshop. Good that you wrote it down. If the next driver doesn't know and a child gets trapped, that's your fault.)

**Scenario C: Der Notfall / Die Krise (The Workplace Crisis)**

*Beat 1 — The Incident (17:45, evening rush hour, November rain):* The player is driving **Linie 171** southbound on **Grenzallee** in heavy rain. Visibility is poor, the windshield wipers are on maximum, and the road is slick with **Laub** (wet leaves) and **Regenwasser** (rainwater). As the player approaches the intersection at **Grenzallee / Buschkrugallee**, a **Lieferwagen** (delivery van) runs a red light from the right side, T-boning the front-right corner of the bus. The impact is violent — the bus shudders, glass shatters, passengers scream, and the **Airbag** in the driver's cabin deploys with a deafening **BANG**. The bus skids sideways and comes to a stop against a **Laternenpfahl** (lamppost). Several passengers are injured — one is bleeding from a head wound, another is trapped under a collapsed seat, and an elderly woman is unconscious.

*Beat 2 — Rapid-Fire Emergency Protocol:* The player must execute the **BVG-Notfallprotokoll** in the correct order within a 120-second timer:

(1) **Motor aus, Warnblinklicht an, Handbremse ziehen.** (Engine off, hazard lights on, handbrake engaged.) — Prevents fire and further movement.

(2) **Notruf absetzen:** The player grabs the **Funkgerät** and calls the **Leitstelle** with the **Notrufschema** (emergency call protocol): *"Leitstelle, hier Bus 171, Notfall! Unfall auf der Grenzallee Ecke Buschkrugallee. Kollision mit Lieferwagen. Mehrere Verletzte, mindestens eine Person eingeklemmt. Rettungsdienst und Feuerwehr sofort! Ich sichere die Unfallstelle."* The player must speak clearly despite the chaos — the **Leitstelle** operator asks for confirmation: *"171, verstanden. RTW und Feuerwehr sind unterwegs. ETA 6 Minuten. Evakuieren Sie den Bus, wenn Brandgefahr besteht. Quittieren."* The player responds: *"Quittiert. Keine Brandgefahr, Diesel läuft nicht aus. Evakuierung eingeleitet."*

(3) **Evakuierung einleiten:** The player opens all doors manually (the pneumatic system is damaged, so the player must use the **Notentriegelung** (emergency release) above each door). Using the **Mikrofon** (which still works on battery backup), the player announces: *"Bitte ruhig bleiben! Verlassen Sie den Bus durch die hinteren Türen! Helfen Sie den Verletzten! Nicht drängeln!"* (Please remain calm! Exit the bus through the rear doors! Help the injured! Don't push!)

(4) **Erste Hilfe leisten:** The player grabs the **Verbandkasten** and applies a **Druckverband** (pressure bandage) to the bleeding passenger's head wound while waiting for the **Rettungsdienst** (ambulance). The game simulates the first aid steps: apply sterile gauze, press firmly, elevate the head, check consciousness.

*Beat 3 — Resolution & Documentation:* The **Rettungsdienst**, **Feuerwehr** (fire department), and **Polizei** arrive within 8 minutes. The **Unfallstelle** (accident scene) is secured with **Warnkegel** (warning cones) and **Warnleuchten** (warning lights). The player must give a **Unfallbericht** (accident report) to the **Polizei** and the **BVG-Unfallaufnahme** (accident investigation team): *"Ich fuhr mit circa 35 km/h auf der Grenzallee in Richtung Süden. Die Ampel war grün. Der Lieferwagen kam von rechts bei Rot und rammte die Beifahrerseite. Ich konnte nicht mehr bremsen, die Straße war nass."* The vocabulary is precise and legalistic. The player must also complete the **BVG-Unfallmeldebogen** (accident report form) with: **Unfallzeit** (time of accident), **Unfallort** (location), **Wetterbedingungen** (weather conditions), **Fahrzeugnummer** (vehicle number), **Linie** (route), **Anzahl der Verletzten** (number of injured), **Schadensbeschreibung** (damage description), and **Zeugen** (witnesses — names and contact details of passengers). Brandt arrives at the scene and puts a hand on the player's shoulder: *"Du hast alles richtig gemacht. Notruf, Evakuierung, Erste Hilfe — sauber. Der Rest ist Versicherungssache. Geh nach Hause, ruh dich aus. Morgen ist Dienstbesprechung."* (You did everything right. Emergency call, evacuation, first aid — clean. The rest is insurance business. Go home, rest. Tomorrow is the debriefing.)

---

5. **The Certification & Exam Gateway (Prüfungsvorbereitung)**

**Certification Path:** The player must obtain the **Fahrerlaubnis Klasse D** (bus driving license) and the **Fahrerqualifizierungsnachweis (FQN)** (driver qualification certificate) under the **Berufskraftfahrer-Qualifikations-Gesetz (BKrFQG)**. The certification process includes:

- **Theoretische Prüfung (Theory Exam):** 55 questions covering **Straßenverkehrsordnung (StVO)** (traffic regulations), **Fahrzeugtechnik** (vehicle technology — air brakes, ABS, ESP, retarder), **Sozialvorschriften** (social regulations — driving and rest times under **EU-Verordnung 561/2006**), **Gefahrgutbeförderung** (hazardous goods transport basics), **Erste Hilfe** (first aid), and **Kundenkommunikation** (customer communication — handling difficult passengers, accessibility requirements under the **PBefG** (Passenger Transport Act)).

- **Praktische Prüfung (Practical Driving Exam):** A 75-minute driving test through Berlin traffic, including: **Abfahrtkontrolle** (pre-trip inspection), **Grundfahraufgaben** (basic maneuvers — reversing into a loading bay, parallel parking a 12-meter bus within 30 cm of the curb), **Fahren im Realverkehr** (driving in real traffic — navigating roundabouts, narrow streets, bus lanes, and tram crossings), and **Haltestellenanfahrt** (stop approach — smooth braking, precise curb alignment, door operation, mirror checks). The examiner sits beside the player with a clipboard and grades every mirror check, every signal, every braking maneuver.

- **BVG-Interne Schulung (Internal BVG Training):** An additional 4-week **Streckenausbildung** (route training) where the player memorizes every stop, every traffic light sequence, every detour route, and every **Umleitung** (diversion) for their assigned lines. The player must pass a **Streckenkenntnisprüfung** (route knowledge exam) by driving the route from memory without GPS.

- **Key Exam Vocabulary:** die Abfahrtkontrolle (pre-trip inspection), die Druckluftbremse (air brake), der Totwinkel (blind spot), die Vorfahrtsregel (right-of-way rule), das erhöhte Beförderungsentgelt (increased fare / penalty fare), die Fahrgastrechte (passenger rights), die Barrierefreiheit (accessibility), die Betriebsstörung (service disruption), der Schienenersatzverkehr (rail replacement service), die Umleitung (detour), die Haltestellenansage (stop announcement), der Fahrdienstleiter (shift supervisor / dispatcher).

**Visa Impact:** Obtaining the **Klasse D** license and **FQN** qualifies the player as a **Berufskraftfahrer** (professional driver), a recognized **Mangelberuf** (shortage occupation) in Germany. This enables a **Fachkräftevisum** under §18a AufenthG and provides a stable, union-protected salary under the **TV-N Berlin** (collective bargaining agreement for public transport). After 3 years of accident-free driving, the player can apply for the **Triebfahrzeugführerschein** (train driver's license) to transition to the **U-Bahn** or **S-Bahn**, which offers higher pay and a direct path to **Niederlassungserlaubnis** (permanent residence).
